func (r *Sessions) SoftDelete(ctx context.Context, d Decision, expectedRevision int64) error {
        if err := d.require(relGM); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        return r.owned.SoftDelete(ctx, d.actor, d.actor, d.session, expectedRevision)
}

// Update changes the session's settings at expectedRevision and returns it. Only the GM may.
func (r *Sessions) Update(ctx context.Context, d Decision, expectedRevision int64, set map[string]any) (Session, error) {
        if err := d.require(relGM); err != nil {
                return Session{}, err
        }
        return r.owned.Update(ctx, d.actor, d.actor, d.session, expectedRevision, set)
}

// ResumeDeletion returns the Decision to finish deleting a session this user already deleted: the
// session document is soft-deleted and owned by actor, who deleted it. A deleted session has no
// OpenFGA tuples left to ask (and its record is gone from Live), so Mongo is the proof here, and
// the Decision is the GM's; it only exists so that cleanup steps interrupted after the soft delete
// can be run again. Anyone else, or a session that is not deleted, is ErrNotFound.
func (r *Sessions) ResumeDeletion(ctx context.Context, actor, sessionID string) (Decision, error) {
        if err := checkUserID(actor); err != nil {
                return Decision{}, err
        }
        id, ok := cleanID(sessionID)
        if !ok {
                return Decision{}, ErrNotFound
        }
        var s Session
        err := r.c.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}, {Key: "owner_user_id", Value: actor}, {Key: "deleted_by", Value: actor},
                {Key: "deleted_at", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$ne", Value: nil}}}}, &s)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return Decision{}, ErrNotFound
        }
        if err != nil {
                return Decision{}, err
        }
        return Decision{actor: actor, session: id, relation: relGM}, nil
}

// LimitItems is the part of the owner-scoped repository the limit reconciler needs: which sessions
// an owner has and which are inactive. It reads no session content.
type LimitItems struct{ o *Owned[Session, *Session] }

// Limits returns the limit reconciler's view of the sessions.
func (r *Sessions) Limits() LimitItems { return LimitItems{r.owned} }

// ItemStates lists owner's sessions with their activity state.
func (l LimitItems) ItemStates(ctx context.Context, owner string) ([]ItemState, error) {
        return l.o.ItemStates(ctx, owner)
}

// SetInactive sets or clears a session's inactive mark.
func (l LimitItems) SetInactive(ctx context.Context, owner, id string, inactive bool) error {
        return l.o.SetInactive(ctx, owner, id, inactive)
}

// SetKeepRanks records the owner's keep order.
func (l LimitItems) SetKeepRanks(ctx context.Context, owner string, ids []string) error {
        return l.o.SetKeepRanks(ctx, owner, ids)
}

// Live returns the session if it exists and is not deleted.
func (r *Sessions) Live(ctx context.Context, d Decision) (Session, error) {
        var s Session
        if err := d.require(relView); err != nil {
                return s, err
        }
        err := r.c.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: d.session}, {Key: "deleted_at", Value: nil}}, &s)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return s, ErrNotFound
        }
        return s, err
}

// AddMember records userID in the session with role. An existing member is ErrConflict.
func (r *Sessions) AddMember(ctx context.Context, d Decision, userID, role string) (Member, error) {
        if err := pairOK(d, relApprove, userID); err != nil {
                return Member{}, err
        }
        m := Member{ID: newID(), SessionID: d.session, UserID: userID, Role: role, CreatedAt: r.now(), CreatedBy: d.actor, Live: true}
        if err := r.members.InsertOne(ctx, m); errors.Is(err, store.ErrDuplicate) {
                return Member{}, ErrConflict
        } else if err != nil {
                return Member{}, err
        }
        return m, nil
}

// Member returns userID's membership, or ErrNotFound.
func (r *Sessions) Member(ctx context.Context, d Decision, userID string) (Member, error) {
        var m Member
        if err := pairOK(d, relView, userID); err != nil {
                return m, err
        }
        err := r.members.FindOne(ctx, sessionUser(d.session, userID), &m)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return m, ErrNotFound
        }
        return m, err
}

// CountMembers returns how many live members the session has besides its GM: co-GMs, players and
// observers, the ones the member limit counts.
func (r *Sessions) CountMembers(ctx context.Context, d Decision) (int64, error) {
        if err := d.require(relView); err != nil {
                return 0, err
        }
        return r.members.CountDocuments(ctx, append(sessionScope(d.session), bson.E{Key: "role", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$ne", Value: RoleGM}}}))
}

func byCreated(q Query) Query {
        if len(q.Sort) == 0 {
                q.Sort = []SortKey{{Field: "created_at"}}
        }
        return q
}

// Members lists the session's members, oldest first.
func (r *Sessions) Members(ctx context.Context, d Decision, q Query) (Page[Member], error) {
        if err := d.require(relView); err != nil {
                return Page[Member]{}, err
        }
        return runQuery[Member](ctx, r.members, SessionMemberSpec, sessionScope(d.session), byCreated(q))
}

// ManagerIDs returns the user ids of the session's live GM and co-GMs: the audience of a change
// request. It is an addressing lookup for the event path, not a read on behalf of a user, and
// returns ids only.
func (r *Sessions) ManagerIDs(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) ([]string, error) {
        var ms []Member
        filter := bson.D{{Key: "session_id", Value: sessionID}, {Key: "role", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$in", Value: bson.A{RoleGM, RoleCoGM}}}}, {Key: "live", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$exists", Value: true}}}}
        if err := r.members.Find(ctx, filter, store.FindOptions{Limit: maxManagers}, &ms); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        ids := make([]string, 0, len(ms))
        for _, m := range ms {
                ids = append(ids, m.UserID)
        }
        return ids, nil
}

// maxManagers bounds the managers one session can notify.
const maxManagers = 100

// SessionIDsOf lists the sessions userID belongs to. It is keyed by the caller's own membership
// records and returns no session data; the caller authorizes each session before reading it.
func (r *Sessions) SessionIDsOf(ctx context.Context, userID string, q Query) (Page[Member], error) {
        if err := checkUserID(userID); err != nil {
                return Page[Member]{}, err
        }
        return runQuery[Member](ctx, r.members, SessionMemberSpec, bson.D{{Key: "user_id", Value: userID}, {Key: "deleted_at", Value: nil}}, byCreated(q))
}

// RecordOwnerLimits stores the owner's current entitled values on the session. It changes neither
// the revision nor the audit stamp: nobody edited the session. Only the owner may: the write is
// owner-scoped to the decision's actor.
func (r *Sessions) RecordOwnerLimits(ctx context.Context, d Decision, limits map[string]int, flags map[string]bool) error {
        if err := d.require(relView); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        var discard Session
        err := r.c.FindOneAndUpdate(ctx, scopeID(d.actor, d.session), bson.D{{Key: "$set", Value: bson.D{
                {Key: "owner_limits", Value: limits}, {Key: "owner_flags", Value: flags}, {Key: "owner_limits_at", Value: r.now()},
        }}}, &discard)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return ErrNotFound
        }
        return err
}

// SetRole changes a member's role.
func (r *Sessions) SetRole(ctx context.Context, d Decision, userID, role string) (Member, error) {
        var m Member
        if err := pairOK(d, relEdit, userID); err != nil {
                return m, err
        }
        err := r.members.FindOneAndUpdate(ctx, sessionUser(d.session, userID), bson.D{{Key: "$set", Value: bson.D{{Key: "role", Value: role}}}}, &m)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return m, ErrNotFound
        }
        return m, err
}

// RemoveMember ends a membership (soft delete).
func (r *Sessions) RemoveMember(ctx context.Context, d Decision, userID string) error {
        need := relEdit
        if userID == d.actor {
                need = relView // leaving needs only membership
        }
        if err := pairOK(d, need, userID); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        return r.softDelete(ctx, r.members, sessionUser(d.session, userID), d.actor)
}

// AddInvite stores an invite for the session.
func (r *Sessions) AddInvite(ctx context.Context, d Decision, codeHash string, expires time.Time) (Invite, error) {
        if err := d.require(relEdit); err != nil {
                return Invite{}, err
        }
        in := Invite{ID: newID(), SessionID: d.session, CodeHash: codeHash, ExpiresAt: expires, CreatedAt: r.now(), CreatedBy: d.actor, Live: true}
        return in, r.invites.InsertOne(ctx, in)
}

// JoinTicket is an invite the data layer has resolved and validated (known, live, not revoked, not
// expired) for one user. It is the gate for joining (initiative-sessions, "Join by code") and can
// only come from ResolveInvite, so the join calls cannot be aimed at an arbitrary session. The zero
// value is refused.
type JoinTicket struct{ actor, session string }

// ResolveInvite turns a presented code's hash into a ticket for actor, judging expiry at now. An unknown, deleted,
// revoked, or expired invite is ErrNotFound, whatever the reason.
func (r *Sessions) ResolveInvite(ctx context.Context, actor, codeHash string, now time.Time) (JoinTicket, error) {
        if err := checkUserID(actor); err != nil {
                return JoinTicket{}, err
        }
        if codeHash == "" {
                return JoinTicket{}, ErrNotFound
        }
        var in Invite
        err := r.invites.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "code_hash", Value: codeHash}, {Key: "deleted_at", Value: nil}}, &in)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return JoinTicket{}, ErrNotFound
        }
        if err != nil {
                return JoinTicket{}, err
        }
        if in.RevokedAt != nil || !now.Before(in.ExpiresAt) {
                return JoinTicket{}, ErrNotFound
        }
        return JoinTicket{actor: actor, session: in.SessionID}, nil
}

func (t JoinTicket) check() error {
        if t.actor == "" || t.session == "" {
                return ErrForbidden
        }
        return nil
}

// Invites lists the session's invites, oldest first.
func (r *Sessions) Invites(ctx context.Context, d Decision, q Query) (Page[Invite], error) {
        if err := d.require(relEdit); err != nil {
                return Page[Invite]{}, err
        }
        return runQuery[Invite](ctx, r.invites, SessionInviteSpec, sessionScope(d.session), byCreated(q))
}

// RevokeInvite ends an invite; revoking twice is harmless.
func (r *Sessions) RevokeInvite(ctx context.Context, d Decision, inviteID string) (Invite, error) {
        var in Invite
        if err := d.require(relEdit); err != nil {
                return in, err
        }
        id, ok := cleanID(inviteID)
        if !ok {
                return in, ErrNotFound
        }
        filter := append(bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}}, sessionScope(d.session)...)
        err := r.invites.FindOne(ctx, filter, &in)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return in, ErrNotFound
        }
        if err != nil || in.RevokedAt != nil {
                return in, err
        }
        err = r.invites.FindOneAndUpdate(ctx, append(filter, bson.E{Key: "revoked_at", Value: nil}),
                bson.D{{Key: "$set", Value: bson.D{{Key: "revoked_at", Value: r.now()}, {Key: "revoked_by", Value: d.actor}}}}, &in)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) { // revoked concurrently
                return in, r.invites.FindOne(ctx, filter, &in)
        }
        return in, err
}

// JoinState is what a presented invite lets its holder learn: whether the session still exists
// and whether they are blocked or already a member. It carries no session data.
type JoinState struct {
        Exists, Active, Blocked, Member bool
}

// ForJoin reads the join state for the ticket holder. The ticket is the gate (initiative-sessions,
// "Join by code"), and the pending request created from it grants nothing.
func (r *Sessions) ForJoin(ctx context.Context, t JoinTicket) (JoinState, error) {
        var st JoinState
        if err := t.check(); err != nil {
                return st, err
        }
        actor := t.actor
        var sess Session
        err := r.c.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: t.session}, {Key: "deleted_at", Value: nil}}, &sess)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return st, nil
        }
        if err != nil {
                return st, err
        }
        st.Exists, st.Active = true, sess.InactiveAt == nil
        var b Block
        switch err := r.blocks.FindOne(ctx, sessionUser(t.session, actor), &b); {
        case err == nil:
                st.Blocked = true
        case !errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound):
                return st, err
        }
        var m Member
        switch err := r.members.FindOne(ctx, sessionUser(t.session, actor), &m); {
        case err == nil:
                st.Member = true
        case !errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound):
                return st, err
        }
        return st, nil
}

// RequestJoin records a pending request from the ticket holder for the ticket's session. An earlier settled
// request becomes pending again (the caller has checked the user is not a member, so an approved
// one means the approval never finished); an existing pending one is returned unchanged.
func (r *Sessions) RequestJoin(ctx context.Context, t JoinTicket) (JoinRequest, error) {
        var jr JoinRequest
        if err := t.check(); err != nil {
                return jr, err
        }
        actor, sessionID := t.actor, t.session
        err := r.requests.FindOne(ctx, sessionUser(sessionID, actor), &jr)
        switch {
        case errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound):
                jr = JoinRequest{ID: newID(), SessionID: sessionID, UserID: actor, Status: RequestPending, CreatedAt: r.now(), Live: true}
                return jr, r.requests.InsertOne(ctx, jr)
        case err != nil:
                return jr, err
        case jr.Status == RequestPending:
                return jr, nil
        }
        // Decode into a fresh value: decoding over jr would keep the fields the update removed.
        var reset JoinRequest
        err = r.requests.FindOneAndUpdate(ctx, sessionUser(sessionID, actor), bson.D{
                {Key: "$set", Value: bson.D{{Key: "status", Value: RequestPending}, {Key: "created_at", Value: r.now()}}},
                {Key: "$unset", Value: bson.D{{Key: "reason", Value: ""}, {Key: "role", Value: ""}, {Key: "decided_by", Value: ""}, {Key: "decided_at", Value: ""}}},
        }, &reset)
        return reset, err
}

// Requests lists the session's requests, optionally only those with status.
func (r *Sessions) Requests(ctx context.Context, d Decision, status string, q Query) (Page[JoinRequest], error) {
        if err := d.require(relApprove); err != nil {
                return Page[JoinRequest]{}, err
        }
        q = byCreated(q)
        if status != "" {
                q.Filters = append(q.Filters, Filter{Field: "status", Op: OpEq, Values: []any{status}})
        }
        return runQuery[JoinRequest](ctx, r.requests, SessionRequestSpec, sessionScope(d.session), q)
}

// Decide settles a pending request as approved (with role) or rejected (with reason). A request
// already settled is ErrConflict.
func (r *Sessions) Decide(ctx context.Context, d Decision, requestID, status, role, reason string) (JoinRequest, error) {
        var jr JoinRequest
        if err := d.require(relApprove); err != nil {
                return jr, err
        }
        id, ok := cleanID(requestID)
        if !ok {
                return jr, ErrNotFound
        }
        filter := append(bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}}, sessionScope(d.session)...)
        set := bson.D{{Key: "status", Value: status}, {Key: "decided_by", Value: d.actor}, {Key: "decided_at", Value: r.now()}}
        if role != "" {
                set = append(set, bson.E{Key: "role", Value: role})
        }
        if reason != "" {
                set = append(set, bson.E{Key: "reason", Value: reason})
        }
        err := r.requests.FindOneAndUpdate(ctx, append(filter, bson.E{Key: "status", Value: RequestPending}), bson.D{{Key: "$set", Value: set}}, &jr)
        if !errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return jr, err
        }
        if ferr := r.requests.FindOne(ctx, filter, &jr); ferr == nil {
                return jr, ErrConflict
        }
        return jr, ErrNotFound
}

// PendingUser returns the user who made the still-pending request requestID, so work that must
// precede the decision (scrubbing stale combatant controls) can run before the request settles.
// A request that is not pending is ErrNotFound: a retried approval of a member who already has
// controls must not be scrubbed.
func (r *Sessions) PendingUser(ctx context.Context, d Decision, requestID string) (string, error) {
        if err := d.require(relApprove); err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        id, ok := cleanID(requestID)
        if !ok {
                return "", ErrNotFound
        }
        var jr JoinRequest
        filter := append(bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}, {Key: "status", Value: RequestPending}}, sessionScope(d.session)...)
        if err := r.requests.FindOne(ctx, filter, &jr); err != nil {
                if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                        return "", ErrNotFound
                }
                return "", err
        }
        return jr.UserID, nil
}

// DropRequest removes userID's request, if any.
func (r *Sessions) DropRequest(ctx context.Context, d Decision, userID string) error {
        if err := pairOK(d, relEdit, userID); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if err := r.softDelete(ctx, r.requests, sessionUser(d.session, userID), d.actor); !errors.Is(err, ErrNotFound) {
                return err
        }
        return nil
}

// AddBlock keeps userID out of the session; blocking twice is harmless.
func (r *Sessions) AddBlock(ctx context.Context, d Decision, userID string) error {
        if err := pairOK(d, relEdit, userID); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        err := r.blocks.InsertOne(ctx, Block{ID: newID(), SessionID: d.session, UserID: userID, CreatedAt: r.now(), CreatedBy: d.actor, Live: true})
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrDuplicate) {
                return nil
        }
        return err
}

// RemoveBlock lifts a block.
func (r *Sessions) RemoveBlock(ctx context.Context, d Decision, userID string) error {
        if err := pairOK(d, relEdit, userID); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        return r.softDelete(ctx, r.blocks, sessionUser(d.session, userID), d.actor)
}

// Blocked reports whether userID is blocked from the session.
func (r *Sessions) Blocked(ctx context.Context, d Decision, userID string) (bool, error) {
        if err := pairOK(d, relEdit, userID); err != nil {
                return false, err
        }
        var b Block
        err := r.blocks.FindOne(ctx, sessionUser(d.session, userID), &b)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return false, nil
        }
        return err == nil, err
}

// Blocks lists the session's blocked users.
func (r *Sessions) Blocks(ctx context.Context, d Decision, q Query) (Page[Block], error) {
        if err := d.require(relEdit); err != nil {
                return Page[Block]{}, err
        }
        return runQuery[Block](ctx, r.blocks, SessionBlockSpec, sessionScope(d.session), byCreated(q))
}

// DropSession soft-deletes every membership record of the session: members, invites, requests,
// and blocks. The session document itself is soft-deleted by SoftDelete.
func (r *Sessions) DropSession(ctx context.Context, d Decision) error {
        if err := d.require(relGM); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        return r.dropRecords(ctx, d.session, d.actor)
}

// dropRecords soft-deletes every membership record of the session as actor.
func (r *Sessions) dropRecords(ctx context.Context, sessionID, actor string) error {
        for _, c := range []store.Collection{r.members, r.invites, r.requests, r.blocks} {
                for {
                        err := r.softDelete(ctx, c, sessionScope(sessionID), actor)
                        if errors.Is(err, ErrNotFound) {
                                break
                        }
                        if err != nil {
                                return err
                        }
                }
        }
        return nil
}

package data

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/model-core.go/models"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson"
)

// ScanMembers pages through every membership record, live and soft-deleted, across all sessions.
// It exists for the OpenFGA reconciler (PADR-0035, PADR-0038), which has no acting user and must
// also see deleted records so it can remove their tuples. It is not reachable from a request.
func (r *Sessions) ScanMembers(ctx context.Context, q Query) (Page[Member], error) {
        if len(q.Sort) == 0 {
                q.Sort = []SortKey{{Field: "created_at"}}
        }
        return runQuery[Member](ctx, r.members, SessionMemberSpec, bson.D{}, q)
}

// SessionMembers returns every membership record of one session, live and soft-deleted, read
// fresh. Reconciler only: it has no acting user (PADR-0035, PADR-0038).
func (r *Sessions) SessionMembers(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) ([]Member, error) {
        id, ok := cleanID(sessionID)
        if !ok {
                return nil, ErrNotFound
        }
        var out []Member
        cursor := ""
        for {
                page, err := runQuery[Member](ctx, r.members, SessionMemberSpec, bson.D{{Key: "session_id", Value: id}},
                        Query{Sort: []SortKey{{Field: "created_at"}}, Limit: MaxLimit, Cursor: cursor})
                if err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                out = append(out, page.Items...)
                if page.NextCursor == "" {
                        return out, nil
                }
                cursor = page.NextCursor
        }
}

// LookupSessions maps each of ids that has a session record to whether it is soft-deleted; an ID
// with no record is absent. It reads no session content. Reconciler only: it decides which
// minted session IDs never became sessions (PADR-0040).
func (r *Sessions) LookupSessions(ctx context.Context, ids []string) (map[string]bool, error) {
        var found []Session
        in := make(bson.A, 0, len(ids))
        for _, id := range ids {
                in = append(in, id)
        }
        if err := r.c.Find(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$in", Value: in}}}}, store.FindOptions{}, &found); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        out := make(map[string]bool, len(found))
        for _, s := range found {
                out[s.ID] = s.DeletedAt != nil
        }
        return out, nil
}

// liveGMFilter matches live GM member records of the sessions in session (an id or an $in). The
// live predicate lets Mongo use the partial session_user index; deleted_at alone would scan.
func liveGMFilter(session any) bson.D {
        return bson.D{{Key: "session_id", Value: session}, {Key: "role", Value: RoleGM}, {Key: "live", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$exists", Value: true}}}}
}

// SessionsWithGM returns the IDs among ids that have a live GM member record. Reconciler only.
func (r *Sessions) SessionsWithGM(ctx context.Context, ids []string) (map[string]bool, error) {
        in := make(bson.A, 0, len(ids))
        for _, id := range ids {
                in = append(in, id)
        }
        var gms []Member
        if err := r.members.Find(ctx, liveGMFilter(bson.D{{Key: "$in", Value: in}}), store.FindOptions{}, &gms); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        out := make(map[string]bool, len(gms))
        for _, m := range gms {
                out[m.SessionID] = true
        }
        return out, nil
}

// gmLessMinAge matches the 15-minute min_age the reconciler lists mints with, and is re-checked
// here against the session itself.
const gmLessMinAge = 15 * time.Minute

// RemoveGMLess soft-deletes a live session that has no live GM record, and every membership record
// of it, as the system. Such a session is left by a creation whose GM record could not be stored
// and whose removal also failed (Open); nobody can delete it, and it counts toward the owner's
// sessions_per_gm. It refuses (ErrConflict) a session that does have a GM or is younger than gmLessMinAge, so a lookup that raced
// a creation cannot remove a real session. Reconciler only.
func (r *Sessions) RemoveGMLess(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) error {
        id, ok := cleanID(sessionID)
        if !ok {
                return ErrNotFound
        }
        var s Session
        err := r.c.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}, {Key: "deleted_at", Value: nil}}, &s)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return nil // already gone
        }
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if s.CreatedAt.After(r.now().Add(-gmLessMinAge)) {
                return ErrConflict // too young: Open may still be between its two inserts
        }
        var gm Member
        switch err := r.members.FindOne(ctx, liveGMFilter(id), &gm); {
        case err == nil:
                return ErrConflict
        case !errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound):
                return err
        }
        // Records first: a failure here leaves the session live, so the next pass finds it again.
        if err := r.dropRecords(ctx, id, models.SystemActor); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        return r.owned.SoftDelete(ctx, s.OwnerUserID, models.SystemActor, id, s.Revision)
}

package data

import (
        "context"
        "fmt"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson"
)

// Spec declares one collection: its name, the fields a client may filter, sort, and project by
// (allow-lists; anything else is rejected, PADR-0030), fields to unset on soft delete, and its
// indexes.
type Spec struct {
        Name        string
        Filterable  []string
        Sortable    []string
        Projectable []string
        // Updatable lists the fields Owned.Update may set. State changes with their own rules
        // (credential revocation, last use, scopes, secret) are not here; they get dedicated functions.
        Updatable []string
        // StateFields lists the fields only Encounters.SetState may set: results of domain rules.
        StateFields   []string
        UnsetOnDelete []string
        // ConflictOnDuplicate maps a unique-index rejection to ErrConflict. Set it only where the
        // collection's one unique index is a user-facing constraint (theme slot); elsewhere the raw
        // store.ErrDuplicate is returned so it is not mislabeled.
        ConflictOnDuplicate bool
        Indexes             []store.IndexSpec
}

func asc(fields ...string) bson.D {
        d := make(bson.D, 0, len(fields))
        for _, f := range fields {
                d = append(d, bson.E{Key: f, Value: 1})
        }
        return d
}

// Specs for each collection. Every owner-scoped index leads with owner_user_id, because every
// query filters by it.
var (
        RosterSpec = Spec{
                Name:        CollectionRosters,
                Filterable:  []string{"name", "favorite"},
                Sortable:    []string{"name", "favorite", "created_at", "updated_at"},
                Projectable: []string{"name", "favorite", "characters", "revision", "owner_user_id", "created_at", "updated_at", "created_by", "updated_by"},
                Updatable:   []string{"name", "favorite", "characters"},
                Indexes: []store.IndexSpec{
                        {Name: "owner_name", Keys: asc("owner_user_id", "name")},
                        {Name: "owner_updated", Keys: bson.D{{Key: "owner_user_id", Value: 1}, {Key: "updated_at", Value: -1}}},
                },
        }

        // EncounterSpec: the launcher lists an owner's encounters by position, optionally by status.
        // Combat state (status, round, current_combatant_id, turn_queue, combatants) is not
        // updatable here: it changes only through the domain functions that apply the tracking rules.
        EncounterSpec = Spec{
                Name:          CollectionEncounters,
                Filterable:    []string{"name", "status", "game_system_id", "position"},
                Sortable:      []string{"name", "status", "position", "created_at", "updated_at"},
                Projectable:   []string{"name", "game_system_id", "ordering", "tie_breaker", "labels", "colorblind", "theme_id", "status", "round", "current_combatant_id", "turn_queue", "position", "combatants", "revision", "owner_user_id", "created_at", "updated_at", "created_by", "updated_by"},
                Updatable:     []string{"name", "game_system_id", "ordering", "tie_breaker", "labels", "colorblind", "theme_id", "position"},
                StateFields:   []string{"status", "round", "current_combatant_id", "turn_queue", "combatants", "ref_keys"},
                UnsetOnDelete: []string{"ref_keys"},
                Indexes: []store.IndexSpec{
                        {Name: "session_position", Keys: asc("session_id", "position")},
                        {Name: "owner_position", Keys: asc("owner_user_id", "position")},
                        {Name: "owner_ref_keys", Keys: asc("owner_user_id", "ref_keys")},
                        {Name: "owner_name", Keys: asc("owner_user_id", "name")},
                        {Name: "owner_created_at", Keys: asc("owner_user_id", "created_at")},
                        {Name: "owner_updated_at", Keys: bson.D{{Key: "owner_user_id", Value: 1}, {Key: "updated_at", Value: -1}}},
                        {Name: "owner_status_updated", Keys: bson.D{{Key: "owner_user_id", Value: 1}, {Key: "status", Value: 1}, {Key: "updated_at", Value: -1}}},
                },
        }

        // ThemeSpec: slot numbers are unique per owner among live themes. Soft delete unsets slot, and
        // the partial filter ($exists: true is the supported form) leaves slot-less rows out.
        ThemeSpec = Spec{
                Name:                CollectionThemes,
                Filterable:          []string{"name", "slot"},
                Sortable:            []string{"name", "slot", "created_at", "updated_at"},
                Projectable:         []string{"name", "style", "slot", "main_color", "accent1_color", "accent2_color", "background1_color", "background2_color", "primary_font_name", "secondary_font_name", "background_image", "revision", "owner_user_id", "created_at", "updated_at"},
                Updatable:           []string{"name", "style", "main_color", "accent1_color", "accent2_color", "background1_color", "background2_color", "primary_font_name", "secondary_font_name", "background_image", "slot"},
                UnsetOnDelete:       []string{"slot"},
                ConflictOnDuplicate: true,
                Indexes: []store.IndexSpec{
                        {Name: "owner_slot", Keys: asc("owner_user_id", "slot"), Unique: true,
                                Partial: bson.D{{Key: "slot", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$exists", Value: true}}}}},
                },
        }

        // CredentialSpec: secret_hash is unique so a presented secret resolves to one credential.
        CredentialSpec = Spec{
                Name:        CollectionCredentials,
                Filterable:  []string{"label"},
                Sortable:    []string{"label", "created_at"},
                Updatable:   []string{"label"},
                Projectable: []string{"label", "scopes", "revoked_at", "last_used_at", "revision", "owner_user_id", "created_at", "updated_at"},
                Indexes: []store.IndexSpec{
                        {Name: "owner_created", Keys: bson.D{{Key: "owner_user_id", Value: 1}, {Key: "created_at", Value: -1}}},
                        {Name: "secret_hash", Keys: asc("secret_hash"), Unique: true},
                },
        }

        // SessionSpec: a session is owned by its GM, so owner-scoped create and soft delete apply to
        // the GM only. Reads by members go through Sessions.
        SessionSpec = Spec{
                Name:        CollectionSessions,
                Filterable:  []string{"name"},
                Sortable:    []string{"name", "created_at"},
                Projectable: []string{"name", "revision", "owner_user_id", "created_at", "updated_at", "created_by", "updated_by"},
                Updatable:   []string{"name", "allow_co_gm_create_encounter"},
                Indexes:     []store.IndexSpec{{Name: "owner_created", Keys: bson.D{{Key: "owner_user_id", Value: 1}, {Key: "created_at", Value: -1}}}},
        }

        // The membership collections are addressed by session_id, set from a record the caller was
        // already authorized for, never from request input alone.
        SessionMemberSpec = Spec{
                Name: CollectionSessionMembers, Sortable: []string{"created_at"},
                Indexes: []store.IndexSpec{
                        {Name: "session_user", Keys: asc("session_id", "user_id"), Unique: true,
                                Partial: bson.D{{Key: "live", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$exists", Value: true}}}}},
                        {Name: "user", Keys: asc("user_id")},
                },
        }
        SessionInviteSpec = Spec{
                Name: CollectionSessionInvites, Sortable: []string{"created_at"},
                Indexes: []store.IndexSpec{
                        {Name: "code_hash", Keys: asc("code_hash"), Unique: true},
                        {Name: "session", Keys: asc("session_id")},
                },
        }
        SessionRequestSpec = Spec{
                Name: CollectionSessionRequests, Sortable: []string{"created_at"}, Filterable: []string{"status"},
                Indexes: []store.IndexSpec{{Name: "session_user", Keys: asc("session_id", "user_id"), Unique: true,
                        Partial: bson.D{{Key: "live", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$exists", Value: true}}}}}},
        }
        SessionBlockSpec = Spec{
                Name: CollectionSessionBlocks, Sortable: []string{"created_at"},
                Indexes: []store.IndexSpec{{Name: "session_user", Keys: asc("session_id", "user_id"), Unique: true,
                        Partial: bson.D{{Key: "live", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$exists", Value: true}}}}}},
        }

        // ChangeRequestSpec: a session encounter's player-submitted changes, listed oldest first and
        // filtered by status.
        ChangeRequestSpec = Spec{
                Name: CollectionChangeRequests, Sortable: []string{"created_at"}, Filterable: []string{"status", "combatant_id"},
                Indexes: []store.IndexSpec{
                        {Name: "session_encounter_created", Keys: asc("session_id", "encounter_id", "created_at")},
                        {Name: "session_requester_status", Keys: asc("session_id", "requested_by", "status")},
                },
        }

        // SystemSpec: aliases (including Foundry system IDs) are unique across systems.
        // SystemSpec has no Updatable fields: definitions change only through SystemRepo.Upsert.
        SystemSpec = Spec{
                Name:        CollectionGameSystems,
                Filterable:  []string{"family", "gated", "aliases", "name"},
                Sortable:    []string{"name", "family"},
                Projectable: []string{"name", "family", "aliases", "gated", "fields", "revision"},
                Indexes: []store.IndexSpec{
                        {Name: "aliases", Keys: asc("aliases"), Unique: true},
                        {Name: "family_name", Keys: asc("family", "name")},
                },
        }
)

// AllSpecs lists every collection the service owns.
func AllSpecs() []Spec {
        return []Spec{RosterSpec, EncounterSpec, ThemeSpec, CredentialSpec, SystemSpec,
                SessionSpec, SessionMemberSpec, SessionInviteSpec, SessionRequestSpec, SessionBlockSpec, ChangeRequestSpec, NotificationPrefSpec}
}

// EnsureIndexes creates every collection's indexes. Safe to run at each startup.
func EnsureIndexes(ctx context.Context, db store.Database) error {
        for _, s := range AllSpecs() {
                if err := db.Collection(s.Name).EnsureIndexes(ctx, s.Indexes); err != nil {
                        return fmt.Errorf("ensure indexes on %s: %w", s.Name, err)
                }
        }
        if err := db.Collection(CollectionStreamTickets).EnsureIndexes(ctx, ticketIndexes); err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("ensure indexes on %s: %w", CollectionStreamTickets, err)
        }
        if err := db.Collection(CollectionStreamLeases).EnsureIndexes(ctx, leaseIndexes); err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("ensure indexes on %s: %w", CollectionStreamLeases, err)
        }
        return nil
}

package data

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "log/slog"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson"
)

// CollectionStreamLeases holds one row per open (or just dropped) event stream, so a user's
// concurrent streams can be counted across pods.
const CollectionStreamLeases = "stream_leases"

// ErrStreamLimit means the user already holds the most concurrent streams allowed.
var ErrStreamLimit = errors.New("data: too many concurrent streams")

// ErrStreamUnavailable means the seat store could not be reached or written, so no seat could be
// granted. A new connect fails closed on it; streams already open stay up (see Renew).
var ErrStreamUnavailable = errors.New("data: stream seats are unavailable")

// leaseRowTTL keeps rows past their lease for a while before the TTL index removes them; the
// counts filter on Until, never on the index.
const leaseRowTTL = time.Minute

// cleanupTimeout bounds the writes that undo or release a seat, which must finish even when the
// request that took the seat is gone.
const cleanupTimeout = 5 * time.Second

var leaseIndexes = []store.IndexSpec{
        {Name: "until_ttl", Keys: bson.D{{Key: "until", Value: 1}}, ExpireAfter: leaseRowTTL},
        {Name: "bucket_until", Keys: bson.D{{Key: "bucket", Value: 1}, {Key: "until", Value: 1}}},
}

// StreamLease is one stream's seat. Until is when the seat lapses unless renewed: a pod that
// crashes leaves seats that expire on their own, and a dropped client keeps its seat for a grace
// period so a reconnect does not need a free one. Bucket is what the cap counts: "user:<id>" for a
// user's stream, "cred:<id>" for an integration credential's, so a runaway credential cannot lock
// its owner out of their own stream. Every pod stamps Until from its own clock and the pods compare
// them, so the cluster's clocks must agree to within a few seconds (a skew moves a seat's lapse by
// that much). Pod and SessionID are not read yet; the per-session member count (7.12b) will.
type StreamLease struct {
        ID        string    `bson:"_id"`
        Bucket    string    `bson:"bucket"`
        UserID    string    `bson:"user_id"`
        SessionID string    `bson:"session_id,omitempty"`
        ClientID  string    `bson:"client_id,omitempty"`
        Pod       string    `bson:"pod"`
        Until     time.Time `bson:"until"`
        Dropped   bool      `bson:"dropped,omitempty"`
}

// StreamLeases is the lease store.
type StreamLeases struct {
        c   store.Collection
        now func() time.Time
}

// NewStreamLeases returns the lease store on db.
func NewStreamLeases(db store.Database) *StreamLeases {
        return &StreamLeases{c: db.Collection(CollectionStreamLeases), now: time.Now}
}

// SetClock replaces the clock seats are aged by (tests).
func (r *StreamLeases) SetClock(now func() time.Time) { r.now = now }

func (r *StreamLeases) live(l StreamLease) bson.D {
        f := bson.D{{Key: "bucket", Value: l.Bucket}, {Key: "until", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$gt", Value: r.now()}}}}
        if l.ClientID != "" {
                // A dropped seat of the same client is the one this stream takes over, not a second seat.
                f = append(f, bson.E{Key: "$or", Value: bson.A{
                        bson.D{{Key: "dropped", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$ne", Value: true}}}},
                        bson.D{{Key: "client_id", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$ne", Value: l.ClientID}}}},
                }})
        }
        return f
}

// retract removes seat id even when the request that took it has ended.
func (r *StreamLeases) retract(ctx context.Context, id string) {
        ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), cleanupTimeout)
        defer cancel()
        if err := r.c.DeleteOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}}); err != nil && !errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                slog.Error("could not give back a stream seat; it lapses on its own", "seat", id, "error", err)
        }
}

// Acquire gives l a seat valid for ttl, or ErrStreamLimit when its bucket already holds max, or
// ErrStreamUnavailable when the store fails. Two acquisitions can both pass the count, so the seat
// is recounted after the insert and given back if the bucket is over: both may then be refused, but
// neither is kept over the limit. A dropped seat of the same client is taken over once the new one
// is in, not before, so a refused reconnect keeps its old seat.
// ponytail: concurrent connects at the limit can all be refused; a retry succeeds.
func (r *StreamLeases) Acquire(ctx context.Context, l StreamLease, ttl time.Duration, max int) error {
        if err := checkUserID(l.UserID); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if l.Bucket == "" {
                l.Bucket = "user:" + l.UserID
        }
        l.Until, l.Dropped = r.now().Add(ttl), false
        if err := r.c.InsertOne(ctx, l); err != nil {
                r.retract(ctx, l.ID) // the write may have landed before the error
                return fmt.Errorf("%w: %v", ErrStreamUnavailable, err)
        }
        n, err := r.c.CountDocuments(ctx, r.live(l))
        if err != nil {
                r.retract(ctx, l.ID)
                return fmt.Errorf("%w: %v", ErrStreamUnavailable, err)
        }
        if n > int64(max) {
                r.retract(ctx, l.ID)
                return ErrStreamLimit
        }
        if l.ClientID != "" {
                r.takeOver(ctx, l)
        }
        return nil
}

// takeOver removes every dropped seat of l's client other than l, however many two streams that
// shared a client id left behind.
func (r *StreamLeases) takeOver(ctx context.Context, l StreamLease) {
        ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), cleanupTimeout)
        defer cancel()
        for range 8 {
                err := r.c.DeleteOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "bucket", Value: l.Bucket}, {Key: "client_id", Value: l.ClientID}, {Key: "dropped", Value: true}, {Key: "_id", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$ne", Value: l.ID}}}})
                if err != nil {
                        return // none left, or the store failed: the stale seat lapses on its own
                }
        }
}

// ponytail: Renew can bring back a seat that lapsed (after more than the lease TTL of failed
// renewals) without recounting, so a bucket may sit one over its cap until that stream ends; a
// recount here would close a healthy stream on a store blip.
// Renew pushes the seat's end to ttl from now. If the row is gone (its TTL ran out while renewals
// were failing) it is put back, so a healthy stream does not lose its seat. A renewal that fails
// leaves the stream up; its seat lapses after the lease TTL if renewals keep failing.
func (r *StreamLeases) Renew(ctx context.Context, l StreamLease, ttl time.Duration) error {
        var out StreamLease
        err := r.c.FindOneAndUpdate(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: l.ID}},
                bson.D{{Key: "$set", Value: bson.D{{Key: "until", Value: r.now().Add(ttl)}}}}, &out)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                l.Until, l.Dropped = r.now().Add(ttl), false
                return r.c.InsertOne(ctx, l)
        }
        return err
}

// Release ends the seat. With keep, a client that has an id keeps it for grace so its reconnect
// does not need a free seat; every other end (evicted, over its age, revoked, never opened) frees
// it at once.
func (r *StreamLeases) Release(ctx context.Context, l StreamLease, grace time.Duration, keep bool) error {
        ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), cleanupTimeout)
        defer cancel()
        if !keep || grace <= 0 || l.ClientID == "" {
                return ignoreMissing(r.c.DeleteOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: l.ID}}))
        }
        var out StreamLease
        return ignoreMissing(r.c.FindOneAndUpdate(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: l.ID}},
                bson.D{{Key: "$set", Value: bson.D{{Key: "dropped", Value: true}, {Key: "until", Value: r.now().Add(grace)}}}}, &out))
}

func ignoreMissing(err error) error {
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return nil
        }
        return err
}

package data

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/constants"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson"
)

// CollectionStreamTickets holds the one-time tickets that open a WebSocket for a browser, which
// cannot send an Authorization header on an upgrade.
const CollectionStreamTickets = "stream_tickets"

// ticketRowTTL is the TTL index on a ticket row. MongoDB removes expired rows about once a minute,
// so Redeem checks the age itself; the index only keeps the collection small.
const ticketRowTTL = constants.MaxStreamTicketTTL

var ticketIndexes = []store.IndexSpec{{Name: "at_ttl", Keys: bson.D{{Key: "at", Value: 1}}, ExpireAfter: ticketRowTTL}}

// StreamTicket is what a minted ticket stands for. Only the SHA-256 of the ticket is stored, as the
// row id, so a database read does not yield a usable ticket. Nothing here is trusted beyond who
// asked, for what, and at what level: access is re-read when the ticket is redeemed.
type StreamTicket struct {
        ID          string    `bson:"_id"` // hex SHA-256 of the ticket
        UserID      string    `bson:"user_id"`
        SessionID   string    `bson:"session_id,omitempty"` // empty: the user's private encounters
        EncounterID string    `bson:"encounter_id,omitempty"`
        Level       ViewLevel `bson:"level"` // what the user could see when the ticket was minted
        ClientID    string    `bson:"client_id,omitempty"`
        // UserLevel marks a ticket for the user-level stream: addressed events across every session
        // the user belongs to, no encounter state. It carries no session or encounter.
        UserLevel bool      `bson:"user_level,omitempty"`
        At        time.Time `bson:"at"`
}

// StreamTickets is the ticket store.
type StreamTickets struct {
        c   store.Collection
        now func() time.Time
}

// NewStreamTickets returns the ticket store on db.
func NewStreamTickets(db store.Database) *StreamTickets {
        return &StreamTickets{c: db.Collection(CollectionStreamTickets), now: time.Now}
}

// SetClock replaces the clock the ticket ages are read from (tests).
func (r *StreamTickets) SetClock(now func() time.Time) { r.now = now }

// Mint stores t (its ID already the hash of the ticket handed to the caller).
func (r *StreamTickets) Mint(ctx context.Context, t StreamTicket) error {
        if err := checkUserID(t.UserID); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        t.At = r.now()
        return r.c.InsertOne(ctx, t)
}

// Peek reads the ticket without spending it, so a connect that will be refused for a limit leaves
// it redeemable. Redeem is still what makes it single-use.
func (r *StreamTickets) Peek(ctx context.Context, id string, maxAge time.Duration) (StreamTicket, error) {
        var t StreamTicket
        if err := r.c.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}}, &t); err != nil {
                if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                        return StreamTicket{}, ErrNotFound
                }
                return StreamTicket{}, err
        }
        if r.now().Sub(t.At) > maxAge {
                return StreamTicket{}, ErrNotFound
        }
        return t, nil
}

// Redeem spends the ticket whose hash is id: it finds the row, then deletes it by _id and requires
// that the delete removed one row. Two redeems can both find the row, but only one delete removes
// it (the other reports ErrNotFound, which store.DeleteOne raises on a deleted count of zero), so a
// ticket opens one stream even when two pods are asked at once.
// A ticket older than maxAge, unknown, or already spent is ErrNotFound.
func (r *StreamTickets) Redeem(ctx context.Context, id string, maxAge time.Duration) (StreamTicket, error) {
        var t StreamTicket
        if err := r.c.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}}, &t); err != nil {
                if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                        return StreamTicket{}, ErrNotFound
                }
                return StreamTicket{}, err
        }
        if err := r.c.DeleteOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}}); err != nil {
                if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                        return StreamTicket{}, ErrNotFound
                }
                return StreamTicket{}, err
        }
        if r.now().Sub(t.At) > maxAge {
                return StreamTicket{}, ErrNotFound
        }
        return t, nil
}

// IsMember reports whether userID is a live member of the live session sessionID. It is the
// per-delivery check behind the user-level stream, which spans sessions and so is not evicted when
// one membership ends.
func (r *Sessions) IsMember(ctx context.Context, userID, sessionID string) (bool, error) {
        _, err := r.TicketView(ctx, userID, sessionID, ViewGM)
        switch {
        case errors.Is(err, ErrNotMember):
                return false, nil
        case err != nil:
                return false, err
        }
        return true, nil
}

// TicketView returns the Viewer a redeemed ticket opens: the user's live member record decides, not
// the ticket. OpenFGA was asked when the ticket was minted and is not asked again (a redeem carries
// no bearer to ask with), so the live record is the check: the session must exist, the user must
// still be a member, and their role's level must not exceed minted (a member promoted since gets a
// new ticket). A lower level is served at the lower level. Every refusal is ErrNotMember.
func (r *Sessions) TicketView(ctx context.Context, userID, sessionID string, minted ViewLevel) (Viewer, error) {
        id, ok := cleanID(sessionID)
        if !ok || checkUserID(userID) != nil {
                return Viewer{}, ErrNotMember
        }
        var s Session
        if err := r.c.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}, {Key: "deleted_at", Value: nil}}, &s); err != nil {
                if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                        return Viewer{}, ErrNotMember
                }
                return Viewer{}, err
        }
        var m Member
        if err := r.members.FindOne(ctx, sessionUser(id, userID), &m); err != nil {
                if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                        return Viewer{}, ErrNotMember
                }
                return Viewer{}, err
        }
        level := roleLevel(m.Role)
        if level == ViewNone || level > minted {
                return Viewer{}, ErrNotMember
        }
        return Viewer{actor: userID, session: id, level: level}, nil
}

// roleLevel is the view level a session role carries.
func roleLevel(role string) ViewLevel {
        switch role {
        case RoleGM, RoleCoGM:
                return ViewGM
        case RolePlayer:
                return ViewPlayer
        case RoleObserver:
                return ViewObserver
        }
        return ViewNone
}

package data

import (
        "context"
        "crypto/sha256"
        "encoding/hex"
        "encoding/json"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "regexp"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/model-core.go/models"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson"
)

// Outcome is what Upsert did with a definition.
type Outcome string

// Upsert outcomes.
const (
        Created   Outcome = "created"
        Updated   Outcome = "updated"
        Unchanged Outcome = "unchanged"
)

var slug = regexp.MustCompile(`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`)

// SystemRepo reads and seeds game-system definitions. They are shared reference data with no
// owner, so there is no owner scoping; the only writer is the seed loader, as the system actor.
type SystemRepo struct {
        c   store.Collection
        now func() time.Time
}

// NewSystemRepo builds the repository for the game_systems collection in db.
func NewSystemRepo(db store.Database) *SystemRepo {
        return &SystemRepo{c: db.Collection(SystemSpec.Name), now: func() time.Time { return time.Now().UTC() }}
}

// Get returns the system whose ID or alias equals idOrAlias.
func (s *SystemRepo) Get(ctx context.Context, idOrAlias string) (GameSystem, error) {
        var out GameSystem
        filter := bson.D{{Key: "$or", Value: bson.A{
                bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: idOrAlias}}, bson.D{{Key: "aliases", Value: idOrAlias}},
        }}}
        err := s.c.FindOne(ctx, filter, &out)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return out, ErrNotFound
        }
        return out, err
}

// DefaultSystemID is "No System": the simple tracker every user can use, and the definition an
// encounter falls back to when its game system has no definition.
const DefaultSystemID = "none"

// GetOrDefault returns the system for idOrAlias, or the default system when none is defined for it.
func (s *SystemRepo) GetOrDefault(ctx context.Context, idOrAlias string) (GameSystem, error) {
        sys, err := s.Get(ctx, idOrAlias)
        if errors.Is(err, ErrNotFound) {
                return s.Get(ctx, DefaultSystemID)
        }
        return sys, err
}

// List returns one page of every system, gated or not. It is for the loader and tools; an API
// caller's list goes through ListAvailable.
func (s *SystemRepo) List(ctx context.Context, q Query) (Page[GameSystem], error) {
        return runQuery[GameSystem](ctx, s.c, SystemSpec, nil, q)
}

// ListAvailable returns one page of the systems a caller may use: every system when entitled,
// otherwise only the ungated ones (No System). The restriction is part of the query itself and is
// ANDed with the client's filters, so a filter can never widen it.
func (s *SystemRepo) ListAvailable(ctx context.Context, q Query, entitled bool) (Page[GameSystem], error) {
        var base bson.D
        if !entitled {
                base = bson.D{{Key: "gated", Value: bson.D{{Key: "$ne", Value: true}}}}
        }
        return runQuery[GameSystem](ctx, s.c, SystemSpec, base, q)
}

// Upsert creates or updates sys by ID. A definition identical to the stored one (same content
// hash) is left untouched, so re-seeding keeps IDs, revisions, and audit fields stable.
func (s *SystemRepo) Upsert(ctx context.Context, sys GameSystem) (Outcome, error) {
        if err := ValidateSystem(sys); err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        hash, err := definitionHash(sys)
        if err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        sys.DefinitionHash = hash

        // A concurrent loader can change the row between our read and write; re-read and retry once
        // before giving up, so a lost race is never reported as "unchanged".
        for attempt := 0; attempt < 2; attempt++ {
                var (
                        cur GameSystem
                        out Outcome
                )
                err = s.c.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: sys.ID}}, &cur)
                switch {
                case errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound):
                        out, err = s.create(ctx, sys)
                case err != nil:
                        return "", err
                case cur.DefinitionHash == hash:
                        return Unchanged, nil
                default:
                        out, err = s.update(ctx, sys, cur.DefinitionHash)
                }
                if !errors.Is(err, errLostRace) {
                        return out, err
                }
        }
        return "", fmt.Errorf("system %q: %w", sys.ID, errLostRace)
}

// errLostRace means another writer changed the definition between the read and the write.
var errLostRace = errors.New("data: concurrent change to system definition")

func (s *SystemRepo) create(ctx context.Context, sys GameSystem) (Outcome, error) {
        sys.Revision = 1
        models.StampCreate(&sys.Auditable, models.SystemActor, s.now())
        err := s.c.InsertOne(ctx, sys)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrDuplicate) {
                // Another replica may have inserted this same ID first; if so, retry as an update/no-op.
                var existing GameSystem
                switch rerr := s.c.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: sys.ID}}, &existing); {
                case rerr == nil:
                        return "", errLostRace
                case errors.Is(rerr, store.ErrNotFound):
                        return "", fmt.Errorf("system %q alias conflicts with another system: %w", sys.ID, err)
                default:
                        return "", rerr
                }
        }
        if err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        return Created, nil
}

func (s *SystemRepo) update(ctx context.Context, sys GameSystem, prevHash string) (Outcome, error) {
        filter := bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: sys.ID}, {Key: "definition_hash", Value: prevHash}}
        update := bson.D{
                {Key: "$set", Value: bson.D{
                        {Key: "name", Value: sys.Name}, {Key: "family", Value: sys.Family},
                        {Key: "aliases", Value: sys.Aliases}, {Key: "gated", Value: sys.Gated},
                        {Key: "fields", Value: sys.Fields}, {Key: "definition_hash", Value: sys.DefinitionHash},
                        {Key: "updated_at", Value: s.now()}, {Key: "updated_by", Value: models.SystemActor}}},
                {Key: "$inc", Value: bson.D{{Key: "revision", Value: 1}}},
        }
        var out GameSystem
        err := s.c.FindOneAndUpdate(ctx, filter, update, &out)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrDuplicate) {
                return "", fmt.Errorf("system %q alias conflicts with another system: %w", sys.ID, err)
        }
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return "", errLostRace
        }
        if err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        return Updated, nil
}

func definitionHash(sys GameSystem) (string, error) {
        b, err := json.Marshal(struct {
                Name    string         `json:"name"`
                Family  string         `json:"family"`
                Aliases []string       `json:"aliases"`
                Gated   bool           `json:"gated"`
                Fields  []TrackerField `json:"fields"`
        }{sys.Name, sys.Family, sys.Aliases, sys.Gated, sys.Fields})
        if err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        sum := sha256.Sum256(b)
        return hex.EncodeToString(sum[:]), nil
}

// ValidateSystem checks a definition is self-consistent: slug IDs and aliases, known field
// kinds, unique keys, bounds that make sense, and max references that resolve.
func ValidateSystem(sys GameSystem) error {
        bad := func(format string, a ...any) error {
                return fmt.Errorf("%w: system %q: %s", ErrInvalid, sys.ID, fmt.Sprintf(format, a...))
        }
        if !slug.MatchString(sys.ID) {
                return bad("id must be a lowercase slug")
        }
        if sys.Name == "" || sys.Family == "" || len(sys.Fields) == 0 {
                return bad("name, family and at least one field are required")
        }
        for _, a := range sys.Aliases {
                if !slug.MatchString(a) {
                        return bad("alias %q must be a lowercase slug", a)
                }
        }
        byKey := map[string]TrackerField{}
        for _, f := range sys.Fields {
                if f.Key == "" || byKey[f.Key].Key != "" {
                        return bad("field keys must be non-empty and unique (%q)", f.Key)
                }
                byKey[f.Key] = f
        }
        for _, f := range sys.Fields {
                if err := validateField(f, byKey); err != nil {
                        return bad("%v", err)
                }
        }
        return nil
}

func validateField(f TrackerField, byKey map[string]TrackerField) error {
        switch f.Kind {
        case FieldCounter, FieldPool, FieldFlag, FieldTrack:
        default:
                return fmt.Errorf("field %q has unknown kind %q", f.Key, f.Kind)
        }
        if f.Max != nil && f.MaxRef != "" {
                return fmt.Errorf("field %q sets both max and max_ref", f.Key)
        }
        if f.Kind == FieldFlag && (f.Def < 0 || f.Def > 1 || f.MaxRef != "" ||
                (f.Min != nil && *f.Min < 0) || (f.Max != nil && *f.Max > 1)) {
                return fmt.Errorf("flag field %q must stay within 0 and 1", f.Key)
        }
        if f.MaxRef != "" {
                if ref, ok := byKey[f.MaxRef]; !ok || f.MaxRef == f.Key {
                        return fmt.Errorf("field %q max_ref %q does not name another field", f.Key, f.MaxRef)
                } else if f.Def > ref.Def {
                        return fmt.Errorf("field %q default exceeds its max_ref %q default", f.Key, f.MaxRef)
                }
        }
        if f.Min != nil && f.Max != nil && *f.Min > *f.Max {
                return fmt.Errorf("field %q min exceeds max", f.Key)
        }
        if (f.Min != nil && f.Def < *f.Min) || (f.Max != nil && f.Def > *f.Max) {
                return fmt.Errorf("field %q default is out of bounds", f.Key)
        }
        return nil
}

package data

import (
        "context"
        "errors"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson"
)

// Viewer is the proof of how a caller may see one encounter. Every encounter view is built from a
// Viewer and nothing else, so the view builder never recomputes membership (PADR-0035). The only
// ways to get a granted Viewer are AuthorizeView (a session member, asked of OpenFGA with the
// caller's own bearer) and an owner-scoped load of a private encounter. The zero value grants
// nothing.
type Viewer struct {
        actor, session string
        level          ViewLevel
}

// ViewLevel is the least a Viewer may see, weakest first. The zero value sees nothing.
type ViewLevel int

// View levels: Observer and Player both get the non-GM view; only the Player controls combatants.
const (
        ViewNone ViewLevel = iota
        ViewObserver
        ViewPlayer
        ViewGM // gm, co_gm, or the owner of a private encounter
)

// Actor returns the user the viewer is.
func (v Viewer) Actor() string { return v.actor }

// SessionID returns the session the viewer covers; empty for the owner of a private encounter.
func (v Viewer) SessionID() string { return v.session }

// Level returns what the viewer may see.
func (v Viewer) Level() ViewLevel { return v.level }

// BusViewer is the viewer the platform-bus form of an event is rendered for: observer level, no
// actor, bound to the session of the encounter being rendered ("" for a private encounter). It
// grants no read: nothing can load an encounter with it, it only lets the view builder decide what
// an unprivileged consumer may be told about a document already in memory.
func BusViewer(sessionID string) Viewer { return Viewer{session: sessionID, level: ViewObserver} }

// ownerViewer is the viewer for a private encounter the caller owns.
func ownerViewer(owner string) Viewer { return Viewer{actor: owner, level: ViewGM} }

// AuthorizeView returns the Viewer for actor on the session. OpenFGA decides membership (can_view,
// PADR-0035) and the actor's live member record names the role; the role's own relation is then
// checked too, and the two must agree or the answer is ErrForbidden and the zero Viewer. A caller
// who is not a member, or whose record and tuples disagree (for example during a role change),
// learns nothing else. When no answer can be obtained the error is relations.ErrUnavailable or
// relations.ErrUnauthorized, so session data fails closed.
func (r *Sessions) AuthorizeView(ctx context.Context, actor, sessionID string) (Viewer, error) {
        d, err := Authorize(ctx, actor, sessionID, relView)
        if errors.Is(err, ErrForbidden) {
                return Viewer{}, ErrNotMember
        }
        if err != nil {
                return Viewer{}, err
        }
        m, err := r.Member(ctx, d, actor)
        if errors.Is(err, ErrNotFound) {
                return Viewer{}, ErrNotMember
        }
        if err != nil {
                return Viewer{}, err
        }
        level := roleLevel(m.Role)
        var relation string
        switch m.Role {
        case RoleGM, RoleCoGM:
                relation = relEdit
        case RolePlayer:
                relation = RolePlayer
        case RoleObserver:
                relation = RoleObserver
        default:
                return Viewer{}, ErrNotMember
        }
        if _, err := Authorize(ctx, actor, sessionID, relation); errors.Is(err, ErrForbidden) {
                return Viewer{}, ErrNotMember
        } else if err != nil {
                return Viewer{}, err
        }
        return Viewer{actor: d.actor, session: d.session, level: level}, nil
}

// OwnerViewer is the viewer for owner's own private encounters.
func OwnerViewer(owner string) (Viewer, error) {
        if err := checkUserID(owner); err != nil {
                return Viewer{}, err
        }
        return ownerViewer(owner), nil
}

// LoadOwnedView reads the owner's private encounter with the viewer to render it with. An
// encounter that belongs to a session is not served this way: its owner reaches it through the
// session like any other GM (AuthorizeView), so a removed GM keeps no side door.
func (r *Encounters) LoadOwnedView(ctx context.Context, owner, id string) (Encounter, Viewer, error) {
        e, err := r.Load(ctx, owner, id)
        if err != nil {
                return Encounter{}, Viewer{}, err
        }
        if e.SessionID != "" {
                return Encounter{}, Viewer{}, ErrForbidden
        }
        return e, ownerViewer(owner), nil
}

// LoadForViewer reads one encounter of the viewer's session. A zero viewer, or an encounter in
// another session, is not found: the answer never distinguishes the two.
func (r *Encounters) LoadForViewer(ctx context.Context, v Viewer, id string) (Encounter, error) {
        if v.level == ViewNone || v.session == "" {
                return Encounter{}, ErrForbidden
        }
        id, ok := cleanID(id)
        if !ok {
                return Encounter{}, ErrNotFound
        }
        var e Encounter
        err := r.c.FindOne(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "_id", Value: id}, {Key: "session_id", Value: v.session}, {Key: "deleted_at", Value: nil}}, &e)
        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrNotFound) {
                return Encounter{}, ErrNotFound
        }
        return e, err
}

// ListForViewer reads the viewer's session's encounters in display order, at most limit (the
// store default when zero).
func (r *Encounters) ListForViewer(ctx context.Context, v Viewer, limit int64) ([]Encounter, error) {
        if v.level == ViewNone || v.session == "" {
                return nil, ErrForbidden
        }
        var out []Encounter
        err := r.c.Find(ctx, bson.D{{Key: "session_id", Value: v.session}, {Key: "deleted_at", Value: nil}},
                store.FindOptions{Sort: bson.D{{Key: "position", Value: 1}, {Key: "_id", Value: 1}}, Limit: limit}, &out)
        return out, err
}

// ListOwnedViews returns one page of owner's private encounters (those with no session) and the
// viewer to render them with. Filtering on session_id happens in the query, so pages are full and
// the cursor is stable; session encounters are read through the session.
func (r *Encounters) ListOwnedViews(ctx context.Context, owner string, q Query) (Page[Encounter], Viewer, error) {
        if err := checkUserID(owner); err != nil {
                return Page[Encounter]{}, Viewer{}, err
        }
        base := append(scope(owner), bson.E{Key: "session_id", Value: nil})
        page, err := runQuery[Encounter](ctx, r.c, r.spec, base, q)
        return page, ownerViewer(owner), err
}

// OwnedViewer returns the viewer for rendering e, an encounter the caller has just read or written
// on the owner path. It refuses an encounter that is not the owner's or that belongs to a session,
// so an owner viewer is never issued for data the owner path may not show.
func (r *Encounters) OwnedViewer(owner string, e Encounter) (Viewer, error) {
        if checkUserID(owner) != nil || e.OwnerUserID != owner || e.SessionID != "" {
                return Viewer{}, ErrForbidden
        }
        return ownerViewer(owner), nil
}

// RequireSystem rejects a game system that has no definition.
func (r *Encounters) RequireSystem(ctx context.Context, sysID string) error {
        return r.requireSystem(ctx, sysID)
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "log/slog"
        "maps"
        "slices"
        "strings"
        "unicode"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
)

// ErrChangeStale means an approved change no longer applies: the combatant is gone, its
// requester no longer controls it, or the values no longer validate against the game system. The
// request stays pending.
var ErrChangeStale = errors.New("domain: the requested change no longer applies")

// maxApplyAttempts bounds how often an approval re-reads an encounter that changed under it.
const maxApplyAttempts = 3

// ChangeNotifier is the hook point for telling users about change requests (the GM and co-GMs
// when one is submitted, the requester when it is decided). Events arrive in a later change; an
// implementation must not block or fail the request, and the default does nothing.
type ChangeNotifier interface {
        ChangeSubmitted(ctx context.Context, req data.ChangeRequest)
        ChangeDecided(ctx context.Context, req data.ChangeRequest)
}

// maxRejectReason bounds a rejection reason, in runes.
const maxRejectReason = 500

// ChangeInput is what a player proposes: health values to set, and/or conditions to add (or
// update by name) and remove by name.
type ChangeInput struct {
        Health           map[string]int
        AddConditions    []data.Condition
        RemoveConditions []string
}

func (in ChangeInput) empty() bool {
        return len(in.Health) == 0 && len(in.AddConditions) == 0 && len(in.RemoveConditions) == 0
}

// ChangeRequests implements change requests: submit, list, approve, and reject.
type ChangeRequests struct {
        repo      *data.ChangeRequests
        encounter *Encounters
        sessions  *Sessions
        // Notifier is told about submissions and decisions; nil means nobody is.
        Notifier ChangeNotifier
}

// NewChangeRequests builds the service on db.
func NewChangeRequests(db store.Database, encounters *Encounters, sessions *Sessions) *ChangeRequests {
        return &ChangeRequests{repo: data.NewChangeRequests(db), encounter: encounters, sessions: sessions}
}

// applyChange returns c with in applied. Only health and conditions can change here, so a change
// request can never alter initiative, notes, visibility, or controllers. Health values are set;
// conditions change by name against c as it is now: removals first, then each added condition
// replaces the one with its name or is appended. Applying it twice gives the same result.
func applyChange(c data.Combatant, in ChangeInput) data.Combatant {
        if len(in.Health) > 0 {
                merged := maps.Clone(c.Health)
                if merged == nil {
                        merged = map[string]int{}
                }
                maps.Copy(merged, in.Health)
                c.Health = merged
        }
        if len(in.AddConditions) > 0 || len(in.RemoveConditions) > 0 {
                conds := slices.DeleteFunc(slices.Clone(c.Conditions), func(x data.Condition) bool {
                        return slices.Contains(in.RemoveConditions, x.Name)
                })
                for _, add := range in.AddConditions {
                        if i := slices.IndexFunc(conds, func(x data.Condition) bool { return x.Name == add.Name }); i >= 0 {
                                conds[i] = add
                        } else {
                                conds = append(conds, add)
                        }
                }
                if conds == nil {
                        conds = []data.Condition{}
                }
                c.Conditions = conds
        }
        return c
}

// checkInput validates the shape of a proposal before any encounter is read.
func checkInput(in ChangeInput) error {
        if in.empty() {
                return invalid("a change request needs health values or conditions")
        }
        if len(in.AddConditions) > data.MaxConditions || len(in.RemoveConditions) > data.MaxConditions {
                return invalid("at most %d conditions can be added or removed in one request", data.MaxConditions)
        }
        for _, name := range in.RemoveConditions {
                if strings.TrimSpace(name) == "" {
                        return invalid("a condition to remove needs a name")
                }
                if err := data.CheckText("condition name", name, data.MaxNameLen); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
        }
        return nil
}

// Submit records the player's change request for a combatant they control. The caller must hold
// a live player Viewer on the session and be listed on the combatant now: an observer, a GM, a
// removed member, and a stale or demoted controller are all refused. The change is validated
// against the game system at submission as well as at approval.
func (s *ChangeRequests) Submit(ctx context.Context, actor, sessionID, encounterID, combatantID string, in ChangeInput) (data.ChangeRequest, error) {
        if err := checkInput(in); err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        if sess, _, _, err := s.sessions.as(ctx, actor, sessionID, ActView); err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        } else if sess.InactiveAt != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, data.ErrInactive
        }
        v, err := s.sessions.Viewer(ctx, actor, sessionID)
        if err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        if v.Level() != data.ViewPlayer {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, data.ErrForbidden
        }
        enc, err := s.encounter.repo.LoadForViewer(ctx, v, encounterID)
        if err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        c, ok := findLive(enc, combatantID)
        if !ok || (c.Hidden && !controls(c, v)) {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, data.ErrNotFound
        }
        if !controls(c, v) {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, data.ErrForbidden
        }
        switch {
        case enc.LockedAt != nil:
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, data.ErrLocked
        case enc.InactiveAt != nil:
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, data.ErrInactive
        }
        if err := s.encounter.repo.ValidateCombatantChange(ctx, enc.GameSystemID, c, applyChange(c, in)); err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        req, err := s.repo.Create(ctx, v, data.ChangeRequest{
                EncounterID: enc.ID, CombatantID: c.ID, Health: in.Health, AddConditions: in.AddConditions,
                RemoveConditions: in.RemoveConditions, BaseRevision: enc.Revision,
        })
        if err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        if s.Notifier != nil {
                s.Notifier.ChangeSubmitted(ctx, req)
        }
        return req, nil
}

func findLive(enc data.Encounter, combatantID string) (data.Combatant, bool) {
        i := slices.IndexFunc(enc.Combatants, func(c data.Combatant) bool { return c.ID == combatantID && !c.Removed })
        if i < 0 {
                return data.Combatant{}, false
        }
        return enc.Combatants[i], true
}

// List returns one page of the encounter's change requests: all of them for a GM or co-GM, the
// caller's own for a player, and an observer is refused. status narrows to one status.
func (s *ChangeRequests) List(ctx context.Context, actor, sessionID, encounterID, status string, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.ChangeRequest], error) {
        v, err := s.sessions.Viewer(ctx, actor, sessionID)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Page[data.ChangeRequest]{}, err
        }
        if _, err := s.encounter.repo.LoadForViewer(ctx, v, encounterID); err != nil {
                return data.Page[data.ChangeRequest]{}, err
        }
        return s.repo.List(ctx, v, encounterID, status, q)
}

// Approve applies a pending request through the same atomic, revision-bumping encounter write as
// any other edit, and records who approved it. The change is re-validated against the encounter
// as it is now, and the request's requester must still control the combatant as a player; a
// change that no longer applies is ErrChangeStale and the request stays pending. An encounter
// that moved on since the request was submitted is not a reason to fail: the request is applied
// to the current revision.
//
// The request moves pending -> approving -> approved. The claim is taken before the write, so a
// concurrent reject or second approval loses; if the write fails the claim is undone. A claim that
// is never finished (a crash) is younger than ClaimTimeout: 409, retry later; older: any GM's
// approval resumes it, which is safe because the change is idempotent.
func (s *ChangeRequests) Approve(ctx context.Context, actor, sessionID, encounterID, requestID string) (data.ChangeRequest, error) {
        _, _, d, err := s.sessions.as(ctx, actor, sessionID, ActManageMembers)
        if err != nil {
