                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        viewer, err := s.sessions.Viewer(ctx, actor, sessionID)
        if err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        req, err := s.repo.Load(ctx, d, encounterID, requestID)
        if err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        next, enc, err := s.build(ctx, viewer, d, req)
        if err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        if req, err = s.repo.Claim(ctx, d, encounterID, requestID); err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        applied, err := s.write(ctx, viewer, d, req, next, enc)
        // Settling the claim must survive the caller going away: a request cancelled after the
        // encounter write would otherwise stay approving, and a later approval would re-apply values
        // that may be stale by then.
        settle := context.WithoutCancel(ctx)
        if err != nil {
                if uerr := s.repo.Unclaim(settle, d, encounterID, req.ID); uerr != nil {
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "could not release a change request claim after a failed approval; a GM can resume it once the claim is older than the timeout",
                                "request", req.ID, "session", sessionID, "encounter", encounterID, "error", uerr)
                }
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        done, err := s.repo.Finish(settle, d, encounterID, req.ID, applied.Revision)
        if err != nil {
                // The change is applied but the request was not recorded as approved: another GM resumed
                // it after the timeout, it was cancelled, or the store failed. A resume re-applies the
                // idempotent change, so report what happened.
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "change applied but the request was not recorded as approved", "request", req.ID,
                        "session", sessionID, "encounter", encounterID, "applied_revision", applied.Revision, "error", err)
                req.Status, req.AppliedRevision = data.ChangeApproved, applied.Revision
                done = req
        }
        if s.Notifier != nil {
                s.Notifier.ChangeDecided(ctx, done)
        }
        return done, nil
}

// write stores next as the encounter's combatants at enc's revision. If the encounter changed
// since it was read it is re-read and the change rebuilt and re-validated, up to
// maxApplyAttempts times.
func (s *ChangeRequests) write(ctx context.Context, v data.Viewer, d data.Decision, req data.ChangeRequest, next []data.Combatant, enc data.Encounter) (data.Encounter, error) {
        for attempt := 0; ; attempt++ {
                updated, err := s.encounter.repo.UpdateInSession(data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventCombatantUpdated, req.CombatantID), d, enc.ID, enc.Revision, map[string]any{"combatants": next})
                if !errors.Is(err, data.ErrRevisionConflict) || attempt+1 == maxApplyAttempts {
                        return updated, err
                }
                if next, enc, err = s.build(ctx, v, d, req); err != nil {
                        return data.Encounter{}, err
                }
        }
}

// build loads the encounter fresh and returns its combatants with req applied, and the encounter
// they were built from. Anything that stops the change applying is ErrChangeStale.
func (s *ChangeRequests) build(ctx context.Context, v data.Viewer, d data.Decision, req data.ChangeRequest) ([]data.Combatant, data.Encounter, error) {
        enc, err := s.encounter.repo.LoadForViewer(ctx, v, req.EncounterID)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, enc, err
        }
        i := slices.IndexFunc(enc.Combatants, func(c data.Combatant) bool { return c.ID == req.CombatantID && !c.Removed })
        if i < 0 {
                return nil, enc, fmt.Errorf("%w: the combatant is gone", ErrChangeStale)
        }
        c := enc.Combatants[i]
        if !slices.Contains(c.ControllerUserIDs, req.RequestedBy) {
                return nil, enc, fmt.Errorf("%w: the requester no longer controls the combatant", ErrChangeStale)
        }
        m, err := s.sessions.repo.Member(ctx, d, req.RequestedBy)
        switch {
        case errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound):
                return nil, enc, fmt.Errorf("%w: the requester has left the session", ErrChangeStale)
        case err != nil:
                return nil, enc, err
        case m.Role != data.RolePlayer:
                return nil, enc, fmt.Errorf("%w: the requester is no longer a player", ErrChangeStale)
        }
        changed := applyChange(c, ChangeInput{Health: req.Health, AddConditions: req.AddConditions, RemoveConditions: req.RemoveConditions})
        if err := s.encounter.repo.ValidateCombatantChange(ctx, enc.GameSystemID, c, changed); err != nil {
                if errors.Is(err, data.ErrInvalid) {
                        return nil, enc, fmt.Errorf("%w: %s", ErrChangeStale, strings.TrimPrefix(err.Error(), data.ErrInvalid.Error()+": "))
                }
                return nil, enc, err
        }
        cs := slices.Clone(enc.Combatants)
        cs[i] = changed
        return cs, enc, nil
}

// Reject settles a pending request with a reason; the combatant is unchanged and the requester
// sees the reason.
func (s *ChangeRequests) Reject(ctx context.Context, actor, sessionID, encounterID, requestID, reason string) (data.ChangeRequest, error) {
        reason = strings.TrimSpace(strings.Map(func(r rune) rune {
                if unicode.IsControl(r) {
                        return -1
                }
                return r
        }, reason))
        if reason == "" {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, invalid("a reason is required to reject a change request")
        }
        if err := data.CheckText("reason", reason, maxRejectReason); err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        _, _, d, err := s.sessions.as(ctx, actor, sessionID, ActManageMembers)
        if err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        req, err := s.repo.Reject(ctx, d, encounterID, requestID, reason)
        if err != nil {
                return data.ChangeRequest{}, err
        }
        if s.Notifier != nil {
                s.Notifier.ChangeDecided(ctx, req)
        }
        return req, nil
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "maps"
        "slices"
        "strings"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// CombatantPatch is a partial combatant edit: nil fields are left alone. Health values are merged
// over the stored map (keys not in the patch keep their values); the merged map is what gets
// validated.
type CombatantPatch struct {
        Name       *string
        Kind       *string
        Initiative *float64
        Notes      *string
        Health     map[string]int
        // Visibility settings (initiative-player-access).
        Hidden           *bool
        HealthVisibility *string
        NotesShared      *bool
        // Conditions replaces the combatant's conditions when non-nil; the data layer validates each.
        Conditions *[]data.Condition
}

func newCombatant(c data.Combatant, id string) (data.Combatant, error) {
        c.Name = strings.TrimSpace(c.Name)
        if c.Name == "" {
                return c, invalid("combatant name is required")
        }
        if !slices.Contains(kinds, c.Kind) {
                return c, invalid("combatant kind %q", c.Kind)
        }
        c.ID, c.Removed = id, false
        c.Health, c.ExternalRefs = maps.Clone(c.Health), maps.Clone(c.ExternalRefs)
        c.Conditions = slices.Clone(c.Conditions)
        if c.Conditions == nil {
                c.Conditions = []data.Condition{}
        }
        return c, nil
}

func findCombatant(st *data.EncounterState, id string) (*data.Combatant, error) {
        i := slices.IndexFunc(st.Combatants, func(c data.Combatant) bool { return c.ID == id })
        if i < 0 {
                return nil, data.ErrNotFound
        }
        return &st.Combatants[i], nil
}

// AddCombatant appends c to the encounter with a new ID.
func (s *Encounters) AddCombatant(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, c data.Combatant) (data.Encounter, error) {
        n, err := newCombatant(c, s.newID())
        if err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        ctx = data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventCombatantAdded, n.ID)
        return s.editQueue(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(st *data.EncounterState, _ data.Encounter) error {
                st.Combatants = append(st.Combatants, n)
                return nil
        })
}

// UpdateCombatant applies patch to the combatant with ID combatantID.
func (s *Encounters) UpdateCombatant(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, combatantID string, patch CombatantPatch) (data.Encounter, error) {
        ctx = data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventCombatantUpdated, combatantID)
        return s.editQueue(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(st *data.EncounterState, _ data.Encounter) error {
                c, err := findCombatant(st, combatantID)
                if err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                return applyPatch(c, patch)
        })
}

func applyPatch(c *data.Combatant, p CombatantPatch) error {
        if p.Name != nil {
                name := strings.TrimSpace(*p.Name)
                if name == "" {
                        return invalid("combatant name is required")
                }
                c.Name = name
        }
        if p.Kind != nil {
                if !slices.Contains(kinds, *p.Kind) {
                        return invalid("combatant kind %q", *p.Kind)
                }
                c.Kind = *p.Kind
        }
        if p.Initiative != nil {
                v := *p.Initiative
                c.Initiative = &v
        }
        if p.Notes != nil {
                c.Notes = *p.Notes
        }
        if p.Conditions != nil {
                c.Conditions = slices.Clone(*p.Conditions)
                if c.Conditions == nil {
                        c.Conditions = []data.Condition{}
                }
        }
        if p.Hidden != nil {
                c.Hidden = *p.Hidden
        }
        if p.NotesShared != nil {
                c.NotesShared = *p.NotesShared
        }
        if p.HealthVisibility != nil {
                if !slices.Contains([]string{data.HealthFull, data.HealthStatus, data.HealthHidden}, *p.HealthVisibility) {
                        return invalid("health visibility %q", *p.HealthVisibility)
                }
                c.HealthVisibility = *p.HealthVisibility
        }
        if p.Health != nil {
                merged := maps.Clone(c.Health)
                if merged == nil {
                        merged = map[string]int{}
                }
                maps.Copy(merged, p.Health)
                c.Health = merged
        }
        return nil
}

// RemoveCombatant marks the combatant removed. It stays in the record and leaves the turn queue;
// if it was current, the turn passes to the combatant that takes its place in the queue.
func (s *Encounters) RemoveCombatant(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, combatantID string) (data.Encounter, error) {
        ctx = data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventCombatantRemoved, combatantID)
        return s.editQueue(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(st *data.EncounterState, _ data.Encounter) error {
                c, err := findCombatant(st, combatantID)
                if err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                c.Removed = true
                return nil
        })
}

// RestoreCombatant clears the removed mark and puts the combatant back in the turn queue.
func (s *Encounters) RestoreCombatant(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, combatantID string) (data.Encounter, error) {
        ctx = data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventCombatantRestored, combatantID)
        return s.editQueue(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(st *data.EncounterState, _ data.Encounter) error {
                c, err := findCombatant(st, combatantID)
                if err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                c.Removed = false
                return nil
        })
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "slices"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// controllerRoles are the member roles that may control a combatant. An observer has no control
// over any combatant (initiative-player-access), so assigning one is refused.
var controllerRoles = []string{data.RoleGM, data.RoleCoGM, data.RolePlayer}

// AssignControllers sets the controllers of one combatant of a session's encounter, at the
// encounter revision rev, and returns the encounter as the caller (a GM or co-GM) sees it. The
// list may be empty. Every listed user must hold a live member record in the session with a
// controlling role, checked now against the session's records; a non-member or an observer is an
// invalid argument and nothing is written. The write is one atomic compare-and-set on the
// revision (data.Encounters.UpdateInSession).
func (s *Encounters) AssignControllers(ctx context.Context, sessions *Sessions, actor, sessionID, encounterID, combatantID string, userIDs []string, rev int64) (EncounterView, error) {
        _, _, d, err := sessions.as(ctx, actor, sessionID, ActManageMembers)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        viewer, err := sessions.Viewer(ctx, actor, sessionID)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        if len(userIDs) > data.MaxCombatants {
                return nil, invalid("at most %d controllers", data.MaxCombatants)
        }
        ids, err := sessions.controllersOf(ctx, d, userIDs)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        enc, err := s.repo.LoadForViewer(ctx, viewer, encounterID)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        if enc.Revision != rev {
                return nil, data.ErrRevisionConflict
        }
        cs := slices.Clone(enc.Combatants)
        i := slices.IndexFunc(cs, func(c data.Combatant) bool { return c.ID == combatantID && !c.Removed })
        if i < 0 {
                return nil, data.ErrNotFound
        }
        cs[i].ControllerUserIDs = ids
        updated, err := s.repo.UpdateInSession(data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventControllers, combatantID), d, encounterID, rev, map[string]any{"combatants": cs})
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        // A listed user may have left between the membership check and the write. If so, scrub them
        // and report a conflict so the GM reassigns against the new membership.
        if gone := sessions.notControllers(ctx, d, ids); len(gone) > 0 {
                for _, u := range gone {
                        sessions.scrubControllers(ctx, d, u)
                }
                return nil, data.ErrConflict
        }
        return s.render(ctx, updated, viewer)
}

// notControllers returns the users in ids who no longer hold a controlling membership. A lookup
// that fails for any reason counts as "gone": the user is then scrubbed, which a legitimate
// controller can undo by being assigned again, whereas keeping an unverified id could not be.
func (s *Sessions) notControllers(ctx context.Context, d data.Decision, ids []string) []string {
        var gone []string
        for _, u := range ids {
                m, err := s.repo.Member(ctx, d, u)
                if err != nil || !slices.Contains(controllerRoles, m.Role) {
                        gone = append(gone, u)
                }
        }
        return gone
}

// controllersOf validates userIDs against the session's live member records and returns them
// de-duplicated, in order. nil for an empty list.
func (s *Sessions) controllersOf(ctx context.Context, d data.Decision, userIDs []string) ([]string, error) {
        var out []string
        for _, u := range userIDs {
                if slices.Contains(out, u) {
                        continue
                }
                m, err := s.repo.Member(ctx, d, u)
                if err != nil && !isNotFoundOrInvalid(err) {
                        return nil, err
                }
                if err != nil {
                        return nil, invalid("%q is not a member of this session", u)
                }
                if !slices.Contains(controllerRoles, m.Role) {
                        return nil, invalid("%q cannot control a combatant: an observer has no control", u)
                }
                out = append(out, u)
        }
        return out, nil
}

func isNotFoundOrInvalid(err error) bool {
        return errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) || errors.Is(err, data.ErrInvalid)
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "crypto/rand"
        "crypto/sha256"
        "encoding/base64"
        "encoding/hex"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "slices"
        "strings"
        "sync/atomic"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/entitlements"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
)

// KeyIntegrationCredentials is the entitlement limit on active integration credentials.
const KeyIntegrationCredentials = "initiative.integration_credentials"

// SecretPrefix starts every integration secret, so the auth middleware can tell one from a user
// token without a lookup.
const SecretPrefix = "ic_"

const maxLabel = 100

// Credentials issues, lists, revokes, and authenticates integration credentials.
type Credentials struct {
        repo *data.Credentials
        open atomic.Bool
}

// ErrCredentialsClosed means integration credentials are off: the auth-event consumer is not
// caught up, or it fell behind the stream's retention and awaits a reconcile (PADR-0039). It is
// fail closed, so no credential authenticates and none is issued.
var ErrCredentialsClosed = errors.New("integration credentials are closed")

// NewCredentials builds the service on db.
func NewCredentials(db store.Database) *Credentials {
        return &Credentials{repo: data.NewCredentials(db)}
}

// hashSecret is the stored form of a secret. The secret is 256 random bits, so a plain SHA-256 is
// enough; a slow hash would only add cost to every request.
func hashSecret(secret string) string {
        sum := sha256.Sum256([]byte(secret))
        return hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])
}

func newSecret() (string, error) {
        b := make([]byte, 32)
        if _, err := rand.Read(b); err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        return SecretPrefix + base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(b), nil
}

func validateScopes(scopes []string) error {
        if len(scopes) == 0 {
                return fmt.Errorf("%w: at least one scope is required", data.ErrInvalid)
        }
        for _, s := range scopes {
                if s != data.ScopeRead && s != data.ScopeWrite {
                        return fmt.Errorf("%w: unknown scope %q", data.ErrInvalid, s)
                }
        }
        return nil
}

// Open lets credentials work, unless a persisted close from an earlier run is still in force.
// Call it once the auth-event consumer has caught up. It reports whether the gate is now open.
func (s *Credentials) Open(ctx context.Context) (bool, error) {
        closed, err := s.repo.GateClosed(ctx)
        if err != nil || closed {
                return false, err
        }
        s.open.Store(true)
        return true, nil
}

// Pause turns credentials off in this process without persisting it: Open reopens them (unless a
// persisted close is in force) once the auth-event consumer has caught up after an outage.
func (s *Credentials) Pause() { s.open.Store(false) }

// Close turns credentials off and persists that, so a restart stays closed until Reopen.
func (s *Credentials) Close(ctx context.Context) error {
        s.open.Store(false)
        return s.repo.SetGate(ctx, true, "retention-gap")
}

// Reopen clears a persisted close on an administrator's say-so; actor is recorded on the gate.
func (s *Credentials) Reopen(ctx context.Context, actor string) error {
        if err := s.repo.SetGate(ctx, false, actor); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        s.open.Store(true)
        return nil
}

// RevokeOwner ends every active credential of an owner the auth service has denied or stripped of
// a role, and returns how many it ended. Credentials stay for audit.
func (s *Credentials) RevokeOwner(ctx context.Context, owner, reason string) (int, error) {
        if reason != data.ReasonOwnerDenied && reason != data.ReasonOwnerRoleRemoved {
                return 0, fmt.Errorf("%w: unknown revoke reason %q", data.ErrInvalid, reason)
        }
        return s.repo.RevokeAllByOwner(ctx, owner, reason)
}

// Create issues a credential for owner and returns it with its secret, which is never available
// again. At or over the entitled limit of active credentials it returns *EntitlementError.
func (s *Credentials) Create(ctx context.Context, owner, label string, scopes []string, chk Checker) (data.IntegrationCredential, string, error) {
        if !s.open.Load() {
                return data.IntegrationCredential{}, "", ErrCredentialsClosed
        }
        label = strings.TrimSpace(label)
        if label == "" || len([]rune(label)) > maxLabel {
                return data.IntegrationCredential{}, "", fmt.Errorf("%w: label is required and at most %d characters", data.ErrInvalid, maxLabel)
        }
        if err := validateScopes(scopes); err != nil {
                return data.IntegrationCredential{}, "", err
        }
        res, err := chk.Check(ctx, KeyIntegrationCredentials, entitlements.FailClosed)
        if err != nil {
                return data.IntegrationCredential{}, "", err
        }
        // ponytail: count-then-insert can overshoot the limit by the number of concurrent creates.
        // Tighten with a per-owner slot index if that ever matters.
        n, err := s.repo.CountActive(ctx, owner)
        if err != nil {
                return data.IntegrationCredential{}, "", err
        }
        if n >= int64(res.Limit) {
                return data.IntegrationCredential{}, "", &EntitlementError{Key: KeyIntegrationCredentials, Limit: res.Limit}
        }
        secret, err := newSecret()
        if err != nil {
                return data.IntegrationCredential{}, "", err
        }
        c := data.IntegrationCredential{Label: label, Scopes: slices.Compact(slices.Sorted(slices.Values(scopes))), SecretHash: hashSecret(secret)}
        if err := s.repo.Create(ctx, owner, owner, &c); err != nil {
                return data.IntegrationCredential{}, "", err
        }
        return c, secret, nil
}

// List returns owner's credentials, newest first. Revoked ones stay listed.
func (s *Credentials) List(ctx context.Context, owner string, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.IntegrationCredential], error) {
        return s.repo.List(ctx, owner, q)
}

// Revoke ends owner's credential at once; repeating it is harmless.
func (s *Credentials) Revoke(ctx context.Context, owner, id string) (data.IntegrationCredential, error) {
        return s.repo.Revoke(ctx, owner, owner, id)
}

// Authenticate resolves a presented secret to its credential and records the use. A revoked,
// deleted, or unknown secret is data.ErrNotFound; the next request after a revoke fails here.
func (s *Credentials) Authenticate(ctx context.Context, secret string) (data.IntegrationCredential, error) {
        if !strings.HasPrefix(secret, SecretPrefix) {
                return data.IntegrationCredential{}, data.ErrNotFound
        }
        if !s.open.Load() {
                return data.IntegrationCredential{}, ErrCredentialsClosed
        }
        return s.repo.Authenticate(ctx, hashSecret(secret))
}

// ListAll returns credentials across owners for an administrator, optionally for one owner.
func (s *Credentials) ListAll(ctx context.Context, owner string, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.IntegrationCredential], error) {
        if owner != "" {
                return s.repo.ListOwned(ctx, owner, q)
        }
        return s.repo.ListAll(ctx, q)
}

// DeletePermanently removes a credential for good at an administrator's say-so and records the
// reason. The next request with its secret fails, and Live turns false for open streams.
func (s *Credentials) DeletePermanently(ctx context.Context, admin, id, reason string) (data.CredentialDeletion, error) {
        return s.repo.DeletePermanently(ctx, admin, id, reason)
}

// Deletions lists the audit entries of administrator deletions, optionally for one owner.
func (s *Credentials) Deletions(ctx context.Context, owner string, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.CredentialDeletion], error) {
        return s.repo.Deletions(ctx, owner, q)
}

// Live reports whether credential id can still authenticate. A stream authenticated by a
// credential calls it on each heartbeat or event and closes when it turns false.
func (s *Credentials) Live(ctx context.Context, id string) (bool, error) {
        if !s.open.Load() {
                return false, nil
        }
        return s.repo.Live(ctx, id)
}

// Items is the credential repository as limit enforcement sees it; revoked credentials are not counted.
func (s *Credentials) Items() Limited { return s.repo }

package domain

import (
        "context"
        cryptorand "crypto/rand"
        "encoding/hex"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "log/slog"
        "math/rand/v2"
        "slices"
        "strings"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
)

// randomID is a combatant ID: 12 random bytes in hex. It is not an ObjectID, whose counter and
// timestamp would let a player see gaps where hidden combatants were added between visible ones.
func randomID() string {
        b := make([]byte, 12)
        _, _ = cryptorand.Read(b)
        return hex.EncodeToString(b)
}

// ErrTransition is returned for a status change the encounter's current status does not allow.
// It wraps data.ErrInvalid.
var ErrTransition = fmt.Errorf("%w: status transition not allowed", data.ErrInvalid)

// Encounters implements encounter and combatant rules. Turn order (turn_queue) is computed by the
// ordering engine, not here: adding, restoring, or re-scoring a combatant leaves the queue to it.
type Encounters struct {
        repo    *data.Encounters
        themes  *data.Owned[data.Theme, *data.Theme]
        systems *data.SystemRepo
        newID   func() string
        perm    func(n int) []int
}

// NewEncounters builds the encounter service on db.
func NewEncounters(db store.Database) *Encounters {
        return &Encounters{
                repo:    data.NewEncounters(db),
                themes:  data.NewOwned[data.Theme](db, data.ThemeSpec),
                systems: data.NewSystemRepo(db),
                newID:   randomID,
                perm:    rand.Perm,
        }
}

// checkTheme accepts no theme, a packaged theme, or a custom theme the owner has. Another user's
// theme and a made-up ID are the same refusal, so a probe learns nothing about other accounts.
func checkTheme(ctx context.Context, themes *data.Owned[data.Theme, *data.Theme], owner, id string) error {
        if id == "" || strings.HasPrefix(id, PackagedPrefix) {
                return nil
        }
        if _, err := themes.Get(ctx, owner, id); err != nil {
                if errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) {
                        return invalid("theme_id is not one of your themes")
                }
                return err
        }
        return nil
}

// Create stores enc as owner's new draft encounter. The name is required, the game system must
// exist, and any combatants supplied get server-assigned IDs.
func (s *Encounters) Create(ctx context.Context, owner, actor string, enc data.Encounter) (data.Encounter, error) {
        // An encounter joins a session only through CreateInSession, which the session's own rules
        // authorize; a caller-supplied session id here is refused.
        if enc.SessionID != "" {
                return data.Encounter{}, invalid("an encounter cannot be created inside a session here")
        }
        return s.create(ctx, owner, actor, enc, requireSystem)
}

// CreateInSession stores enc as a new draft encounter of the grant's session, owned by the GM, and
// returns it as the grantee sees it. The grant (Sessions.AuthorizeEncounterCreate) carries the
// decisions: who may create, the limit, and which game systems the owner is entitled to. Two
// creates can both pass the limit check, so the new encounter is recounted after the insert and
// retracted if it put the owner over the limit.
// ponytail: concurrent creates near the limit may all be retracted; none is ever kept over it.
func (s *Encounters) CreateInSession(ctx context.Context, g EncounterGrant, enc data.Encounter) (EncounterView, error) {
        enc.SessionID = g.sessionID
        created, err := s.create(ctx, g.Owner, g.actor, enc, g.systemOK)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        n, err := s.repo.CountAll(ctx, g.Owner)
        if err == nil && n > int64(g.limit) {
                err = &EntitlementError{Key: KeySavedEncounters, Limit: g.limit}
        }
        if err != nil {
                // The encounter must not stay live over the limit while the caller is told it failed: a
                // retry would add another, and the limit reconciler never lists session encounters. If it
                // cannot be retracted, report that (a server error) instead of the limit.
                if rerr := s.repo.RetractNew(ctx, g.decision, created.ID); rerr != nil {
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "retracting an over-limit session encounter failed", "encounter", created.ID, "error", rerr)
                        return nil, fmt.Errorf("could not undo a session encounter created over the limit: %w", rerr)
                }
                return nil, err
        }
        return s.render(ctx, created, g.viewer)
}

func (s *Encounters) create(ctx context.Context, owner, actor string, enc data.Encounter, systemOK func(context.Context, string) error) (data.Encounter, error) {
        if err := checkTheme(ctx, s.themes, owner, enc.ThemeID); err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        enc.Name = strings.TrimSpace(enc.Name)
        if enc.Name == "" {
                return data.Encounter{}, invalid("encounter name is required")
        }
        _, err := s.systems.Get(ctx, enc.GameSystemID)
        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) {
                return data.Encounter{}, invalid("the encounter's game system is not defined")
        }
        if err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        if err := systemOK(ctx, enc.GameSystemID); err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        combatants := make([]data.Combatant, 0, len(enc.Combatants))
        for _, c := range enc.Combatants {
                n, err := newCombatant(c, s.newID())
                if err != nil {
                        return data.Encounter{}, err
                }
                combatants = append(combatants, n)
        }
        enc.Combatants = combatants
        if err := s.repo.Create(ctx, owner, actor, &enc); err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        return enc, nil
}

// Get returns owner's encounter.
func (s *Encounters) Get(ctx context.Context, owner, id string) (data.Encounter, error) {
        return s.repo.Get(ctx, owner, id)
}

// List returns a page of owner's encounters.
func (s *Encounters) List(ctx context.Context, owner string, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.Encounter], error) {
        return s.repo.List(ctx, owner, q)
}

// Count returns how many live encounters owner holds, private and in a session alike: both count
// against the saved-encounters limit. The limit reconciler's item list is the owner's private
// encounters only (data.NewEncounters narrows reads to session_id nil), so an owner over a reduced
// limit through session encounters clears it by deleting some or by ending the session.
func (s *Encounters) Count(ctx context.Context, owner string) (int64, error) {
        return s.repo.CountAll(ctx, owner)
}

// UpdateSettings sets the encounter's settings (name, game_system_id, ordering, tie_breaker,
// display flags, theme_id, position). Combat state cannot be set here, but a new ordering, tie
// breaker, or game system recomputes the turn queue in the same write.
func (s *Encounters) UpdateSettings(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, set map[string]any) (data.Encounter, error) {
        if name, ok := set["name"].(string); ok {
                set["name"] = strings.TrimSpace(name)
        }
        if theme, ok := set["theme_id"].(string); ok {
                if err := checkTheme(ctx, s.themes, owner, theme); err != nil {
                        return data.Encounter{}, err
                }
        }
        if sysID, ok := set["game_system_id"].(string); ok {
                if err := requireSystem(ctx, sysID); err != nil {
                        return data.Encounter{}, err
                }
        }
        _, ord := set["ordering"]
        _, tie := set["tie_breaker"]
        _, sys := set["game_system_id"]
        if !ord && !tie && !sys {
                return s.repo.Update(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, set)
        }
        cur, err := s.repo.Load(ctx, owner, id)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        o, t, sysID := cur.Ordering, cur.TieBreaker, cur.GameSystemID
        if v, ok := set["ordering"].(string); ok {
                o = v
        }
        if v, ok := set["game_system_id"].(string); ok {
                sysID = v
        }
        if v, ok := set["tie_breaker"].(string); ok {
                t = v
        }
        st := stateOf(cur)
        s.recompute(&st, effectiveOrdering(o, sysID), t)
        return s.repo.Write(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, set, st)
}

func stateOf(cur data.Encounter) data.EncounterState {
        return data.EncounterState{
                Status: cur.Status, Round: cur.Round, CurrentCombatantID: cur.CurrentCombatantID,
                TurnQueue: slices.Clone(cur.TurnQueue), Combatants: slices.Clone(cur.Combatants),
        }
}

// editQueue is edit followed by a turn-queue recompute under the encounter's ordering.
func (s *Encounters) editQueue(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, change func(*data.EncounterState, data.Encounter) error) (data.Encounter, error) {
        return s.edit(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(st *data.EncounterState, cur data.Encounter) error {
                if err := change(st, cur); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                s.recompute(st, effectiveOrdering(cur.Ordering, cur.GameSystemID), cur.TieBreaker)
                return nil
        })
}

// Delete soft-deletes the encounter.
func (s *Encounters) Delete(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64) error {
        return s.repo.SoftDelete(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev)
}

// edit reads the encounter, applies change to its state, and writes the result compare-and-set on
// rev. A missed read is classified 403/404 like the write paths.
// change also receives the encounter as read, for its settings.
func (s *Encounters) edit(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, change func(*data.EncounterState, data.Encounter) error) (data.Encounter, error) {
        cur, err := s.repo.Load(ctx, owner, id)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        st := stateOf(cur)
        if err := change(&st, cur); err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        return s.repo.SetState(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, st)
}

// FindByExternalRef returns owner's combatants whose external_refs map key to value.
func (s *Encounters) FindByExternalRef(ctx context.Context, owner, key, value string) ([]data.RefMatch, error) {
        return s.repo.FindByExternalRef(ctx, owner, key, value)
}

// SetSink installs the sink told of every committed encounter change made through this service.
func (s *Encounters) SetSink(k data.Sink) { s.repo.SetSink(k) }

// Items is the encounter repository as limit enforcement sees it.
func (s *Encounters) Items() Limited { return s.repo }

package domain

import (
        "cmp"
        "slices"
        "strings"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// Pure turn-order logic: no I/O, no clock. Randomness comes in as a permutation function.

type entry struct {
        c       data.Combatant
        listIdx int
        prevIdx int // position in the previous queue, -1 if it was not queued
        rank    int // random rank among newly queued combatants
}

func kindGroup(ordering, kind string) int {
        if ordering != data.OrderPCVsAdversary {
                return 0
        }
        switch kind {
        case data.KindPC:
                return 0
        case data.KindAdversary:
                return 1
        }
        return 2
}

// initiativeCmp orders by initiative, unset last. lowToHigh ascends; everything else descends.
func initiativeCmp(ordering string, a, b *float64) int {
        switch {
        case a == nil && b == nil:
                return 0
        case a == nil:
                return 1
        case b == nil:
                return -1
        case ordering == data.OrderLowToHigh:
                return cmp.Compare(*a, *b)
        }
        return cmp.Compare(*b, *a)
}

func tieCmp(tie string, prevLen int, a, b entry) int {
        switch tie {
        case data.TieAlphabetical:
                if c := strings.Compare(strings.ToLower(a.c.Name), strings.ToLower(b.c.Name)); c != 0 {
                        return c
                }
                return strings.Compare(a.c.ID, b.c.ID)
        case data.TieRandom:
                // Combatants already queued keep their relative order, so a recompute never reshuffles
                // ties mid-combat. Newly queued ones fall in behind them in random order.
                return cmp.Compare(randKey(a, prevLen), randKey(b, prevLen))
        }
        return cmp.Compare(a.listIdx, b.listIdx) // data.TieQuery: encounter list order
}

func randKey(e entry, prevLen int) int {
        if e.prevIdx >= 0 {
                return e.prevIdx
        }
        return prevLen + e.rank
}

// buildQueue returns the IDs of the non-removed combatants in turn order. prev is the existing
// queue. perm(n) returns a random permutation of 0..n-1.
//
// playerManaged keeps prev's order, dropping departed combatants and appending new ones in list
// order. The other orderings sort by kind group (pcVsAdversary only: pc, adversary, object), then
// initiative, then the tie breaker.
func buildQueue(ordering, tie string, cs []data.Combatant, prev []string, perm func(int) []int) []string {
        prevIdx := make(map[string]int, len(prev))
        for i, id := range prev {
                prevIdx[id] = i
        }
        var es []entry
        for i, c := range cs {
                if c.Removed {
                        continue
                }
                pi, ok := prevIdx[c.ID]
                if !ok {
                        pi = -1
                }
                es = append(es, entry{c: c, listIdx: i, prevIdx: pi})
        }
        if ordering == data.OrderPlayerManaged {
                return manualQueue(es)
        }
        assignRanks(es, perm)
        slices.SortStableFunc(es, func(a, b entry) int {
                return cmp.Or(
                        cmp.Compare(kindGroup(ordering, a.c.Kind), kindGroup(ordering, b.c.Kind)),
                        initiativeCmp(ordering, a.c.Initiative, b.c.Initiative),
                        tieCmp(tie, len(prev), a, b),
                        cmp.Compare(a.listIdx, b.listIdx),
                )
        })
        return ids(es)
}

func assignRanks(es []entry, perm func(int) []int) {
        var fresh []int
        for i, e := range es {
                if e.prevIdx < 0 {
                        fresh = append(fresh, i)
                }
        }
        if len(fresh) == 0 {
                return
        }
        for r, p := range perm(len(fresh)) {
                es[fresh[r]].rank = p
        }
}

func manualQueue(es []entry) []string {
        slices.SortStableFunc(es, func(a, b entry) int {
                switch {
                case a.prevIdx >= 0 && b.prevIdx >= 0:
                        return cmp.Compare(a.prevIdx, b.prevIdx)
                case a.prevIdx >= 0:
                        return -1
                case b.prevIdx >= 0:
                        return 1
                }
                return cmp.Compare(a.listIdx, b.listIdx)
        })
        return ids(es)
}

func ids(es []entry) []string {
        out := make([]string, 0, len(es))
        for _, e := range es {
                out = append(out, e.c.ID)
        }
        return out
}

// expireConditions applies the start-of-turn rule to c: each timed condition loses one round and
// is removed at zero. Open-ended conditions are untouched. It builds a new slice, so shared
// backing arrays are never written.
func expireConditions(c *data.Combatant) {
        next := make([]data.Condition, 0, len(c.Conditions))
        for _, cond := range c.Conditions {
                if cond.RoundsRemaining == nil {
                        next = append(next, cond)
                        continue
                }
                if left := *cond.RoundsRemaining - 1; left > 0 {
                        next = append(next, data.Condition{Name: cond.Name, RoundsRemaining: &left})
                }
        }
        c.Conditions = next
}

// nextTurn returns the queue position after pos and whether it wrapped past the end. pos -1 (no
// current combatant) yields position 0 without wrapping.
func nextTurn(pos, n int) (idx int, wrapped bool) {
        if pos+1 >= n {
                return 0, pos >= 0
        }
        return pos + 1, false
}

// prevTurn returns the queue position before pos and whether it wrapped past the start.
func prevTurn(pos, n int) (idx int, wrapped bool) {
        if pos <= 0 {
                return n - 1, true
        }
        return pos - 1, false
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "log/slog"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/entitlements"
)

// KeyGameSystemsExtra is the flag that unlocks every game system other than No System.
const KeyGameSystemsExtra = "initiative.game_systems_extra"

// EntitlementRequiredError is the typed denial for a flag the caller does not hold.
type EntitlementRequiredError struct{ Key string }

func (e *EntitlementRequiredError) Error() string {
        return fmt.Sprintf("domain: entitlement %s is required", e.Key)
}

// ErrLimitsNotSynced is returned to an integration credential when the owner's entitlement value it
// needs has not been recorded yet. A credential has no user bearer to read entitlements with, so the
// owner must use Initiative once with their own sign-in (the app's usage and create calls record
// the values). It fails closed.
var ErrLimitsNotSynced = errors.New("domain: the owner's entitlement values have not been recorded yet")

type checkerKey struct{}

// WithChecker attaches the calling user's entitlement checker to ctx, so rules deep in the domain
// (a system switch inside a settings update) can check without every signature carrying it. The
// server sets it per request, bound to that user's own bearer (PADR-0037).
func WithChecker(ctx context.Context, c Checker) context.Context {
        return context.WithValue(ctx, checkerKey{}, c)
}

// requireSystem rejects use of a gated game system by a caller without the flag. No System is
// free. Creates and switches fail closed: with no checker in ctx or entitlements unreachable the
// call is refused.
func requireSystem(ctx context.Context, systemID string) error {
        if systemID == "" || systemID == data.DefaultSystemID {
                return nil
        }
        chk, ok := ctx.Value(checkerKey{}).(Checker)
        if !ok {
                return entitlements.ErrUnavailable
        }
        res, err := chk.Check(ctx, KeyGameSystemsExtra, entitlements.FailClosed)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if !res.Flag {
                return &EntitlementRequiredError{Key: KeyGameSystemsExtra}
        }
        return nil
}

// Locks keeps encounters that use a gated game system in step with the owner's flag: locked while
// it is missing, restored unchanged when it returns.
type Locks struct {
        repo *data.Encounters
}

// Locks returns the lock reconciler for these encounters.
func (s *Encounters) Locks() *Locks { return &Locks{repo: s.repo} }

// Reconcile reads the flag and locks or unlocks owner's encounters to match. When entitlements
// cannot be read it changes nothing, so an outage never locks anyone out.
func (l *Locks) Reconcile(ctx context.Context, owner string, chk Checker) error {
        res, err := chk.Check(ctx, KeyGameSystemsExtra, entitlements.FailOpen)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if res.Degraded {
                return nil
        }
        var n int
        if res.Flag {
                n, err = l.repo.UnlockAll(ctx, owner)
        } else {
                n, err = l.repo.LockGated(ctx, owner)
        }
        if n > 0 {
                slog.InfoContext(ctx, "game-system locks changed", "locked", !res.Flag, "count", n)
        }
        return err
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "log/slog"
        "slices"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/entitlements"
)

// Entitlement keys for the stored-item limits.
const (
        KeySavedRosters    = "initiative.saved_rosters"
        KeySavedEncounters = "initiative.saved_encounters"
        KeySessionsPerGM   = "initiative.sessions_per_gm"
        // KeyMembersPerSession is recorded on each session and enforced when a member is approved.
        KeyMembersPerSession = "initiative.members_per_session"
)

// Limited is a repository whose items can go inactive when the owner's limit falls.
type Limited interface {
        ItemStates(ctx context.Context, owner string) ([]data.ItemState, error)
        SetInactive(ctx context.Context, owner, id string, inactive bool) error
        SetKeepRanks(ctx context.Context, owner string, ids []string) error
}

// Kind is one limited item type: its URL name, its entitlement key, and its items.
type Kind struct {
        Name string
        Key  string
        Repo Limited
}

// Limits keeps only as many of an owner's items active as the entitled limit allows (spec
// initiative-entitlement-gating). It never deletes or archives anything.
type Limits struct {
        kinds []Kind
}

// NewLimits builds the enforcer for kinds.
func NewLimits(kinds ...Kind) *Limits { return &Limits{kinds: kinds} }

// plan returns the IDs to activate and to deactivate so that exactly the first limit items in
// preference order are active: the owner's choice (by rank) first, then oldest first. states must
// arrive oldest first.
func plan(states []data.ItemState, limit int) (activate, deactivate []string) {
        ordered := slices.Clone(states)
        slices.SortStableFunc(ordered, func(a, b data.ItemState) int {
                switch {
                case a.KeepRank > 0 && b.KeepRank > 0:
                        return a.KeepRank - b.KeepRank
                case a.KeepRank > 0:
                        return -1
                case b.KeepRank > 0:
                        return 1
                }
                return 0
        })
        for i, st := range ordered {
                switch {
                case i < limit && st.Inactive:
                        activate = append(activate, st.ID)
                case i >= limit && !st.Inactive:
                        deactivate = append(deactivate, st.ID)
                }
        }
        return activate, deactivate
}

func (l *Limits) kind(name string) (Kind, bool) {
        for _, k := range l.kinds {
                if k.Name == name {
                        return k, true
                }
        }
        return Kind{}, false
}

func (l *Limits) apply(ctx context.Context, owner string, k Kind, limit int) error {
        states, err := k.Repo.ItemStates(ctx, owner)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        activate, deactivate := plan(states, limit)
        for _, id := range deactivate {
                if err := k.Repo.SetInactive(ctx, owner, id, true); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
        }
        for _, id := range activate {
                if err := k.Repo.SetInactive(ctx, owner, id, false); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
        }
        return nil
}

// Reconcile brings every kind in line with owner's current limits. When entitlements cannot be
// read it leaves that kind as it is, so an outage never deactivates anything. It tries every
// kind and returns the first error.
func (l *Limits) Reconcile(ctx context.Context, owner string, chk Checker) error {
        var first error
        for _, k := range l.kinds {
                res, err := chk.Check(ctx, k.Key, entitlements.FailOpen)
                if err == nil && res.Degraded {
                        continue
                }
                if err == nil {
                        err = l.apply(ctx, owner, k, res.Limit)
                }
                if err != nil {
                        slog.WarnContext(ctx, "limit reconcile failed", "kind", k.Name, "error", err)
                        if first == nil {
                                first = err
                        }
                }
        }
        return first
}

// Choose records which items of a kind the owner wants active, in order, then applies the limit.
// ids must be distinct items the owner holds, at most the limit; if fewer than the limit are
// chosen, the remaining places go to the oldest other items. Returns the active IDs afterward.
func (l *Limits) Choose(ctx context.Context, owner, kind string, ids []string, chk Checker) ([]string, error) {
        k, ok := l.kind(kind)
        if !ok {
                return nil, data.ErrNotFound
        }
        res, err := chk.Check(ctx, k.Key, entitlements.FailClosed)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        states, err := k.Repo.ItemStates(ctx, owner)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        if len(ids) > res.Limit {
                return nil, invalid("at most %d items can be active", res.Limit)
        }
        held := make(map[string]bool, len(states))
        for _, st := range states {
                held[st.ID] = true
        }
        seen := map[string]bool{}
        for _, id := range ids {
                if !held[id] || seen[id] {
                        return nil, invalid("ids must be distinct items you own")
                }
                seen[id] = true
        }
        if err := k.Repo.SetKeepRanks(ctx, owner, ids); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        if err := l.apply(ctx, owner, k, res.Limit); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        after, err := k.Repo.ItemStates(ctx, owner)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        active := []string{}
        for _, st := range after {
                if !st.Inactive {
                        active = append(active, st.ID)
                }
        }
        return active, nil
}

package domain

import (
        "context"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
)

// NotificationPrefs lets a session member mute notice types for that session.
type NotificationPrefs struct {
        repo     *data.NotificationPrefs
        sessions *Sessions
}

// NewNotificationPrefs builds the service on db.
func NewNotificationPrefs(db store.Database, sessions *Sessions) *NotificationPrefs {
        return &NotificationPrefs{repo: data.NewNotificationPrefs(db), sessions: sessions}
}

// Get returns the types actor has muted for the session. Only a member may read them.
func (s *NotificationPrefs) Get(ctx context.Context, actor, sessionID string) ([]string, error) {
        if _, _, _, err := s.sessions.as(ctx, actor, sessionID, ActView); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        return s.repo.Muted(ctx, actor, sessionID)
}

// Set replaces the types actor has muted for the session and returns the stored list.
func (s *NotificationPrefs) Set(ctx context.Context, actor, sessionID string, muted []string) ([]string, error) {
        if _, _, _, err := s.sessions.as(ctx, actor, sessionID, ActView); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        return s.repo.SetMuted(ctx, actor, sessionID, muted)
}

// Muted is the event path's read of a user's mutes (stream.Preferences): the fan-out acts for the
// addressee, so it reads that one user's record and nobody else's.
func (s *NotificationPrefs) Muted(ctx context.Context, userID, sessionID string) ([]string, error) {
        return s.repo.Muted(ctx, userID, sessionID)
}

// Package domain holds initiative-api's rules over the owner-scoped data layer: rosters and
// characters, and moving cast between rosters and encounters. All access goes through data's
// owner-scoped repositories, so every call carries the caller's user ID.
package domain

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "maps"
        "slices"
        "strings"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson/primitive"
)

var kinds = data.Kinds

// Rosters implements roster and character rules.
type Rosters struct {
        themes     *data.Owned[data.Theme, *data.Theme]
        rosters    *data.Owned[data.Roster, *data.Roster]
        encounters *data.Encounters
        newID      func() string
}

// NewRosters builds the roster service on db.
func NewRosters(db store.Database) *Rosters {
        return &Rosters{
                themes:     data.NewOwned[data.Theme](db, data.ThemeSpec),
                rosters:    data.NewOwned[data.Roster](db, data.RosterSpec),
                encounters: data.NewEncounters(db),
                newID:      func() string { return primitive.NewObjectID().Hex() },
        }
}

// SetSink installs the sink told of the encounters this service creates from a roster.
func (s *Rosters) SetSink(k data.Sink) { s.encounters.SetSink(k) }

func invalid(format string, a ...any) error {
        return fmt.Errorf("%w: %s", data.ErrInvalid, fmt.Sprintf(format, a...))
}

// normalize trims and validates a character, copying its maps so the stored value does not alias
// the caller's. The ID is left for the caller to assign.
func normalize(c data.Character) (data.Character, error) {
        c.Name = strings.TrimSpace(c.Name)
        if c.Name == "" {
                return c, invalid("character name is required")
        }
        if err := errors.Join(data.CheckText("character name", c.Name, data.MaxNameLen), data.CheckText("character notes", c.Notes, data.MaxNotesLen),
                data.CheckMaps("character", c.Health, c.ExternalRefs)); err != nil {
                return c, err
        }
        if !slices.Contains(kinds, c.Kind) {
                return c, invalid("character kind %q", c.Kind)
        }
        c.Health, c.ExternalRefs = maps.Clone(c.Health), maps.Clone(c.ExternalRefs)
        return c, nil
}

func (s *Rosters) prepare(chars []data.Character) ([]data.Character, error) {
        if len(chars) > data.MaxCharacters {
                return nil, invalid("a roster holds at most %d characters", data.MaxCharacters)
        }
        out := make([]data.Character, 0, len(chars))
        for _, c := range chars {
                n, err := normalize(c)
                if err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                n.ID = s.newID()
                out = append(out, n)
        }
        return out, nil
}

// Create stores a new roster for owner. Character IDs are assigned here; any supplied are ignored.
func (s *Rosters) Create(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, name string, favorite bool, chars []data.Character) (data.Roster, error) {
        name = strings.TrimSpace(name)
        if err := data.CheckName("roster name", name); err != nil {
                return data.Roster{}, err
        }
        prepared, err := s.prepare(chars)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Roster{}, err
        }
        r := data.Roster{Name: name, Favorite: favorite, Characters: prepared}
        if err := s.rosters.Create(ctx, owner, actor, &r); err != nil {
                return data.Roster{}, err
        }
        return r, nil
}

// Load returns owner's roster; a miss is 403 when the roster belongs to another owner and 404
// when there is none, decided after the owner-scoped read has already failed.
func (s *Rosters) Load(ctx context.Context, owner, id string) (data.Roster, error) {
        r, err := s.rosters.Get(ctx, owner, id)
        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) {
                if cerr := s.rosters.Missing(ctx, owner, id); cerr != nil {
                        return data.Roster{}, cerr
                }
        }
        return r, err
}

// Get returns owner's roster.
func (s *Rosters) Get(ctx context.Context, owner, id string) (data.Roster, error) {
        return s.rosters.Get(ctx, owner, id)
}

// List returns a page of owner's rosters.
func (s *Rosters) List(ctx context.Context, owner string, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.Roster], error) {
        return s.rosters.List(ctx, owner, q)
}

// Count returns how many rosters owner holds.
func (s *Rosters) Count(ctx context.Context, owner string) (int64, error) {
        return s.rosters.Count(ctx, owner, nil)
}

// Rename sets the roster's name and favorite flag, compare-and-set on revision.
func (s *Rosters) Rename(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, name string, favorite bool) (data.Roster, error) {
        name = strings.TrimSpace(name)
        if err := data.CheckName("roster name", name); err != nil {
                return data.Roster{}, err
        }
        return s.rosters.Update(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, map[string]any{"name": name, "favorite": favorite})
}

// Delete soft-deletes the roster. Encounters created from it keep their combatants.
func (s *Rosters) Delete(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64) error {
        return s.rosters.SoftDelete(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev)
}

// editCharacters applies edit to a copy of the roster's characters and writes the result
// compare-and-set on rev.
func (s *Rosters) editCharacters(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, edit func([]data.Character) ([]data.Character, error)) (data.Roster, error) {
        cur, err := s.rosters.Get(ctx, owner, id)
        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) {
                // The read is only needed to build the new character list; classify a miss as 403 or 404
                // like the write paths do.
                if cerr := s.rosters.Missing(ctx, owner, id); cerr != nil {
                        return data.Roster{}, cerr
                }
        }
        if err != nil {
                return data.Roster{}, err
        }
        next, err := edit(slices.Clone(cur.Characters))
        if err != nil {
                return data.Roster{}, err
        }
        if len(next) > data.MaxCharacters {
                return data.Roster{}, invalid("a roster holds at most %d characters", data.MaxCharacters)
        }
        return s.rosters.Update(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, map[string]any{"characters": next})
}

func indexOf(chars []data.Character, id string) int {
        return slices.IndexFunc(chars, func(c data.Character) bool { return c.ID == id })
}

// AddCharacter appends c to the roster with a new ID.
func (s *Rosters) AddCharacter(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, c data.Character) (data.Roster, error) {
        n, err := normalize(c)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Roster{}, err
        }
        n.ID = s.newID()
        return s.editCharacters(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(cs []data.Character) ([]data.Character, error) {
                return append(cs, n), nil
        })
}

// UpdateCharacter replaces the roster character with ID charID, keeping its ID.
func (s *Rosters) UpdateCharacter(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, charID string, c data.Character) (data.Roster, error) {
        n, err := normalize(c)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Roster{}, err
        }
        n.ID = charID
        return s.editCharacters(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(cs []data.Character) ([]data.Character, error) {
                i := indexOf(cs, charID)
                if i < 0 {
                        return nil, data.ErrNotFound
                }
                cs[i] = n
                return cs, nil
        })
}

// RemoveCharacter deletes the roster character with ID charID.
func (s *Rosters) RemoveCharacter(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, charID string) (data.Roster, error) {
        return s.editCharacters(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(cs []data.Character) ([]data.Character, error) {
                i := indexOf(cs, charID)
                if i < 0 {
                        return nil, data.ErrNotFound
                }
                return slices.Delete(cs, i, i+1), nil
        })
}

// Items is the roster repository as limit enforcement sees it.
func (s *Rosters) Items() Limited { return s.rosters }

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "log/slog"
        "slices"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/relations"
)

// SessionAuthority is auth-api's session surface (PADR-0040): minting a session ID, ending a
// session, and writing membership tuples. *relations.Client implements it. Every call forwards the
// caller's own bearer (PADR-0037).
type SessionAuthority interface {
        Mint(ctx context.Context, token string) (string, error)
        EndSession(ctx context.Context, token, sessionID string) error
        WriteTuples(ctx context.Context, token, sessionID string, writes, deletes []relations.Tuple) error
}

// ErrSyncPending means a membership change was saved but its OpenFGA tuple could not be written.
// The member is denied until the tuple exists, which is the safe direction; repeating the request
// finishes the write, and the reconciler repairs it within one interval otherwise.
var ErrSyncPending = errors.New("domain: membership saved, tuple sync pending")

type authorityKey struct{}

type authority struct {
        svc   SessionAuthority
        token string
}

// WithAuthority puts auth-api's session surface and the caller's bearer on ctx. Without it every
// session change fails closed.
func WithAuthority(ctx context.Context, svc SessionAuthority, token string) context.Context {
        return context.WithValue(ctx, authorityKey{}, authority{svc, token})
}

func authorityOf(ctx context.Context) (authority, error) {
        a, ok := ctx.Value(authorityKey{}).(authority)
        if !ok || a.svc == nil {
                return authority{}, relations.ErrUnavailable
        }
        return a, nil
}

// memberRoles are the relations the membership endpoint writes; gm is never one.
var memberRoles = []string{data.RoleCoGM, data.RolePlayer, data.RoleObserver}

func tupleOf(user, role string) relations.Tuple {
        return relations.Tuple{User: "user:" + user, Relation: role}
}

// syncGrant writes user's role tuple after the Mongo record exists. It deletes the user's other
// non-gm relations in the same atomic write, so a role change or a retry cannot leave two roles;
// a non-GM actor leaves co_gm tuples alone because only the GM changes those (PADR-0040 section 2).
// A refusal from auth-api is returned as such; an outage is ErrSyncPending. A SetRole retry by a
// co_gm when Mongo already holds the new role cannot know the old relation, so it leaves a stale
// co_gm tuple for the reconciler to remove (accepted: only the GM changes co_gm members).
func (s *Sessions) syncGrant(ctx context.Context, actorRole, sessionID, user, role string) error {
        a, err := authorityOf(ctx)
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("%w: %w", ErrSyncPending, err)
        }
        var deletes []relations.Tuple
        for _, r := range memberRoles {
                if r != role && (r != data.RoleCoGM || actorRole == data.RoleGM) {
                        deletes = append(deletes, tupleOf(user, r))
                }
        }
        err = a.svc.WriteTuples(ctx, a.token, sessionID, []relations.Tuple{tupleOf(user, role)}, deletes)
        switch {
        case err == nil:
                return nil
        case errors.Is(err, relations.ErrUnauthorized), errors.Is(err, relations.ErrDenied),
                errors.Is(err, relations.ErrLimited), errors.Is(err, relations.ErrConflict),
                errors.Is(err, relations.ErrRejected):
                // auth-api answered with a refusal the client should see as such (401, 403, 429, 409
                // session_full). Mongo is already written, so the member is denied until the tuple exists,
                // which is the safe direction; the reconciler repairs it. Only an outage is "pending".
                return err
        }
        slog.WarnContext(ctx, "membership tuple write failed; the reconciler will repair it", "session", sessionID, "user", user, "error", err)
        return fmt.Errorf("%w: %w", ErrSyncPending, err)
}

// syncRevoke deletes user's role tuple after the Mongo record is gone. The change has already taken
// effect (the member lost access when the record went), so a failure is logged and left to the
// reconciler instead of failing the request. The GM deletes all three member relations in one
// request, so a stale co_gm tuple left by a demotion whose write failed cannot outlive the removal;
// anyone else deletes only the member's own role tuple, which is all their auth-api rights cover.
func (s *Sessions) syncRevoke(ctx context.Context, actorRole, sessionID, user, role string) {
        if !slices.Contains(memberRoles, role) {
                return
        }
        deletes := []relations.Tuple{tupleOf(user, role)}
        if actorRole == data.RoleGM {
                deletes = deletes[:0]
                for _, r := range memberRoles {
                        deletes = append(deletes, tupleOf(user, r))
                }
        }
        a, err := authorityOf(ctx)
        if err == nil {
                err = a.svc.WriteTuples(ctx, a.token, sessionID, nil, deletes)
        }
        if err != nil {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "membership tuple delete failed; the reconciler will remove it", "session", sessionID, "user", user, "error", err)
        }
}

// endSession tells auth-api to drop every tuple on the session and close its mint, and returns
// the failure. Deleting a session reports it (a retry ends the session again); rolling back a
// failed create only logs it, since garbage collection closes the mint.
func (s *Sessions) endSession(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) error {
        a, err := authorityOf(ctx)
        if err == nil {
                err = a.svc.EndSession(ctx, a.token, sessionID)
        }
        return err
}

// canManageRole reports whether actorRole may assign or remove role. Only the GM changes a co_gm
// (PADR-0040): a co_gm calling auth-api directly could otherwise strip other co-GMs.
func canManageRole(actorRole, role string) bool {
        return role != data.RoleCoGM || actorRole == data.RoleGM
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/entitlements"
)

// defaultOwnerRecordMaxAge is the default OWNER_ENTITLEMENTS_MAX_AGE, used when
// Sessions.OwnerRecordMaxAge is not set.
const defaultOwnerRecordMaxAge = 7 * 24 * time.Hour

// ownerRecordMaxAge bounds how old the GM's entitlements recorded on a session may be when a
// co-GM acts on them: Sessions.OwnerRecordMaxAge (the configured OWNER_ENTITLEMENTS_MAX_AGE), or
// seven days when unset. Past it, or with nothing recorded, a co-GM's create fails closed until
// the GM next uses the session.
func (s *Sessions) ownerRecordMaxAge() time.Duration {
        if s.OwnerRecordMaxAge > 0 {
                return s.OwnerRecordMaxAge
        }
        return defaultOwnerRecordMaxAge
}

// EncounterGrant is the outcome of authorizing one encounter creation in a session: who may
// create, the owner it belongs to (the GM), the saved-encounters limit, how to see the result, and
// which game systems the owner is entitled to. Only AuthorizeEncounterCreate makes one.
type EncounterGrant struct {
        // Owner is the GM, who owns the encounter whoever creates it.
        Owner     string
        actor     string
        sessionID string
        limit     int
        decision  data.Decision
        viewer    data.Viewer
        systemOK  func(ctx context.Context, systemID string) error
}

// AuthorizeEncounterCreate decides whether actor may create an encounter in the session. The GM
// always may; a co-GM only while the GM has the setting on, read fresh on every call so turning it
// off takes effect at once. The GM's saved-encounters limit applies with private and session
// encounters counted together. The GM is checked live with their own entitlements; a co-GM is
// checked against the GM's entitlements recorded on the session, failing closed when none are
// recorded or the record is older than the configured maximum age. That covers the limit and the
// game-systems flag, so a co-GM can neither use a system the GM lacks nor be refused one the GM has.
func (s *Sessions) AuthorizeEncounterCreate(ctx context.Context, actor, id string) (EncounterGrant, error) {
        sess, m, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActCreateEncounter)
        if err != nil {
                return EncounterGrant{}, err
        }
        if m.Role == data.RoleCoGM && !sess.AllowCoGMCreateEncounter {
                return EncounterGrant{}, data.ErrForbidden
        }
        g := EncounterGrant{Owner: sess.OwnerUserID, actor: actor, sessionID: sess.ID, decision: d}
        if g.limit, g.systemOK, err = s.ownerEntitlements(ctx, sess, m.Role); err != nil {
                return EncounterGrant{}, err
        }
        n, err := s.enc.CountAll(ctx, sess.OwnerUserID)
        if err != nil {
                return EncounterGrant{}, err
        }
        if n >= int64(g.limit) {
                return EncounterGrant{}, &EntitlementError{Key: KeySavedEncounters, Limit: g.limit}
        }
        if g.viewer, err = s.repo.AuthorizeView(ctx, actor, id); err != nil {
                return EncounterGrant{}, err
        }
        return g, nil
}

// ownerEntitlements returns the saved-encounters limit and the game-system gate for a creator.
func (s *Sessions) ownerEntitlements(ctx context.Context, sess data.Session, role string) (int, func(context.Context, string) error, error) {
        if role == data.RoleGM {
                chk, ok := ctx.Value(checkerKey{}).(Checker)
                if !ok {
                        return 0, nil, entitlements.ErrUnavailable
                }
                res, err := chk.Check(ctx, KeySavedEncounters, entitlements.FailClosed)
                return res.Limit, requireSystem, err
        }
        if sess.OwnerLimitsAt == nil || s.now().Sub(*sess.OwnerLimitsAt) > s.ownerRecordMaxAge() {
                return 0, nil, ErrOwnerLimitsUnknown
        }
        limit, err := OwnerLimit(sess, KeySavedEncounters)
        if err != nil {
                return 0, nil, err
        }
        return limit, recordedSystemGate(sess), nil
}

// recordedSystemGate gates game systems on the owner's recorded flag. No System is always allowed.
func recordedSystemGate(sess data.Session) func(context.Context, string) error {
        return func(_ context.Context, systemID string) error {
                if systemID == "" || systemID == data.DefaultSystemID {
                        return nil
                }
                flag, ok := sess.OwnerFlags[KeyGameSystemsExtra]
                if !ok {
                        return ErrOwnerLimitsUnknown
                }
                if !flag {
                        return &EntitlementRequiredError{Key: KeyGameSystemsExtra}
                }
                return nil
        }
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "crypto/rand"
        "crypto/sha256"
        "encoding/base32"
        "encoding/hex"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "log/slog"
        "maps"
        "slices"
        "strings"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/constants"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/entitlements"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/relations"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
)

// Action is something a session member may try to do. Allowed is the one place the role rules
// live (spec initiative-sessions, "Roles").
type Action string

// Session actions.
const (
        ActView            Action = "view"
        ActInvite          Action = "invite"
        ActDecideRequest   Action = "decide_request"
        ActManageMembers   Action = "manage_members"
        ActBlock           Action = "block"
        ActLeave           Action = "leave"
        ActDelete          Action = "delete_session"
        ActUpdate          Action = "update_session"
        ActCreateEncounter Action = "create_encounter"
)

var allowed = map[Action][]string{
        ActView:            {data.RoleGM, data.RoleCoGM, data.RolePlayer, data.RoleObserver},
        ActInvite:          {data.RoleGM, data.RoleCoGM},
        ActDecideRequest:   {data.RoleGM, data.RoleCoGM},
        ActManageMembers:   {data.RoleGM, data.RoleCoGM},
        ActBlock:           {data.RoleGM, data.RoleCoGM},
        ActLeave:           {data.RoleCoGM, data.RolePlayer, data.RoleObserver},
        ActDelete:          {data.RoleGM},
        ActUpdate:          {data.RoleGM},
        ActCreateEncounter: {data.RoleGM, data.RoleCoGM},
}

// Allowed reports whether a member holding role may take action.
func Allowed(role string, a Action) bool { return slices.Contains(allowed[a], role) }

// assignable are the roles a manager can give. There is exactly one gm, so gm is never assigned.
var assignable = []string{data.RoleCoGM, data.RolePlayer, data.RoleObserver}

// Invite lifetimes.
const (
        defaultInviteTTL = 7 * 24 * time.Hour
        minInviteTTL     = time.Hour
        maxInviteTTL     = 30 * 24 * time.Hour
        maxSessionName   = 100
)

// ErrInviteInvalid covers an unknown, expired, revoked, or blocked invite alike, so a code reveals
// nothing about why it failed.
var ErrInviteInvalid = fmt.Errorf("%w: invite is not valid", data.ErrInvalid)

// ErrOwnerLimitsUnknown means the session has no recorded owner entitlements yet and the owner is
// not the caller. It is retryable: the GM's next request records them.
var ErrOwnerLimitsUnknown = errors.New("domain: session owner entitlements not recorded yet")

// ownerLimitsRefreshEvery bounds how often an unchanged record is rewritten.
const ownerLimitsRefreshEvery = time.Minute

// sessionKeys are the owner entitlements recorded on a session.
var sessionKeys = []string{KeyMembersPerSession, KeySavedEncounters}

// sessionFlags are the owner flags recorded on a session beside the limits.
var sessionFlags = []string{KeyGameSystemsExtra}

// OwnerLimit returns the owner's recorded value for key, or ErrOwnerLimitsUnknown if none yet.
func OwnerLimit(sess data.Session, key string) (int, error) {
        v, ok := sess.OwnerLimits[key]
        if !ok {
                return 0, ErrOwnerLimitsUnknown
        }
        return v, nil
}

// SessionView is a session with the caller's role in it.
type SessionView struct {
        data.Session
        Role string `json:"role"`
        // AllowCoGMEncounters is the "allow a co-GM to create an encounter" setting. It is set only for
        // the GM and co-GMs; players and observers never see it.
        AllowCoGMEncounters *bool `json:"allow_co_gm_create_encounter,omitempty"`
}

// viewOf builds the view of sess for a member holding role.
func viewOf(sess data.Session, role string) SessionView {
        v := SessionView{Session: sess, Role: role}
        if role == data.RoleGM || role == data.RoleCoGM {
                v.AllowCoGMEncounters = &sess.AllowCoGMCreateEncounter
        }
        return v
}

// Sessions implements session membership and its rules.
type Sessions struct {
        repo    *data.Sessions
        enc     *data.Encounters
        changes *data.ChangeRequests
        // OwnerRecordMaxAge is how old the GM's recorded entitlements may be when a co-GM acts on
        // them (the configured OWNER_ENTITLEMENTS_MAX_AGE). Zero means seven days.
        OwnerRecordMaxAge time.Duration
        // MaxMembers is the configured maximum of the member limit (SESSION_MEMBERS_MAX): an entitled
        // value above it has no effect. NewSessions sets the default; zero (a Sessions built by hand)
        // fails every member addition closed.
        MaxMembers int
        evictor    Evictor
        now        func() time.Time
}

// NewSessions builds the service on db.
func NewSessions(db store.Database) *Sessions {
        return &Sessions{repo: data.NewSessions(db), enc: data.NewEncounters(db), changes: data.NewChangeRequests(db), MaxMembers: constants.DefaultSessionMembersMax, now: func() time.Time { return time.Now().UTC() }}
}

// Viewer returns how actor may see the session's encounters; see data.Sessions.AuthorizeView.
func (s *Sessions) Viewer(ctx context.Context, actor, sessionID string) (data.Viewer, error) {
        return s.repo.AuthorizeView(ctx, actor, sessionID)
}

// Create makes actor the GM of a new session.
// Sessions beyond the GM's entitled count are refused with *EntitlementError; inactive sessions
// count, so a GM over a reduced limit cannot create.
func (s *Sessions) Create(ctx context.Context, actor, name string) (SessionView, error) {
        return s.CreateWith(ctx, actor, name, false)
}

// CreateWith is Create with the co-GM encounter-creation setting chosen up front.
func (s *Sessions) CreateWith(ctx context.Context, actor, name string, allowCoGMCreate bool) (SessionView, error) {
        name, err := cleanSessionName(name)
        if err != nil {
                return SessionView{}, err
        }
        chk, ok := ctx.Value(checkerKey{}).(Checker)
        if !ok {
                return SessionView{}, entitlements.ErrUnavailable
        }
        res, err := chk.Check(ctx, KeySessionsPerGM, entitlements.FailClosed)
        if err != nil {
                return SessionView{}, err
        }
        owned, err := s.repo.Count(ctx, actor)
