        if err != nil {
                return SessionView{}, err
        }
        if owned >= int64(res.Limit) {
                return SessionView{}, &EntitlementError{Key: KeySessionsPerGM, Limit: res.Limit}
        }
        auth, err := authorityOf(ctx)
        if err != nil {
                return SessionView{}, err
        }
        id, err := auth.svc.Mint(ctx, auth.token)
        if err != nil {
                return SessionView{}, err
        }
        sess := data.Session{Name: name, AllowCoGMCreateEncounter: allowCoGMCreate}
        if err := s.repo.Open(ctx, actor, id, &sess); err != nil {
                // Frees the mint slot; garbage collection covers a failure here.
                if eerr := s.endSession(ctx, id); eerr != nil {
                        slog.WarnContext(ctx, "ending a session whose creation failed did not work; garbage collection will close it", "session", id, "error", eerr)
                }
                return SessionView{}, err
        }
        return viewOf(sess, data.RoleGM), nil
}

func cleanSessionName(name string) (string, error) {
        name = strings.TrimSpace(name)
        if name == "" || len([]rune(name)) > maxSessionName {
                return "", invalid("session name is required and at most %d characters", maxSessionName)
        }
        return name, nil
}

// SessionChanges are the settings the GM may change on a session; nil leaves one as it is.
type SessionChanges struct {
        Name                *string
        AllowCoGMEncounters *bool
}

// Update changes the session's settings at rev. Only the GM may: the relation asked of OpenFGA is
// gm and the write is scoped to the session's owner, so a co-GM cannot change the setting that
// grants co-GMs a power.
func (s *Sessions) Update(ctx context.Context, actor, id string, rev int64, ch SessionChanges) (SessionView, error) {
        _, m, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActUpdate)
        if err != nil {
                return SessionView{}, err
        }
        set := map[string]any{}
        if ch.Name != nil {
                name, err := cleanSessionName(*ch.Name)
                if err != nil {
                        return SessionView{}, err
                }
                set["name"] = name
        }
        if ch.AllowCoGMEncounters != nil {
                set["allow_co_gm_create_encounter"] = *ch.AllowCoGMEncounters
        }
        if len(set) == 0 {
                return SessionView{}, invalid("nothing to update")
        }
        sess, err := s.repo.Update(ctx, d, rev, set)
        if err != nil {
                return SessionView{}, err
        }
        return viewOf(sess, m.Role), nil
}

// actionRelation is the OpenFGA relation on the session each action needs. The role rules in
// Allowed still apply on top: OpenFGA says whether the caller may touch the session at all.
var actionRelation = map[Action]string{
        ActView: "can_view", ActLeave: "can_view",
        ActInvite: "can_edit", ActBlock: "can_edit", ActManageMembers: "can_edit",
        ActDecideRequest:   "can_approve",
        ActDelete:          "gm",
        ActUpdate:          "gm",
        ActCreateEncounter: "can_edit",
}

// as authorizes actor for action a on session id with auth-api (OpenFGA), then returns the
// session, actor's membership, and the decision the data layer needs. A denial is ErrForbidden and
// reads nothing; an unavailable decision is relations.ErrUnavailable (503), never an allow.
func (s *Sessions) as(ctx context.Context, actor, id string, a Action) (data.Session, data.Member, data.Decision, error) {
        d, err := data.Authorize(ctx, actor, id, actionRelation[a])
        if err != nil {
                return data.Session{}, data.Member{}, d, err
        }
        sess, err := s.repo.Live(ctx, d)
        if err != nil {
                return sess, data.Member{}, d, err
        }
        m, err := s.repo.Member(ctx, d, actor)
        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) || (err == nil && !Allowed(m.Role, a)) {
                return data.Session{}, data.Member{}, d, data.ErrForbidden
        }
        if err != nil {
                return data.Session{}, data.Member{}, d, err
        }
        if m.Role == data.RoleGM {
                sess = s.refreshOwnerLimits(ctx, d, sess)
        }
        if sess.InactiveAt != nil && a != ActView && a != ActDelete && a != ActLeave {
                return sess, m, d, data.ErrInactive
        }
        return sess, m, d, nil
}

// refreshOwnerLimits re-reads the GM's entitlements with the GM's own token (the checker in ctx)
// and records them on the session. A failed, degraded, or stale (cached past its TTL) read keeps the old record, is logged, and
// never fails the request.
func (s *Sessions) refreshOwnerLimits(ctx context.Context, d data.Decision, sess data.Session) data.Session {
        chk, ok := ctx.Value(checkerKey{}).(Checker)
        if !ok {
                return sess
        }
        limits, flags := make(map[string]int, len(sessionKeys)), make(map[string]bool, len(sessionFlags))
        for _, key := range append(slices.Clone(sessionKeys), sessionFlags...) {
                res, err := chk.Check(ctx, key, entitlements.FailOpen)
                if err != nil || res.Degraded || res.Stale {
                        slog.WarnContext(ctx, "session owner entitlements not refreshed", "session", sess.ID, "key", key, "error", err)
                        return sess
                }
                if slices.Contains(sessionFlags, key) {
                        flags[key] = res.Flag
                } else {
                        limits[key] = res.Limit
                }
        }
        fresh := sess.OwnerLimitsAt != nil && s.now().Sub(*sess.OwnerLimitsAt) < ownerLimitsRefreshEvery
        if fresh && maps.Equal(limits, sess.OwnerLimits) && maps.Equal(flags, sess.OwnerFlags) {
                return sess
        }
        if err := s.repo.RecordOwnerLimits(ctx, d, limits, flags); err != nil {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "session owner entitlements not recorded", "session", sess.ID, "error", err)
                return sess
        }
        now := s.now()
        sess.OwnerLimits, sess.OwnerFlags, sess.OwnerLimitsAt = limits, flags, &now
        return sess
}

// Items exposes the sessions as limited items, so the limit reconciler can deactivate those beyond
// the owner's entitled count.
func (s *Sessions) Items() Limited { return s.repo.Limits() }

// Get returns the session with actor's role. Only members may read it.
func (s *Sessions) Get(ctx context.Context, actor, id string) (SessionView, error) {
        sess, m, _, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActView)
        return viewOf(sess, m.Role), err
}

// List returns the sessions actor belongs to.
func (s *Sessions) List(ctx context.Context, actor string, q data.Query) (data.Page[SessionView], error) {
        page, err := s.repo.SessionIDsOf(ctx, actor, q)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Page[SessionView]{}, err
        }
        out := data.Page[SessionView]{NextCursor: page.NextCursor, Items: []SessionView{}}
        for _, m := range page.Items {
                d, err := data.Authorize(ctx, actor, m.SessionID, actionRelation[ActView])
                if errors.Is(err, data.ErrForbidden) || errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) {
                        continue // membership record the tuples don't back (yet), or malformed
                }
                if err != nil {
                        return data.Page[SessionView]{}, err
                }
                sess, err := s.repo.Live(ctx, d)
                if errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) {
                        continue // deleted while we listed
                }
                if err != nil {
                        return data.Page[SessionView]{}, err
                }
                out.Items = append(out.Items, viewOf(sess, m.Role))
        }
        return out, nil
}

// Delete soft-deletes the session, ends it at auth-api (every tuple and the mint), ends every
// membership, and turns the session's encounters into private encounters of the GM
// (initiative-sessions, "Session deletion"): session_id and controllers are cleared and the GM
// becomes the owner, including of encounters a co-GM authored. Only the GM may; the owner-scoped
// delete enforces it a second time. The soft delete is the commit point. Every later step is
// idempotent and is attempted even if an earlier one fails, and the errors are returned together;
// calling Delete again (the GM, same id) then finishes what is left, without the revision, because
// a deleted session can no longer be authorized through OpenFGA. On such a retry auth-api may
// already have dropped the GM's own tuple, so a denial when ending the session counts as ended.
func (s *Sessions) Delete(ctx context.Context, actor, id string, rev int64) error {
        _, _, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActDelete)
        resumed := false
        switch {
        case err == nil:
                if err := s.repo.SoftDelete(ctx, d, rev); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
        case errors.Is(err, data.ErrForbidden) || errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound):
                // Perhaps a deletion this GM started and did not finish. Anyone else, and a session that
                // was never deleted, gets the original answer.
                rd, rerr := s.repo.ResumeDeletion(ctx, actor, id)
                if rerr != nil {
                        return err
                }
                d, resumed = rd, true
        default:
                return err
        }
        // End the session at auth-api before dropping the member records: once the session record is
        // soft-deleted a retry cannot authorize the call through OpenFGA, so it runs first.
        endErr := s.endSession(ctx, d.SessionID())
        if resumed && errors.Is(endErr, relations.ErrDenied) {
                endErr = nil
        }
        err = errors.Join(
                endErr,
                s.repo.DropSession(ctx, d),
                s.changes.CancelSession(ctx, d),
                s.enc.ReleaseSession(ctx, d),
        )
        // After the release, so the table hears each encounter leave the session before its streams end.
        s.evict(d.SessionID(), "")
        return err
}

func hashCode(code string) string {
        sum := sha256.Sum256([]byte(strings.ToUpper(strings.TrimSpace(code))))
        return hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])
}

func newCode() (string, error) {
        b := make([]byte, 10) // 80 bits
        if _, err := rand.Read(b); err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        return base32.StdEncoding.WithPadding(base32.NoPadding).EncodeToString(b), nil
}

// CreateInvite makes a join code for the session. The code is returned once; only its hash is kept.
// ttl 0 means seven days.
func (s *Sessions) CreateInvite(ctx context.Context, actor, id string, ttl time.Duration) (data.Invite, string, error) {
        _, _, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActInvite)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Invite{}, "", err
        }
        if ttl == 0 {
                ttl = defaultInviteTTL
        }
        if ttl < minInviteTTL || ttl > maxInviteTTL {
                return data.Invite{}, "", invalid("invite lifetime must be between %s and %s", minInviteTTL, maxInviteTTL)
        }
        code, err := newCode()
        if err != nil {
                return data.Invite{}, "", err
        }
        in, err := s.repo.AddInvite(ctx, d, hashCode(code), s.now().Add(ttl))
        return in, code, err
}

// Invites lists the session's invites.
func (s *Sessions) Invites(ctx context.Context, actor, id string, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.Invite], error) {
        _, _, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActInvite)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Page[data.Invite]{}, err
        }
        return s.repo.Invites(ctx, d, q)
}

// RevokeInvite ends an invite at once.
func (s *Sessions) RevokeInvite(ctx context.Context, actor, id, inviteID string) (data.Invite, error) {
        _, _, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActInvite)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Invite{}, err
        }
        return s.repo.RevokeInvite(ctx, d, inviteID)
}

// Join turns a presented code into a pending request. A pending request grants nothing. A code
// that is unknown, expired, revoked, or from a blocked user fails the same way and creates nothing.
func (s *Sessions) Join(ctx context.Context, actor, code string) (data.JoinRequest, error) {
        t, err := s.repo.ResolveInvite(ctx, actor, hashCode(code), s.now())
        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) {
                return data.JoinRequest{}, ErrInviteInvalid
        }
        if err != nil {
                return data.JoinRequest{}, err
        }
        st, err := s.repo.ForJoin(ctx, t)
        if err != nil {
                return data.JoinRequest{}, err
        }
        if !st.Exists || !st.Active || st.Blocked {
                return data.JoinRequest{}, ErrInviteInvalid
        }
        if st.Member {
                return data.JoinRequest{}, data.ErrConflict
        }
        return s.repo.RequestJoin(ctx, t)
}

// Requests lists the session's join requests (pending ones when pendingOnly).
func (s *Sessions) Requests(ctx context.Context, actor, id string, pendingOnly bool, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.JoinRequest], error) {
        _, _, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActDecideRequest)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Page[data.JoinRequest]{}, err
        }
        status := ""
        if pendingOnly {
                status = data.RequestPending
        }
        return s.repo.Requests(ctx, d, status, q)
}

// Approve makes the requester a member with role. The request settles first, so two approvals
// cannot both succeed; a block placed after the request still stops it.
func (s *Sessions) Approve(ctx context.Context, actor, id, requestID, role string) (data.Member, error) {
        if !slices.Contains(assignable, role) {
                return data.Member{}, invalid("role must be one of %s", strings.Join(assignable, ", "))
        }
        sess, am, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActDecideRequest)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Member{}, err
        }
        if !canManageRole(am.Role, role) {
                return data.Member{}, data.ErrForbidden
        }
        // Before the request settles, and failing closed: a stale controller id must not revive on
        // admission. The user is not a member yet, so this cannot race a legitimate assignment, and a
        // failure leaves the request pending to retry rather than approved with no member.
        // Only a request that is not pending (a retried approval, or no such request) skips the scrub;
        // any other lookup error fails closed rather than admit with stale ids intact.
        switch pending, perr := s.repo.PendingUser(ctx, d, requestID); {
        case perr == nil:
                // A request still pending would add a member: the roster must have room, so a full one
                // leaves the request pending for later. A retried approval (not pending) is not an add.
                if err := s.checkRoomForMember(ctx, d, sess, am.Role); err != nil {
                        return data.Member{}, err
                }
                if err := s.enc.ScrubController(ctx, d, pending); err != nil {
                        return data.Member{}, err
                }
        case !errors.Is(perr, data.ErrNotFound):
                return data.Member{}, perr
        }
        jr, err := s.repo.Decide(ctx, d, requestID, data.RequestApproved, role, "")
        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrConflict) && jr.Status == data.RequestApproved && jr.Role == role {
                return s.finishApproval(ctx, d, am.Role, jr)
        }
        if err != nil {
                return data.Member{}, err
        }
        if blocked, err := s.repo.Blocked(ctx, d, jr.UserID); err != nil || blocked {
                if err == nil {
                        err = data.ErrForbidden
                }
                return data.Member{}, err
        }
        m, err := s.repo.AddMember(ctx, d, jr.UserID, role)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Member{}, err
        }
        return m, s.syncGrant(ctx, am.Role, d.SessionID(), m.UserID, role)
}

// errMaxMembersUnset means Sessions.MaxMembers was never configured; additions fail closed.
var errMaxMembersUnset = errors.New("domain: the session member maximum is not configured")

// memberLimit is the session's member limit: the owner's recorded members_per_session value,
// clamped to the configured maximum. It fails closed with ErrOwnerLimitsUnknown when nothing is
// recorded, or (for a co-GM, who cannot read the GM's entitlements) when the record is older than
// the configured maximum age. The GM's own request refreshed the record in as(), but a record the
// GM's own path reads may be arbitrarily old when the entitlements service is down.
func (s *Sessions) memberLimit(sess data.Session, actorRole string) (int, error) {
        if actorRole != data.RoleGM && (sess.OwnerLimitsAt == nil || s.now().Sub(*sess.OwnerLimitsAt) > s.ownerRecordMaxAge()) {
                return 0, ErrOwnerLimitsUnknown
        }
        limit, err := OwnerLimit(sess, KeyMembersPerSession)
        if err != nil {
                return 0, err
        }
        if s.MaxMembers < 1 {
                return 0, errMaxMembersUnset
        }
        return max(0, min(limit, s.MaxMembers)), nil
}

// checkRoomForMember rejects adding a member to a roster that already holds the limit of non-GM
// members, with the typed entitlement error carrying the key and the (clamped) limit. Nothing is
// removed when the limit later falls; this only stops additions. The count and the add are two
// steps, so concurrent approvals can overrun a full roster. Co-GMs are uncapped and one actor can
// approve several pending requests in parallel, so the overrun is bounded by the number of
// requests pending at that moment.
// ponytail: no post-add recount; add one that soft-deletes the member and resets the request to
// pending if overruns matter.
func (s *Sessions) checkRoomForMember(ctx context.Context, d data.Decision, sess data.Session, actorRole string) error {
        limit, err := s.memberLimit(sess, actorRole)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        n, err := s.repo.CountMembers(ctx, d)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if n >= int64(limit) {
                return &EntitlementError{Key: KeyMembersPerSession, Limit: limit}
        }
        return nil
}

// finishApproval is the retry of an approval that settled but whose tuple write failed: the member
// record exists, so only the tuple is written again. Any other state is the original conflict.
func (s *Sessions) finishApproval(ctx context.Context, d data.Decision, actorRole string, jr data.JoinRequest) (data.Member, error) {
        m, err := s.repo.Member(ctx, d, jr.UserID)
        if err != nil || m.Role != jr.Role {
                return data.Member{}, data.ErrConflict
        }
        return m, s.syncGrant(ctx, actorRole, d.SessionID(), m.UserID, m.Role)
}

// Reject settles a pending request with a reason; the requester stays out.
func (s *Sessions) Reject(ctx context.Context, actor, id, requestID, reason string) (data.JoinRequest, error) {
        reason = strings.TrimSpace(reason)
        if reason == "" {
                return data.JoinRequest{}, invalid("a reason is required to reject a request")
        }
        _, _, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActDecideRequest)
        if err != nil {
                return data.JoinRequest{}, err
        }
        return s.repo.Decide(ctx, d, requestID, data.RequestRejected, "", reason)
}

// Members lists the session's members (user id, role). Any member may.
func (s *Sessions) Members(ctx context.Context, actor, id string, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.Member], error) {
        _, _, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActView)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Page[data.Member]{}, err
        }
        return s.repo.Members(ctx, d, q)
}

// target loads the member a manager acts on. Nobody can act on the GM.
func (s *Sessions) target(ctx context.Context, d data.Decision, user string) (data.Member, error) {
        m, err := s.repo.Member(ctx, d, user)
        if err != nil {
                return m, err
        }
        if m.Role == data.RoleGM {
                return m, data.ErrForbidden
        }
        return m, nil
}

// SetRole changes a member's role. The gm role cannot be given or taken.
func (s *Sessions) SetRole(ctx context.Context, actor, id, user, role string) (data.Member, error) {
        if !slices.Contains(assignable, role) {
                return data.Member{}, invalid("role must be one of %s", strings.Join(assignable, ", "))
        }
        _, am, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActManageMembers)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Member{}, err
        }
        t, err := s.target(ctx, d, user)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Member{}, err
        }
        if !canManageRole(am.Role, role) || !canManageRole(am.Role, t.Role) {
                return data.Member{}, data.ErrForbidden
        }
        if t.Role == data.RoleObserver {
                // An observer being promoted starts with no control; failing closed, so a stale id cannot revive.
                if err := s.enc.ScrubController(ctx, d, user); err != nil {
                        return data.Member{}, err
                }
        }
        m, err := s.repo.SetRole(ctx, d, user, role)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Member{}, err
        }
        // Close the streams opened under the old role first: a demoted co-GM must not hear another event
        // that carries a hidden combatant's id, including the scrub's own.
        s.evict(d.SessionID(), user)
        if role == data.RoleObserver {
                s.scrubControllers(ctx, d, user)
        }
        return m, s.syncGrant(ctx, am.Role, d.SessionID(), user, role)
}

// Remove ends a member's access.
func (s *Sessions) Remove(ctx context.Context, actor, id, user string) error {
        _, am, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActManageMembers)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        t, err := s.target(ctx, d, user)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if !canManageRole(am.Role, t.Role) {
                return data.ErrForbidden
        }
        if err := s.repo.RemoveMember(ctx, d, user); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        s.evict(d.SessionID(), user)
        s.scrubControllers(ctx, d, user)
        s.syncRevoke(ctx, am.Role, d.SessionID(), user, t.Role)
        return nil
}

// Leave removes actor from the session. The GM cannot leave; they delete the session.
func (s *Sessions) Leave(ctx context.Context, actor, id string) error {
        _, m, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActView)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if !Allowed(m.Role, ActLeave) {
                return invalid("the GM cannot leave; delete the session instead")
        }
        if err := s.repo.RemoveMember(ctx, d, actor); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        s.evict(d.SessionID(), actor)
        s.scrubControllers(ctx, d, actor)
        s.syncRevoke(ctx, m.Role, d.SessionID(), actor, m.Role)
        return nil
}

// Block keeps user out of the session, removing them and any pending request.
func (s *Sessions) Block(ctx context.Context, actor, id, user string) error {
        _, am, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActBlock)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if user == actor {
                return invalid("you cannot block yourself")
        }
        t, err := s.target(ctx, d, user)
        if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) {
                return err
        }
        if err == nil && !canManageRole(am.Role, t.Role) {
                return data.ErrForbidden
        }
        if err := s.repo.AddBlock(ctx, d, user); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        // The block alone ends access, so streams close now, not only if the removal below succeeds.
        s.evict(d.SessionID(), user)
        if err := s.repo.DropRequest(ctx, d, user); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        switch err := s.repo.RemoveMember(ctx, d, user); {
        case err == nil:
                s.scrubControllers(ctx, d, user)
                s.syncRevoke(ctx, am.Role, d.SessionID(), user, t.Role)
        case !errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound):
                return err
        }
        return nil
}

// Unblock lets user join again.
func (s *Sessions) Unblock(ctx context.Context, actor, id, user string) error {
        _, _, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActBlock)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        return s.repo.RemoveBlock(ctx, d, user)
}

// Blocks lists the blocked users.
func (s *Sessions) Blocks(ctx context.Context, actor, id string, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.Block], error) {
        _, _, d, err := s.as(ctx, actor, id, ActBlock)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Page[data.Block]{}, err
        }
        return s.repo.Blocks(ctx, d, q)
}

// Evictor disconnects open streams. *stream.Hub implements it.
type Evictor interface {
        // Evict closes userID's streams in the session, or everyone's when userID is empty.
        Evict(sessionID, userID string)
}

// SetEvictor installs the evictor told when a member loses or changes access (removed, left,
// blocked, role changed) or the session is deleted, so their open streams end at once.
func (s *Sessions) SetEvictor(e Evictor) { s.evictor = e }

func (s *Sessions) evict(sessionID, userID string) {
        if s.evictor != nil {
                s.evictor.Evict(sessionID, userID)
        }
}

// SetSink installs the sink told of the encounter changes sessions make: controller scrubs and the
// release of a deleted session's encounters.
func (s *Sessions) SetSink(k data.Sink) { s.enc.SetSink(k) }

// scrubControllers removes user from every combatant's controllers in the session's encounters,
// so a removed, blocked, or demoted member cannot regain control by rejoining or being promoted.
// It runs after the membership write and again before admission; it is idempotent and best
// effort, because the live Viewer already denies a non-player any control (domain.controls).
func (s *Sessions) scrubControllers(ctx context.Context, d data.Decision, user string) {
        if err := s.enc.ScrubController(ctx, d, user); err != nil {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "scrubbing a member's combatant controls failed; admission scrubs again", "session", d.SessionID(), "user", user, "error", err)
        }
        // Their pending change requests can no longer be approved (approval needs a current player
        // controller), so close them instead of leaving them in the GM's queue.
        if err := s.changes.CancelPending(ctx, d, user); err != nil {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "cancelling a member's pending change requests failed", "session", d.SessionID(), "user", user, "error", err)
        }
}

// IsMember reports whether userID currently belongs to the live session (stream.Members).
func (s *Sessions) IsMember(ctx context.Context, userID, sessionID string) (bool, error) {
        return s.repo.IsMember(ctx, userID, sessionID)
}

// ManagerIDs lists the GM and co-GM user ids of a session, for addressing change-request notices
// (stream.Managers).
func (s *Sessions) ManagerIDs(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) ([]string, error) {
        return s.repo.ManagerIDs(ctx, sessionID)
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        cryptorand "crypto/rand"
        "crypto/sha256"
        "encoding/base64"
        "encoding/hex"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/constants"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
)

// ErrTicketInvalid means a stream ticket is unknown, expired, or already spent. They are one
// answer, so a probe learns nothing about which.
var ErrTicketInvalid = errors.New("stream ticket is not valid")

// DefaultTicketTTL is how long a minted ticket may be redeemed.
const DefaultTicketTTL = constants.DefaultStreamTicketTTL

// Streams issues and redeems the one-time tickets that open an event stream for a browser.
type Streams struct {
        tickets  *data.StreamTickets
        sessions *Sessions
        enc      *Encounters
        // TTL is how long a ticket lives; DefaultTicketTTL when zero.
        TTL time.Duration
}

// NewStreams builds the stream service on db.
func NewStreams(db store.Database, sessions *Sessions, enc *Encounters) *Streams {
        return &Streams{tickets: data.NewStreamTickets(db), sessions: sessions, enc: enc}
}

// SetClock replaces the clock ticket ages are read from (tests).
func (s *Streams) SetClock(now func() time.Time) { s.tickets.SetClock(now) }

func (s *Streams) ttl() time.Duration {
        if s.TTL > 0 {
                return s.TTL
        }
        return DefaultTicketTTL
}

// TicketRequest names what a ticket is for: a session's encounters (SessionID, and optionally one
// of them), or the caller's own private encounters (no SessionID, optionally one of them).
type TicketRequest struct {
        SessionID   string
        EncounterID string
        ClientID    string
        // UserLevel asks for the user-level stream; it excludes SessionID and EncounterID.
        UserLevel bool
}

// Claim is what a redeemed ticket asks for. It is a request, not proof: View decides.
type Claim struct {
        UserID, SessionID, EncounterID, ClientID string
        Level                                    data.ViewLevel
        UserLevel                                bool
}

// Mint authorizes actor for the scope the way the REST reads do (a non-member is data.ErrNotMember)
// and returns a ticket to open it with, valid for the service's TTL. The caller's own bearer is
// what authorizes (PADR-0037).
func (s *Streams) Mint(ctx context.Context, actor string, req TicketRequest) (string, time.Duration, error) {
        t := data.StreamTicket{UserID: actor, SessionID: req.SessionID, EncounterID: req.EncounterID}
        if data.ValidClientID(req.ClientID) {
                t.ClientID = req.ClientID
        }
        if req.UserLevel {
                if req.SessionID != "" || req.EncounterID != "" {
                        return "", 0, invalid("a user-level stream takes no session or encounter")
                }
                t.UserLevel = true
        } else if req.SessionID != "" {
                v, err := s.sessions.Viewer(ctx, actor, req.SessionID)
                if err != nil {
                        return "", 0, err
                }
                if req.EncounterID != "" {
                        if _, err := s.enc.repo.LoadForViewer(ctx, v, req.EncounterID); err != nil {
                                return "", 0, err
                        }
                }
                t.Level = v.Level()
                t.SessionID = v.SessionID()
        } else {
                v, err := s.ownerView(ctx, actor, req.EncounterID)
                if err != nil {
                        return "", 0, err
                }
                t.Level = v.Level()
        }
        raw := make([]byte, 32)
        if _, err := cryptorand.Read(raw); err != nil {
                return "", 0, err
        }
        ticket := base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(raw)
        t.ID = hashTicket(ticket)
        if err := s.tickets.Mint(ctx, t); err != nil {
                return "", 0, err
        }
        return ticket, s.ttl(), nil
}

func hashTicket(ticket string) string {
        sum := sha256.Sum256([]byte(ticket))
        return hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])
}

// ownerView is the viewer for the owner's private scope; a named encounter must be theirs and
// private (one in a session is data.ErrForbidden, as on the REST path).
func (s *Streams) ownerView(ctx context.Context, owner, encounterID string) (data.Viewer, error) {
        if encounterID == "" {
                return data.OwnerViewer(owner)
        }
        _, v, err := s.enc.repo.LoadOwnedView(ctx, owner, encounterID)
        return v, err
}

// BearerView is the viewer for a caller authenticated by bearer: the REST authorization (OpenFGA
// through the caller's own token, fail closed) for a session scope, ownership for a private one.
func (s *Streams) BearerView(ctx context.Context, actor, sessionID, encounterID string) (data.Viewer, error) {
        if sessionID == "" {
                return s.ownerView(ctx, actor, encounterID)
        }
        v, err := s.sessions.Viewer(ctx, actor, sessionID)
        if err == nil && encounterID != "" {
                _, err = s.enc.repo.LoadForViewer(ctx, v, encounterID)
        }
        return v, err
}

// ErrRecheckFailed means a stream's membership could not be read; the stream's owner counts these
// and closes it after three in a row.
var ErrRecheckFailed = errors.New("stream membership re-check failed")

// ErrAccessChanged means a stream's viewer no longer matches the member's live record.
var ErrAccessChanged = errors.New("stream access changed")

// Recheck re-reads the live member record behind an open stream. It returns ErrAccessChanged when
// the member is gone, the session is deleted, or their level is no longer the one the stream was
// granted at (it must reconnect to be authorized again). A failed read returns ErrRecheckFailed; the
// stream's owner closes it after three in a row.
func (s *Streams) Recheck(ctx context.Context, v data.Viewer) error {
        if v.SessionID() == "" {
                return nil
        }
        now, err := s.sessions.repo.TicketView(ctx, v.Actor(), v.SessionID(), v.Level())
        switch {
        case errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotMember):
                return ErrAccessChanged
        case err != nil:
                return fmt.Errorf("%w: %v", ErrRecheckFailed, err)
        case now.Level() != v.Level():
                return ErrAccessChanged
        }
        return nil
}

// Peek reads what a ticket stands for without spending it; the caller checks its limits and then
// calls Redeem, so a refused connect does not cost the client its ticket.
func (s *Streams) Peek(ctx context.Context, ticket string) (Claim, error) {
        return s.claim(ctx, ticket, s.tickets.Peek)
}

// Redeem spends a ticket. It is the only thing that can be done with it: a second redeem, an
// expired ticket and an unknown one are all ErrTicketInvalid.
func (s *Streams) Redeem(ctx context.Context, ticket string) (Claim, error) {
        return s.claim(ctx, ticket, s.tickets.Redeem)
}

func (s *Streams) claim(ctx context.Context, ticket string, take func(context.Context, string, time.Duration) (data.StreamTicket, error)) (Claim, error) {
        if len(ticket) == 0 || len(ticket) > 128 {
                return Claim{}, ErrTicketInvalid
        }
        t, err := take(ctx, hashTicket(ticket), s.ttl())
        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) {
                return Claim{}, ErrTicketInvalid
        }
        if err != nil {
                return Claim{}, err
        }
        return Claim{UserID: t.UserID, SessionID: t.SessionID, EncounterID: t.EncounterID, ClientID: t.ClientID, Level: t.Level, UserLevel: t.UserLevel}, nil
}

// View is the viewer a redeemed claim opens. Only the user, the scope and the minted level come
// from the ticket; access is read live (data.Sessions.TicketView), so a member removed since the
// mint, a session deleted since, or a role raised since gets nothing.
func (s *Streams) View(ctx context.Context, c Claim) (data.Viewer, error) {
        if c.UserLevel {
                return data.Viewer{}, nil // identity only: the stream's deliveries check membership themselves
        }
        if c.SessionID == "" {
                return s.ownerView(ctx, c.UserID, c.EncounterID)
        }
        v, err := s.sessions.repo.TicketView(ctx, c.UserID, c.SessionID, c.Level)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Viewer{}, err
        }
        if c.EncounterID != "" {
                if _, err := s.enc.repo.LoadForViewer(ctx, v, c.EncounterID); err != nil {
                        return data.Viewer{}, err
                }
        }
        return v, nil
}

// Head is the cheapest thing a client can compare with its cache to learn whether it is current:
// the revision for a GM or co-GM, the view tag for everyone else. Nothing else about the encounter.
type Head struct {
        EncounterID string `json:"encounter_id"`
        Revision    int64  `json:"revision,omitempty"`
        ViewTag     string `json:"view_tag,omitempty"`
}

// MaxHeads is the most heads a stream opens with; more is reported as truncated in hello, and the
// client lists the rest over REST.
const MaxHeads = 100

// Heads returns the head of each encounter in v's scope (or of encounterID only), built from the
// same views the REST reads return.
func (s *Streams) Heads(ctx context.Context, v data.Viewer, encounterID string) ([]Head, bool, error) {
        var views []EncounterView
        var err error
        switch {
        case v.SessionID() != "" && encounterID != "":
                var one EncounterView
                if one, err = s.enc.ViewForSession(ctx, v, encounterID); err == nil {
                        views = []EncounterView{one}
                }
        case v.SessionID() != "":
                views, err = s.enc.ViewsForSession(ctx, v, MaxHeads+1)
        case encounterID != "":
                var one EncounterView
                if one, err = s.enc.ViewOwned(ctx, v.Actor(), encounterID); err == nil {
                        views = []EncounterView{one}
                }
        default:
                var page data.Page[EncounterView]
                if page, err = s.enc.ViewsOwned(ctx, v.Actor(), data.Query{Limit: MaxHeads + 1}); err == nil {
                        views = page.Items
                }
        }
        if err != nil {
                return nil, false, err
        }
        truncated := len(views) > MaxHeads
        if truncated {
                views = views[:MaxHeads]
        }
        out := make([]Head, 0, len(views))
        for _, view := range views {
                h := Head{}
                h.EncounterID, _ = view["id"].(string)
                h.Revision, _ = view["revision"].(int64)
                h.ViewTag, _ = view[FieldViewTag].(string)
                out = append(out, h)
        }
        return out, truncated, nil
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        _ "embed"
        "encoding/json"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "net/url"
        "regexp"
        "strings"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/entitlements"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
)

// KeyThemeSlots is the entitlement limit on custom theme slots (docs/entitlements-check-contract.md).
const KeyThemeSlots = "initiative.theme_slots"

// PackagedPrefix starts the ID of every packaged theme. Custom theme IDs never carry it.
const PackagedPrefix = "pkg-"

const slotRetries = 3

var (
        // ErrReadOnly means the caller tried to change or delete a packaged theme.
        ErrReadOnly = errors.New("domain: packaged theme is read-only")

        hexColor = regexp.MustCompile(`^#[0-9A-Fa-f]{6}([0-9A-Fa-f]{2})?$`)
)

//go:embed packaged_themes.json
var packagedJSON []byte

// EntitlementError is the typed denial for a stored-item limit the caller has reached.
type EntitlementError struct {
        Key   string
        Limit int
}

func (e *EntitlementError) Error() string {
        return fmt.Sprintf("domain: entitlement limit reached for %s (%d)", e.Key, e.Limit)
}

// Checker answers entitlement checks for the calling user; the server binds the user and token.
type Checker interface {
        Check(ctx context.Context, key string, policy entitlements.Policy) (entitlements.Result, error)
}

// ThemeInput is the full field set of a custom theme.
type ThemeInput struct {
        Name, Style, MainColor, Accent1Color, Accent2Color, Background1Color, Background2Color string
        PrimaryFontName, SecondaryFontName, BackgroundImage                                    string
}

// ThemePatch changes the fields that are non-nil.
type ThemePatch struct {
        Name, Style, MainColor, Accent1Color, Accent2Color, Background1Color, Background2Color *string
        PrimaryFontName, SecondaryFontName, BackgroundImage                                    *string
}

// Themes implements custom theme rules: slot enforcement and read-only packaged themes.
type Themes struct {
        themes   *data.Owned[data.Theme, *data.Theme]
        packaged []data.Theme
}

// NewThemes builds the theme service on db.
func NewThemes(db store.Database) (*Themes, error) {
        var pk []data.Theme
        if err := json.Unmarshal(packagedJSON, &pk); err != nil {
                return nil, fmt.Errorf("packaged themes: %w", err)
        }
        return &Themes{themes: data.NewOwned[data.Theme](db, data.ThemeSpec), packaged: pk}, nil
}

// Packaged returns the read-only packaged themes.
func (s *Themes) Packaged() []data.Theme { return append([]data.Theme(nil), s.packaged...) }

func isPackaged(id string) bool { return strings.HasPrefix(id, PackagedPrefix) }

// Get returns a packaged theme by ID or owner's custom theme.
func (s *Themes) Get(ctx context.Context, owner, id string) (data.Theme, error) {
        if isPackaged(id) {
                for _, t := range s.packaged {
                        if t.ID == id {
                                return t, nil
                        }
                }
                return data.Theme{}, data.ErrNotFound
        }
        return s.themes.Get(ctx, owner, id)
}

// List returns a page of owner's custom themes in slot order.
func (s *Themes) List(ctx context.Context, owner string, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.Theme], error) {
        if len(q.Sort) == 0 {
                q.Sort = []data.SortKey{{Field: "slot"}}
        }
        return s.themes.List(ctx, owner, q)
}

// Create stores a custom theme in the lowest free slot below the entitled limit. At or over the
// limit it returns *EntitlementError; if entitlements-api is unreachable, entitlements.ErrUnavailable.
func (s *Themes) Create(ctx context.Context, owner, actor string, in ThemeInput, chk Checker) (data.Theme, error) {
        t, err := buildTheme(in)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Theme{}, err
        }
        res, err := chk.Check(ctx, KeyThemeSlots, entitlements.FailClosed)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Theme{}, err
        }
        // Inactive themes count toward the total owned, so a user over a reduced limit cannot create.
        owned, err := s.themes.Count(ctx, owner, nil)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Theme{}, err
        }
        if owned >= int64(res.Limit) {
                return data.Theme{}, &EntitlementError{Key: KeyThemeSlots, Limit: res.Limit}
        }
        for range slotRetries {
                slot, err := s.freeSlot(ctx, owner, res.Limit)
                if err != nil {
                        return data.Theme{}, err
                }
                t.Slot = &slot
                err = s.themes.Create(ctx, owner, actor, &t)
                if !errors.Is(err, data.ErrConflict) {
                        return t, err
                }
        }
        return data.Theme{}, data.ErrConflict
}

// freeSlot returns the lowest unused slot in [0, limit), or *EntitlementError when all are used.
func (s *Themes) freeSlot(ctx context.Context, owner string, limit int) (int, error) {
        page, err := s.themes.List(ctx, owner, data.Query{
                Sort: []data.SortKey{{Field: "slot"}}, Fields: []string{"slot"}, Limit: limit,
        })
        if err != nil {
                return 0, err
        }
        used := map[int]bool{}
        for _, t := range page.Items {
                if t.Slot != nil {
                        used[*t.Slot] = true
                }
        }
        for i := range limit {
                if !used[i] {
                        return i, nil
                }
        }
        return 0, &EntitlementError{Key: KeyThemeSlots, Limit: limit}
}

// Update changes owner's custom theme, compare-and-set on rev. Packaged themes return ErrReadOnly.
func (s *Themes) Update(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64, p ThemePatch) (data.Theme, error) {
        if isPackaged(id) {
                return data.Theme{}, ErrReadOnly
        }
        set, err := patchSet(p)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Theme{}, err
        }
        return s.themes.Update(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, set)
}

// Delete soft-deletes owner's custom theme, freeing its slot. Packaged themes return ErrReadOnly.
func (s *Themes) Delete(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64) error {
        if isPackaged(id) {
                return ErrReadOnly
        }
        return s.themes.SoftDelete(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev)
}

// Items is the theme repository as limit enforcement sees it.
func (s *Themes) Items() Limited { return s.themes }

// Count returns how many custom themes owner holds.
func (s *Themes) Count(ctx context.Context, owner string) (int64, error) {
        return s.themes.Count(ctx, owner, nil)
}

func buildTheme(in ThemeInput) (data.Theme, error) {
        set, err := patchSet(ThemePatch{
                Name: &in.Name, Style: &in.Style, MainColor: &in.MainColor, Accent1Color: &in.Accent1Color,
                Accent2Color: &in.Accent2Color, Background1Color: &in.Background1Color,
                Background2Color: &in.Background2Color, PrimaryFontName: &in.PrimaryFontName,
                SecondaryFontName: &in.SecondaryFontName, BackgroundImage: &in.BackgroundImage,
        })
        if err != nil {
                return data.Theme{}, err
        }
        str := func(k string) string { v, _ := set[k].(string); return v }
        return data.Theme{
                Name: str("name"), Style: str("style"), MainColor: str("main_color"),
                Accent1Color: str("accent1_color"), Accent2Color: str("accent2_color"),
                Background1Color: str("background1_color"), Background2Color: str("background2_color"),
                PrimaryFontName: str("primary_font_name"), SecondaryFontName: str("secondary_font_name"),
                BackgroundImage: str("background_image"),
        }, nil
}

type themeField struct {
        key  string
        val  *string
        kind int // 0 required text, 1 color, 2 optional https URL
}

const (
        fText = iota
        fColor
        fImage
)

// patchSet validates the non-nil fields of p and returns them keyed by stored field name.
func patchSet(p ThemePatch) (map[string]any, error) {
        fields := []themeField{
                {"name", p.Name, fText}, {"style", p.Style, fText},
                {"main_color", p.MainColor, fColor}, {"accent1_color", p.Accent1Color, fColor},
                {"accent2_color", p.Accent2Color, fColor}, {"background1_color", p.Background1Color, fColor},
                {"background2_color", p.Background2Color, fColor},
                {"primary_font_name", p.PrimaryFontName, fText}, {"secondary_font_name", p.SecondaryFontName, fText},
                {"background_image", p.BackgroundImage, fImage},
        }
        set := map[string]any{}
        for _, f := range fields {
                if f.val == nil {
                        continue
                }
                v := strings.TrimSpace(*f.val)
                if err := checkThemeField(f, v); err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                set[f.key] = v
        }
        return set, nil
}

func checkThemeField(f themeField, v string) error {
        switch f.kind {
        case fText:
                if v == "" || len(v) > 100 {
                        return invalid("theme %s is required and at most 100 characters", f.key)
                }
        case fColor:
                if !hexColor.MatchString(v) {
                        return invalid("theme %s must be #RRGGBB or #RRGGBBAA", f.key)
                }
        case fImage:
                if v == "" {
                        return nil
                }
                if u, err := url.Parse(v); err != nil || u.Scheme != "https" || u.Host == "" || len(v) > 2048 {
                        return invalid("theme %s must be an https URL", f.key)
                }
        }
        return nil
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "maps"
        "strings"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// CreateEncounterFromRoster stores enc as owner's new encounter with one combatant per roster
// character, copying name, kind, notes, default health, and external refs. The source roster is
// not modified, and enc's own combatants are replaced.
func (s *Rosters) CreateEncounterFromRoster(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, rosterID string, enc data.Encounter) (data.Encounter, error) {
        enc.Name = strings.TrimSpace(enc.Name)
        if enc.Name == "" {
                return data.Encounter{}, invalid("encounter name is required")
        }
        if err := checkTheme(ctx, s.themes, owner, enc.ThemeID); err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        if err := data.CheckName("encounter name", enc.Name); err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        // An encounter joins a session only through a path that authorizes the caller as that session's GM.
        if enc.SessionID != "" {
                return data.Encounter{}, invalid("an encounter cannot be created inside a session here")
        }
        if err := s.encounters.RequireSystem(ctx, enc.GameSystemID); err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        if err := requireSystem(ctx, enc.GameSystemID); err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        roster, err := s.Load(ctx, owner, rosterID)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        enc.Combatants = make([]data.Combatant, 0, len(roster.Characters))
        for _, c := range roster.Characters {
                enc.Combatants = append(enc.Combatants, data.Combatant{
                        ID: randomID(), Name: c.Name, Kind: c.Kind, Notes: c.Notes,
                        Health: maps.Clone(c.Health), ExternalRefs: maps.Clone(c.ExternalRefs),
                        Conditions: []data.Condition{}, SourceCharacterID: c.ID,
                })
        }
        if err := s.encounters.Create(ctx, owner, actor, &enc); err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        return enc, nil
}

// SaveAsRoster stores a new roster holding one character per combatant of owner's encounter that
// is not removed. Current health becomes the character's defaults. The encounter is not modified.
// An empty name falls back to the encounter's name.
func (s *Rosters) SaveAsRoster(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, encounterID, name string) (data.Roster, error) {
        enc, err := s.encounters.Load(ctx, owner, encounterID)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Roster{}, err
        }
        if strings.TrimSpace(name) == "" {
                name = enc.Name
        }
        chars := make([]data.Character, 0, len(enc.Combatants))
        for _, c := range enc.Combatants {
                if c.Removed {
                        continue
                }
                chars = append(chars, data.Character{
                        Name: c.Name, Kind: c.Kind, Notes: c.Notes, Health: c.Health, ExternalRefs: c.ExternalRefs,
                })
        }
        return s.Create(ctx, owner, actor, name, false, chars)
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "maps"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// Start moves a draft encounter to active at round 1, builds the turn queue, and makes the first
// combatant current (its conditions tick as its turn begins).
func (s *Encounters) Start(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64) (data.Encounter, error) {
        ctx = data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventStarted)
        return s.editQueue(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(st *data.EncounterState, _ data.Encounter) error {
                if st.Status != data.StatusDraft {
                        return fmt.Errorf("%w: cannot start from %s", ErrTransition, st.Status)
                }
                st.Status, st.Round = data.StatusActive, 1
                return nil
        })
}

// End moves an active encounter to ended.
func (s *Encounters) End(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64) (data.Encounter, error) {
        ctx = data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventEnded)
        return s.edit(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(st *data.EncounterState, _ data.Encounter) error {
                if st.Status != data.StatusActive {
                        return fmt.Errorf("%w: cannot end from %s", ErrTransition, st.Status)
                }
                st.Status = data.StatusEnded
                return nil
        })
}

// Reset returns the encounter to draft at round 0 with no current combatant and an empty turn queue, clears every
// combatant's conditions, and sets health values back to the game system's defaults. Combatants,
// their initiative, and removed marks are kept.
func (s *Encounters) Reset(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64) (data.Encounter, error) {
        ctx = data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventReset)
        cur, err := s.repo.Load(ctx, owner, id)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        sys, err := s.systems.GetOrDefault(ctx, cur.GameSystemID)
        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) {
                return data.Encounter{}, invalid("the encounter's game system has no definition")
        }
        if err != nil {
                return data.Encounter{}, err
        }
        defaults := make(map[string]int, len(sys.Fields))
        for _, f := range sys.Fields {
                defaults[f.Key] = f.Def
        }
        return s.edit(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(st *data.EncounterState, _ data.Encounter) error {
                st.Status, st.Round, st.CurrentCombatantID, st.TurnQueue = data.StatusDraft, 0, "", nil
                for i := range st.Combatants {
                        c := &st.Combatants[i]
                        c.Conditions = []data.Condition{}
                        c.Health = maps.Clone(c.Health)
                        for k := range c.Health {
                                if d, ok := defaults[k]; ok {
                                        c.Health[k] = d
                                }
                        }
                }
                return nil
        })
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "fmt"
        "slices"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// effectiveOrdering is the ordering the engine applies. No System orders by initiative value only,
// whatever the stored ordering says: pcVsAdversary grouping needs a system that defines it, and
// none of the current definitions do.
func effectiveOrdering(ordering, gameSystemID string) string {
        if ordering == data.OrderPCVsAdversary && gameSystemID == data.DefaultSystemID {
                return data.OrderHighToLow
        }
        return ordering
}

// recompute rebuilds the turn queue under ordering and tie. In an active encounter it also keeps
// a current combatant: if the current one left the queue, the combatant now at its old position
// takes the turn (wrapping to the front and starting the next round if it was last), and an empty
// current picks the first.
func (s *Encounters) recompute(st *data.EncounterState, ordering, tie string) {
        prev := st.TurnQueue
        st.TurnQueue = buildQueue(ordering, tie, st.Combatants, prev, s.perm)
        if st.Status != data.StatusActive || slices.Contains(st.TurnQueue, st.CurrentCombatantID) {
                return
        }
        if len(st.TurnQueue) == 0 {
                st.CurrentCombatantID = ""
                return
        }
        old := slices.Index(prev, st.CurrentCombatantID)
        pos := max(old, 0)
        if old >= len(st.TurnQueue) {
                st.Round++ // the removed combatant was last: the turn wraps to the front, a new round
        }
        st.CurrentCombatantID = st.TurnQueue[pos%len(st.TurnQueue)]
        beginTurn(st)
}

// beginTurn ticks the conditions of the current combatant.
func beginTurn(st *data.EncounterState) {
        i := slices.IndexFunc(st.Combatants, func(c data.Combatant) bool { return c.ID == st.CurrentCombatantID })
        if i >= 0 {
                expireConditions(&st.Combatants[i])
        }
}

func queuedTurn(st *data.EncounterState) (pos int, err error) {
        if st.Status != data.StatusActive {
                return 0, fmt.Errorf("%w: encounter is %s", ErrTransition, st.Status)
        }
        if len(st.TurnQueue) == 0 {
                return 0, invalid("the turn queue is empty")
        }
        return slices.Index(st.TurnQueue, st.CurrentCombatantID), nil
}

// Advance passes the turn to the next combatant in the queue. Past the last entry it wraps to the
// first and starts the next round. The new current combatant's timed conditions lose one round,
// and expire at zero.
func (s *Encounters) Advance(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64) (data.Encounter, error) {
        ctx = data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventTurn)
        return s.edit(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(st *data.EncounterState, _ data.Encounter) error {
                pos, err := queuedTurn(st)
                if err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                idx, wrapped := nextTurn(pos, len(st.TurnQueue))
                if wrapped {
                        st.Round++
                }
                st.CurrentCombatantID = st.TurnQueue[idx]
                beginTurn(st)
                return nil
        })
}

// StepBack returns the turn to the previous combatant. Stepping back past the first entry goes to
// the last and the previous round; it is rejected on the first turn of round 1. Conditions that
// already ticked are not restored.
func (s *Encounters) StepBack(ctx context.Context, owner, actor, id string, rev int64) (data.Encounter, error) {
        ctx = data.WithOp(ctx, data.EventTurn)
        return s.edit(ctx, owner, actor, id, rev, func(st *data.EncounterState, _ data.Encounter) error {
                pos, err := queuedTurn(st)
                if err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                idx, wrapped := prevTurn(pos, len(st.TurnQueue))
                if wrapped {
                        if st.Round <= 1 {
                                return fmt.Errorf("%w: already at the first turn", ErrTransition)
                        }
                        st.Round--
                }
                st.CurrentCombatantID = st.TurnQueue[idx]
                return nil
        })
}

package domain

import (
        "context"
        "crypto/sha256"
        "encoding/hex"
        "encoding/json"
        "errors"
        "log/slog"
        "maps"
        "reflect"
        "slices"
        "strings"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// EncounterView is an encounter as one caller may see it. It is produced only by BuildView, the
// single place that decides visibility (initiative-api-resources, "Role-aware responses"); REST
// handlers and, later, event payloads serialize nothing else. A field reaches a view only when
// the rule tables below name it and the viewer's level allows it, so a field added to a data type
// is invisible to everyone but the GM-level views until someone decides otherwise (and the test
// in view_test.go fails until they do).
type EncounterView map[string]any

// encounterRules maps each data.Encounter JSON field to the least level that sees it. Fields
// computed per viewer (see computedEncounter) are listed here too, but their raw value is never used.
var encounterRules = map[string]data.ViewLevel{
        "id": data.ViewObserver, "name": data.ViewObserver, "game_system_id": data.ViewObserver,
        "status": data.ViewObserver, "round": data.ViewObserver, "session_id": data.ViewObserver,
        "current_combatant_id": data.ViewObserver, "turn_queue": data.ViewObserver, "combatants": data.ViewObserver,

        "owner_user_id": data.ViewGM, "revision": data.ViewGM, "ordering": data.ViewGM, "tie_breaker": data.ViewGM,
        "labels": data.ViewGM, "colorblind": data.ViewGM, "theme_id": data.ViewGM, "position": data.ViewGM,
        "inactive_at": data.ViewGM, "locked_at": data.ViewGM,
        "created_at": data.ViewGM, "created_by": data.ViewGM, "updated_at": data.ViewGM, "updated_by": data.ViewGM,
        "deleted_at": data.ViewGM, "deleted_by": data.ViewGM,
}

// An encounter-level notes field, when one is added, needs a GM-only rule here; the field test in
// view_test.go fails until it has one.

// computedEncounter fields are filled from the viewer's visible combatants, never copied.
var computedEncounter = map[string]bool{"current_combatant_id": true, "turn_queue": true, "combatants": true}

// combatantRules is the same table for data.Combatant.
var combatantRules = map[string]data.ViewLevel{
        "id": data.ViewObserver, "name": data.ViewObserver, "kind": data.ViewObserver, "conditions": data.ViewObserver,
        "health": data.ViewObserver, "notes": data.ViewObserver,

        "initiative": data.ViewGM, "removed": data.ViewGM, "source_character_id": data.ViewGM, "external_refs": data.ViewGM,
        "controller_user_ids": data.ViewGM, "hidden": data.ViewGM, "health_visibility": data.ViewGM, "notes_shared": data.ViewGM,
}

// computedCombatant fields depend on the viewer and the combatant's visibility settings.
var computedCombatant = map[string]bool{"health": true, "notes": true, "conditions": true}

// conditionRules is the same table for data.Condition.
var conditionRules = map[string]data.ViewLevel{"name": data.ViewObserver, "rounds_remaining": data.ViewObserver}

// Derived view fields that no data type carries.
const (
        fieldHealthStatus   = "health_status"
        fieldMine           = "mine"
        fieldCombatantCount = "combatant_count"
        fieldViewTag        = "view_tag"
)

// FieldViewTag is the key of a non-GM view's digest.
const FieldViewTag = fieldViewTag

// Health statuses a non-GM sees when a combatant's health is shown as status.
const (
        StatusHealthy   = "healthy"
        StatusHurt      = "hurt"
        StatusNearDeath = "near_death"
        StatusDown      = "down"
)

// ErrNoView is returned when a view is asked of a viewer that grants nothing, or for an
// encounter outside the viewer's scope.
var ErrNoView = data.ErrForbidden

// BuildView renders enc for v. def is the encounter's game system tracker definition, used for
// the coarse health status. The viewer must come from the data layer (data.AuthorizeView or an
// owner-scoped load); BuildView does not look at membership.
func BuildView(enc data.Encounter, def []data.TrackerField, v data.Viewer) (EncounterView, error) {
        if v.Level() == data.ViewNone || enc.SessionID != v.SessionID() {
                return nil, ErrNoView
        }
        gm := v.Level() == data.ViewGM
        combatants, queue, current, count := visibleCombatants(enc, def, v)

        out := projectStruct(reflect.ValueOf(enc), encounterRules, v.Level(), func(name string) (any, bool) {
                switch name {
                case "combatants":
                        return combatants, true
                case "turn_queue":
                        return queue, true
                case "current_combatant_id":
                        return current, current != ""
                }
                return nil, false
        }, computedEncounter)
        out[fieldCombatantCount] = count
        if !gm {
                // The stored revision moves on every edit, hidden ones included, so a non-GM gets a tag of
                // what it can see instead: it changes only when its own view does.
                out[fieldViewTag] = viewTag(out)
        }
        return out, nil
}

// Combatant returns the combatant with id from the view, if the view has it. A combatant the
// viewer may not see is not in the view, so it is simply not found.
func (v EncounterView) Combatant(id string) (map[string]any, bool) {
        list, _ := v["combatants"].([]map[string]any)
        for _, c := range list {
                if c["id"] == id {
                        return c, true
                }
        }
        return nil, false
}

// visibleCombatants returns the combatant views, turn queue, current combatant ID and count the
// viewer may see. For a non-GM, hidden and removed combatants are absent, not redacted, and the
// queue and current turn are derived from what remains.
func visibleCombatants(enc data.Encounter, def []data.TrackerField, v data.Viewer) (list []map[string]any, queue []string, current string, count int) {
        gm := v.Level() == data.ViewGM
        shown := map[string]bool{}
        list = []map[string]any{}
        for _, c := range enc.Combatants {
                if !c.Removed {
                        count++
                }
                // A hidden combatant stays visible to the player who controls it; nobody else sees it.
                if !gm && (c.Removed || (c.Hidden && !controls(c, v))) {
                        continue
                }
                shown[c.ID] = true
                list = append(list, combatantView(c, def, v))
        }
        queue = []string{}
        for _, id := range enc.TurnQueue {
                if shown[id] {
                        queue = append(queue, id)
                }
        }
        if shown[enc.CurrentCombatantID] {
                current = enc.CurrentCombatantID
        }
        if !gm {
                // The count a non-GM sees is the count it can see.
                count = len(list)
        }
        return list, queue, current, count
}

// controls reports whether the viewer is a player who controls c. An observer controls nothing,
// even if listed.
func controls(c data.Combatant, v data.Viewer) bool {
        return v.Level() == data.ViewPlayer && slices.Contains(c.ControllerUserIDs, v.Actor())
}

func combatantView(c data.Combatant, def []data.TrackerField, v data.Viewer) map[string]any {
        gm := v.Level() == data.ViewGM
        own := controls(c, v)
        out := projectStruct(reflect.ValueOf(c), combatantRules, v.Level(), func(name string) (any, bool) {
                switch name {
                case "health":
                        if (gm || own || c.HealthVisibility == data.HealthFull) && len(c.Health) > 0 {
                                return maps.Clone(c.Health), true
                        }
                case "notes":
                        if gm || c.NotesShared {
                                return c.Notes, true
                        }
                case "conditions":
                        conds := make([]map[string]any, 0, len(c.Conditions))
                        for _, cond := range c.Conditions {
                                conds = append(conds, projectStruct(reflect.ValueOf(cond), conditionRules, v.Level(), nil, nil))
                        }
                        return conds, true
                }
                return nil, false
        }, computedCombatant)
        if !gm && !own && c.HealthVisibility == data.HealthStatus {
                if s, ok := healthStatus(def, c.Health); ok {
                        out[fieldHealthStatus] = s
                }
        }
        if v.Level() == data.ViewPlayer {
                out[fieldMine] = own
        }
        return out
}

// healthStatus is the coarse state of a combatant: from the first bounded counter in the
// definition (current over its maximum), else the first counter (down at its minimum, otherwise
// healthy). It reports false when the definition has no counter, so nothing is shown.
func healthStatus(def []data.TrackerField, health map[string]int) (string, bool) {
        var first *data.TrackerField
        for i, f := range def {
                if f.Kind != data.FieldCounter {
                        continue
                }
                if first == nil {
                        first = &def[i]
                }
                if f.Max == nil && f.MaxRef == "" {
                        continue
                }
                bound, ok := boundOf(def, f, health)
                if !ok {
                        return "", false // a bound that cannot be resolved shows no status rather than a guessed one
                }
                cur, ok := valueOf(def, health, f.Key)
                if !ok {
                        return "", false
                }
                return statusOf(cur, minOf(f), bound), true
        }
        if first == nil {
                return "", false
        }
        cur, ok := valueOf(def, health, first.Key)
        if !ok {
                return "", false
        }
        if cur <= minOf(*first) {
                return StatusDown, true
        }
        return StatusHealthy, true
}

// valueOf is the stored value of a health key, or the definition's default when none is stored. A
// key the definition does not know and the combatant does not hold has no value, so no status is
// shown rather than a guessed one.
func valueOf(def []data.TrackerField, health map[string]int, key string) (int, bool) {
        if v, ok := health[key]; ok {
                return v, true
        }
        for _, f := range def {
                if f.Key == key {
                        return f.Def, true
                }
        }
        return 0, false
}

func boundOf(def []data.TrackerField, f data.TrackerField, health map[string]int) (int, bool) {
        switch {
        case f.Max != nil:
                return *f.Max, true
        case f.MaxRef != "":
                return valueOf(def, health, f.MaxRef)
        }
        return 0, false
}

func minOf(f data.TrackerField) int {
        if f.Min != nil {
                return *f.Min
        }
        return 0
}

func statusOf(cur, min, bound int) string {
        switch {
        case cur <= min || bound <= min:
                return StatusDown
        case cur >= bound:
                return StatusHealthy
        case float64(cur-min) <= 0.25*float64(bound-min):
                return StatusNearDeath
        default:
                return StatusHurt
        }
}

// projectStruct copies the fields of the struct v that rules allow at level, keyed by JSON name,
// honoring omitempty. A field with no rule is skipped (default deny). A computed field takes its
// value from compute, or is skipped when compute declines; its raw value is never read.
func projectStruct(v reflect.Value, rules map[string]data.ViewLevel, level data.ViewLevel, compute func(string) (any, bool), computed map[string]bool) map[string]any {
        out := map[string]any{}
        t := v.Type()
        for i := 0; i < t.NumField(); i++ {
                f := t.Field(i)
                if f.Anonymous && f.Type.Kind() == reflect.Struct {
                        maps.Copy(out, projectStruct(v.Field(i), rules, level, compute, computed))
                        continue
                }
                name, omitEmpty, ok := jsonName(f)
                if !ok {
                        continue
                }
                min, ruled := rules[name]
                if !ruled || min > level {
                        continue
                }
                if computed[name] {
                        if val, ok := compute(name); ok {
                                out[name] = val
                        }
                        continue
                }
                if omitEmpty && v.Field(i).IsZero() {
                        continue
                }
                out[name] = v.Field(i).Interface()
        }
        return out
}

// jsonName returns the JSON key of f, whether it is omitempty, and false for unexported or "-" fields.
func jsonName(f reflect.StructField) (name string, omitEmpty, ok bool) {
        if !f.IsExported() {
                return "", false, false
        }
        tag := f.Tag.Get("json")
        if tag == "-" {
                return "", false, false
        }
        parts := strings.Split(tag, ",")
        name = parts[0]
        if name == "" {
                name = f.Name
        }
        return name, slices.Contains(parts[1:], "omitempty"), true
}

// viewTag is a short digest of a non-GM view, for ETags and change detection.
func viewTag(view map[string]any) string {
        b, err := json.Marshal(view) // map keys marshal sorted, so equal views give equal tags
        if err != nil {
                return ""
        }
        sum := sha256.Sum256(b)
        return hex.EncodeToString(sum[:8])
}

// ViewOwned renders the owner's private encounter. An encounter that belongs to a session is
// reached through ViewForSession.
func (s *Encounters) ViewOwned(ctx context.Context, owner, id string) (EncounterView, error) {
        enc, v, err := s.repo.LoadOwnedView(ctx, owner, id)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        return s.render(ctx, enc, v)
}

// ViewForSession renders one of the viewer's session's encounters. A missing encounter and one in
// another session are the same error.
func (s *Encounters) ViewForSession(ctx context.Context, v data.Viewer, id string) (EncounterView, error) {
        enc, err := s.repo.LoadForViewer(ctx, v, id)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        return s.render(ctx, enc, v)
}

// ViewsForSession renders the viewer's session's encounters in display order.
func (s *Encounters) ViewsForSession(ctx context.Context, v data.Viewer, limit int64) ([]EncounterView, error) {
        encs, err := s.repo.ListForViewer(ctx, v, limit)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        out := make([]EncounterView, 0, len(encs))
        for _, enc := range encs {
                view, err := s.render(ctx, enc, v)
                if err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                out = append(out, view)
        }
        return out, nil
}

// render looks up the encounter's tracker definition and builds the view. A system with no
// definition leaves the status out; it never widens what is shown.
func (s *Encounters) render(ctx context.Context, enc data.Encounter, v data.Viewer) (EncounterView, error) {
        return BuildView(enc, s.Definition(ctx, enc), v)
}

// Definition returns the tracker fields of enc's game system, or none when they cannot be had. The
// only thing the definition adds to a view is the coarse health status, so a lookup failure must
// not turn a write that already committed into a 500: it is logged and the status is left out.
func (s *Encounters) Definition(ctx context.Context, enc data.Encounter) []data.TrackerField {
        sys, err := s.systems.GetOrDefault(ctx, enc.GameSystemID)
        switch {
        case err == nil:
                return sys.Fields
        case !errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound):
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "game system lookup failed while building a view; omitting health status", "system", enc.GameSystemID, "error", err)
        }
        return nil
}

// ViewsOwned renders one page of the owner's private encounters.
func (s *Encounters) ViewsOwned(ctx context.Context, owner string, q data.Query) (data.Page[EncounterView], error) {
        page, v, err := s.repo.ListOwnedViews(ctx, owner, q)
        if err != nil {
                return data.Page[EncounterView]{}, err
        }
        out := data.Page[EncounterView]{NextCursor: page.NextCursor, Items: make([]EncounterView, 0, len(page.Items))}
        for _, enc := range page.Items {
                view, err := s.render(ctx, enc, v)
                if err != nil {
                        return data.Page[EncounterView]{}, err
                }
                out.Items = append(out.Items, view)
        }
        return out, nil
}

// ViewWritten renders an encounter the owner has just written, so a write's response is built by
// the same function as a read. It refuses an encounter that is not the owner's private one.
func (s *Encounters) ViewWritten(ctx context.Context, owner string, enc data.Encounter) (EncounterView, error) {
        v, err := s.repo.OwnedViewer(owner, enc)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        return s.render(ctx, enc, v)
}

// Package entitlements is the client for entitlements-api's check routes
// (docs/entitlements-check-contract.md in the platform repo, PADR-0034).
//
// The caller's own bearer token is forwarded on every call (PADR-0037, no service tokens), so a
// check only answers for the user holding that token. Results are cached per (user, key) for the
// TTL the service returns. When the service cannot be reached, FailClosed callers (creates and
// expansions) get ErrUnavailable and FailOpen callers (reads and edits) get the last known value
// (at most maxStale past its TTL) or a Degraded result they must not gate on.
package entitlements

import (
        "context"
        "encoding/json"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "io"
        "log/slog"
        "net/http"
        "net/url"
        "sync"
        "time"
)

const (
        // maxTTL bounds a service-supplied TTL so a bad value cannot pin a stale entitlement.
        maxTTL = 5 * time.Minute
        // maxEntries bounds the cache; past it, expired entries go first, then everything.
        maxEntries = 10000
        // maxStale bounds how long FailOpen may serve an expired value during an outage; past it the
        // result is Degraded and the caller skips gating.
        maxStale    = time.Hour
        callTimeout = 3 * time.Second
        maxBody     = 64 << 10
)

// Policy says what a check does when entitlements-api cannot be reached.
type Policy int

const (
        // FailClosed returns ErrUnavailable. Use it for creates and expansions.
        FailClosed Policy = iota
        // FailOpen returns the last known value, or a Degraded result. Use it for reads and edits.
        FailOpen
)

var (
        // ErrUnavailable means entitlements-api could not be reached or answered with a server error.
        ErrUnavailable = errors.New("entitlements: service unavailable")
        // ErrUnauthorized means the service rejected the user's token; the caller returns 401.
        ErrUnauthorized = errors.New("entitlements: token rejected")
        // ErrUnknownKey means the key is not in the catalog: a programming error, never defaulted.
        ErrUnknownKey = errors.New("entitlements: key not registered")
)

// Result is one checked key. Flag is set for type "flag", Limit for type "limit".
type Result struct {
        Key   string
        Type  string
        Flag  bool
        Limit int
        // Degraded is true when no value is known because the service was down under FailOpen.
