        // Flag and Limit are meaningless then; the caller skips gating.
        Degraded bool
        // Stale is true when the value is an expired cached one served because the service was down.
        // It is usable for gating, but a caller that records the value must not treat it as a fresh read.
        Stale bool
}

type entry struct {
        res     Result
        expires time.Time
}

// Client calls entitlements-api. The zero value is not usable; use New.
type Client struct {
        base string
        http *http.Client
        now  func() time.Time

        mu    sync.Mutex
        cache map[string]entry
}

// New returns a Client for the entitlements-api base URL.
func New(baseURL string) *Client {
        return &Client{
                base:  baseURL,
                http:  &http.Client{Timeout: callTimeout},
                now:   time.Now,
                cache: map[string]entry{},
        }
}

// Check returns the user's value for key. user is the verified token subject (the cache key);
// token is that user's bearer token, forwarded to the service.
func (c *Client) Check(ctx context.Context, user, token, key string, policy Policy) (Result, error) {
        ck := user + "\x00" + key
        if res, ok := c.fresh(ck); ok {
                return res, nil
        }

        res, ttl, err := c.fetch(ctx, token, key)
        switch {
        case err == nil:
                c.store(ck, res, ttl)
                return res, nil
        case errors.Is(err, ErrUnavailable) && policy == FailOpen:
                if res, ok := c.stale(ck); ok {
                        res.Stale = true
                        return res, nil
                }
                return Result{Key: key, Degraded: true}, nil
        default:
                if errors.Is(err, ErrUnknownKey) {
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "entitlement key not registered", "key", key)
                }
                return Result{}, err
        }
}

func (c *Client) fetch(ctx context.Context, token, key string) (Result, time.Duration, error) {
        var body checkBody
        if err := c.get(ctx, token, "/check?key="+url.QueryEscape(key), &body); err != nil {
                return Result{}, 0, err
        }
        return body.result(key)
}

// Prefix returns the user's values for every key under prefix in one call and caches each. It
// has no stale fallback: any failure is returned, for callers that only display the values.
func (c *Client) Prefix(ctx context.Context, user, token, prefix string) ([]Result, error) {
        var body struct {
                Results []checkBody `json:"results"`
        }
        if err := c.get(ctx, token, "/check/batch?prefix="+url.QueryEscape(prefix), &body); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        out := make([]Result, 0, len(body.Results))
        for _, b := range body.Results {
                res, ttl, err := b.result(b.Key)
                if err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                c.store(user+"\x00"+b.Key, res, ttl)
                out = append(out, res)
        }
        return out, nil
}

// get calls path with the user's token and decodes a 200 body into out.
func (c *Client) get(ctx context.Context, token, path string, out any) error {
        req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, c.base+path, nil)
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("entitlements: build request: %w", err)
        }
        req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+token)
        req.Header.Set("Accept", "application/json")

        resp, err := c.http.Do(req)
        if err != nil {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "entitlements call failed", "path", path, "error", err)
                return ErrUnavailable
        }
        defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()

        switch resp.StatusCode {
        case http.StatusOK:
                if err := json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, maxBody)).Decode(out); err != nil {
                        return ErrUnavailable
                }
                return nil
        case http.StatusUnauthorized:
                return ErrUnauthorized
        case http.StatusNotFound:
                return ErrUnknownKey
        default:
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "entitlements call rejected", "path", path, "status", resp.StatusCode)
                return ErrUnavailable
        }
}

type checkBody struct {
        Key   string `json:"key"`
        Type  string `json:"type"`
        Value any    `json:"value"`
        TTL   int    `json:"ttl_seconds"`
}

func (b checkBody) result(key string) (Result, time.Duration, error) {
        res := Result{Key: key, Type: b.Type}
        switch v := b.Value.(type) {
        case bool:
                res.Flag = v
        case float64:
                if v < 0 || v != float64(int(v)) {
                        return Result{}, 0, ErrUnavailable
                }
                res.Limit = int(v)
        default:
                return Result{}, 0, ErrUnavailable
        }
        return res, min(time.Duration(b.TTL)*time.Second, maxTTL), nil
}

func (c *Client) fresh(ck string) (Result, bool) {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        e, ok := c.cache[ck]
        if !ok || !c.now().Before(e.expires) {
                return Result{}, false
        }
        return e.res, true
}

func (c *Client) stale(ck string) (Result, bool) {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        e, ok := c.cache[ck]
        return e.res, ok && c.now().Before(e.expires.Add(maxStale))
}

func (c *Client) store(ck string, res Result, ttl time.Duration) {
        if ttl <= 0 {
                return
        }
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        if len(c.cache) >= maxEntries {
                now := c.now()
                for k, e := range c.cache {
                        if !now.Before(e.expires) {
                                delete(c.cache, k)
                        }
                }
                if len(c.cache) >= maxEntries {
                        c.cache = map[string]entry{}
                }
        }
        c.cache[ck] = entry{res: res, expires: c.now().Add(ttl)}
}

// Package events consumes auth-api's change events from the platform bus (PADR-0039): a deny or
// a role removal ends the subject's integration credentials. The event carries no token and the
// consumer holds no service credential (PADR-0037).
package events

import (
        "context"
        "encoding/json"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "log/slog"
        "os"
        "sync"
        "sync/atomic"
        "time"

        "github.com/nats-io/nats.go"
        "github.com/nats-io/nats.go/jetstream"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

const (
        // StreamName is auth-api's JetStream stream, subject auth.events.>.
        StreamName     = "AUTH_EVENTS"
        FilterSubject  = "auth.events.>"
        defaultDurable = "initiative-api-credential-revocation"
        // Change kinds the event's action carries.
        kindDeny        = "deny"
        kindRoleRemoved = "role_removed"
)

// event is the platform envelope; entity_id is the Auth0 subject and action the change kind.
// Unknown fields are ignored so the envelope can grow.
type event struct {
        EventID  string `json:"event_id"`
        EntityID string `json:"entity_id"`
        Action   string `json:"action"`
        Data     struct {
                Service string `json:"service"`
        } `json:"data"`
}

// ServiceID is the name auth-api's deny entries use for this service. A deny of any other service
// says nothing about initiative credentials.
const ServiceID = "initiative-api"

// Revoker ends a subject's credentials. domain.Credentials implements it.
type Revoker interface {
        RevokeOwner(ctx context.Context, owner, reason string) (int, error)
}

// Evictor closes every open stream of a user, on this pod and the others. *stream.Hub implements
// it.
type Evictor interface {
        EvictUser(userID string)
}

// Gate is the on/off switch for integration credentials. domain.Credentials implements it.
type Gate interface {
        Open(ctx context.Context) (bool, error)
        // Close turns credentials off and persists it, so a restart stays closed until an
        // administrator's reconcile (the consumer fell behind retention).
        Close(ctx context.Context) error
        // Pause turns credentials off in this process only (an outage): a reconnect reopens it through
        // Open once the consumer has caught up, with no administrator step.
        Pause()
}

// handle applies one message. A nil error means ack it; an unparseable or irrelevant message is
// acked (redelivery cannot fix it), a failed revoke is returned so the bus redelivers.
//
// A deny for this service or a removed role ends the subject's integration credentials and then
// every open stream of that user (PADR-0039: a denied owner's open event streams close when the
// revocation lands); ev may be nil. Credentials are revoked first and a failure there is retried
// before anything is evicted; the eviction is idempotent, so a redelivery repeats it harmlessly.
func handle(ctx context.Context, rev Revoker, evict Evictor, body []byte) error {
        var ev event
        if err := json.Unmarshal(body, &ev); err != nil {
                slog.Error("dropping unparseable auth event", "error", err)
                return nil
        }
        reason := map[string]string{kindDeny: data.ReasonOwnerDenied, kindRoleRemoved: data.ReasonOwnerRoleRemoved}[ev.Action]
        if reason == "" || ev.EntityID == "" || (ev.Action == kindDeny && ev.Data.Service != ServiceID) {
                return nil
        }
        n, err := rev.RevokeOwner(ctx, ev.EntityID, reason)
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("revoke credentials for event %s: %w", ev.EventID, err)
        }
        slog.Info("revoked integration credentials", "event_id", ev.EventID, "reason", reason, "count", n)
        if evict != nil {
                evict.EvictUser(ev.EntityID)
        }
        return nil
}

// behindRetention reports whether a consumer that last acknowledged ackFloor can no longer replay
// the gap because the stream's oldest retained message is firstSeq. A consumer that never existed
// has nothing to miss: credentials are not issued before it is bound.
func behindRetention(exists bool, ackFloor, firstSeq uint64) bool {
        return exists && firstSeq > 1 && ackFloor+1 < firstSeq
}

// Consumer runs the durable pull consumer on AUTH_EVENTS. A Consumer is single-use: Stop latches,
// so a failed Start is followed by a new Consumer, not a second Start.
//
// Fail closed on the credential path only (PADR-0039). A NATS outage does not stop the API: the
// client reconnects forever, and when the connection has been down longer than MaxDisconnect the
// credential gate is closed, so credentials answer 503 until the consumer is back and has caught up
// (or, if the stream's retention overtook it, until a reconcile).
type Consumer struct {
        rev  Revoker
        gate Gate
        // Stream and Filter default to AUTH_EVENTS and auth.events.>; tests point them at a prefixed stream.
        Stream, Filter string
        // Evictor, when set, closes a denied user's open streams (see handle).
        Evictor Evictor
        // MaxDisconnect is how long NATS may stay unreachable before the gate closes; DefaultMaxDisconnect
        // when zero.
        MaxDisconnect time.Duration

        conn *nats.Conn
        cc   jetstream.ConsumeContext

        stopping    atomic.Bool
        disconnects atomic.Int64
        resyncing   atomic.Bool
        dead        chan struct{}
        deadOnce    sync.Once
        stopOnce    sync.Once
        sleep       func(time.Duration)

        mu           sync.Mutex
        downSince    time.Time // zero while connected
        closedByLoss bool      // the gate was closed by an outage, so a reconnect may reopen it
        now          func() time.Time
        behind       func(ctx context.Context) (bool, error) // whether retention overtook the consumer
        catchUp      func(ctx context.Context)               // reopens the gate once nothing is pending
}

// setupTimeout bounds one attempt at looking up and binding the consumer.
const setupTimeout = 30 * time.Second

// reconnectWait is the pause between reconnect tries.
const reconnectWait = 2 * time.Second

// DefaultMaxDisconnect is how long NATS may be unreachable before credentials fail closed.
const DefaultMaxDisconnect = 10 * time.Minute

// outageCheck is how often a down connection is compared with MaxDisconnect.
const outageCheck = 5 * time.Second

func (c *Consumer) onDisconnect(err error) {
        if c.stopping.Load() {
                return
        }
        n := c.disconnects.Add(1)
        c.mu.Lock()
        if c.downSince.IsZero() {
                c.downSince = c.now()
        }
        c.mu.Unlock()
        slog.Warn("lost the connection to NATS; auth events are not being applied until it returns", "disconnects", n, "error", err)
}

// DownFor is how long NATS has been unreachable (zero while connected).
func (c *Consumer) DownFor() time.Duration {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        if c.downSince.IsZero() {
                return 0
        }
        return c.now().Sub(c.downSince)
}

func (c *Consumer) onReconnect(ctx context.Context) {
        c.mu.Lock()
        c.downSince = time.Time{}
        c.mu.Unlock()
        slog.Info("reconnected to NATS", "disconnects", c.disconnects.Load())
        go c.resync(ctx) // every reconnect: a gap shorter than MaxDisconnect can still outlast the stream's retention
}

// onClosed runs when the client gives up for good (a rejected credential, say): this consumer is
// gone, so credentials are paused and Dead is signalled; the owner builds a new Consumer. The rest of
// the API keeps serving.
func (c *Consumer) onClosed() {
        if c.stopping.Load() {
                return
        }
        slog.Error("the NATS connection is closed for good; auth events can no longer be applied and integration credentials are closed", "disconnects", c.disconnects.Load())
        c.closeGate()
        c.deadOnce.Do(func() { close(c.dead) })
}

// Dead is closed when the connection to NATS is lost for good. The consumer cannot recover; build
// another with New and Start it.
func (c *Consumer) Dead() <-chan struct{} { return c.dead }

func (c *Consumer) closeGate() {
        c.gate.Pause() // in this process only: a restart then rechecks retention itself
        c.mu.Lock()
        c.closedByLoss = true
        c.mu.Unlock()
}

// watchOutage closes the gate once the connection has been down past MaxDisconnect.
func (c *Consumer) watchOutage(ctx context.Context) {
        t := time.NewTicker(outageCheck)
        defer t.Stop()
        for {
                select {
                case <-ctx.Done():
                        return
                case <-t.C:
                }
                if c.stopping.Load() {
                        return
                }
                c.checkOutage(ctx)
        }
}

func (c *Consumer) checkOutage(ctx context.Context) {
        limit := c.MaxDisconnect
        if limit <= 0 {
                limit = DefaultMaxDisconnect
        }
        c.mu.Lock()
        already := c.closedByLoss
        c.mu.Unlock()
        if d := c.DownFor(); d > limit && !already {
                slog.Error("NATS has been unreachable too long to trust the credential list; closing integration credentials", "down_for", d.String(), "limit", limit.String())
                c.closeGate()
        }
}

// resync runs after every reconnect, one at a time. It asks whether the stream's retention overtook
// this consumer while it was away: if so a deny may be lost for good, so the gate is closed for
// everyone (persisted, the way startup does it) until a reconcile. Otherwise, if an outage had paused
// credentials, they reopen once the consumer has drained what was pending. A failed check or close is
// retried with a growing wait until it succeeds or the consumer stops.
func (c *Consumer) resync(ctx context.Context) {
        if !c.resyncing.CompareAndSwap(false, true) {
                return // one already running; it will see the current state
        }
        defer c.resyncing.Store(false)
        wait := time.Second
        for !c.stopping.Load() && ctx.Err() == nil {
                if c.resyncOnce(ctx) {
                        return
                }
                c.sleep(wait)
                wait = min(wait*2, 30*time.Second)
        }
}

// ready returns the stream-bound checks, nil until start has bound the consumer. A reconnect can
// fire while start is still waiting on JetStream (RetryOnFailedConnect); start's own checks cover
// that window.
func (c *Consumer) ready() (behind func(context.Context) (bool, error), catchUp func(context.Context)) {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        return c.behind, c.catchUp
}

// closeIfBehind persists the gate closure when retention overtook the consumer. done is true once
// the question is settled (the gate was closed, or the check does not apply yet); a false done with
// behind false means the consumer is within retention.
func (c *Consumer) closeIfBehind(ctx context.Context) (behind, done bool) {
        check, _ := c.ready()
        if check == nil {
                return false, true
        }
        behind, err := check(ctx)
        if err != nil {
                slog.Error("could not check the auth-event consumer against the stream; will retry", "error", err)
                return false, false
        }
        if !behind {
                return false, true
        }
        slog.Error("auth-event consumer is behind stream retention; closing integration credentials until reconcile")
        if err := c.gate.Close(ctx); err != nil {
                slog.Error("could not persist the credential gate closure; will retry", "error", err)
                return true, false
        }
        c.mu.Lock()
        c.closedByLoss = false // a reconcile reopens it, not a reconnect
        c.mu.Unlock()
        return true, true
}

// resyncOnce reports whether the check is finished.
func (c *Consumer) resyncOnce(ctx context.Context) bool {
        behind, done := c.closeIfBehind(ctx)
        if !done || behind {
                return done
        }
        _, catchUp := c.ready()
        if catchUp == nil {
                return true
        }
        c.mu.Lock()
        reopen := c.closedByLoss
        c.mu.Unlock()
        if reopen {
                catchUp(ctx)
                c.mu.Lock()
                c.closedByLoss = false
                c.mu.Unlock()
        }
        return true
}

// New builds a consumer. The gate must start closed.
func New(rev Revoker, gate Gate) *Consumer {
        return &Consumer{rev: rev, gate: gate, Stream: StreamName, Filter: FilterSubject, now: time.Now, sleep: time.Sleep, dead: make(chan struct{})}
}

// Start connects, binds the durable consumer, and begins revoking. The gate opens only after the
// consumer has drained what was pending, and stays closed when the consumer fell behind the
// stream's retention (until an administrator's reconcile reopens it). A failed Start closes its
// connection; build a new Consumer to try again.
func (c *Consumer) Start(ctx context.Context, natsURL, durable string) error {
        err := c.start(ctx, natsURL, durable)
        if err != nil {
                c.Stop()
        }
        return err
}

func (c *Consumer) start(ctx context.Context, natsURL, durable string) error {
        if c.Evictor == nil {
                // A deny would be acked with its streams still open. Wiring the evictor is the owner's job and
                // must happen before the first event can be consumed, so a consumer without one never starts.
                return errors.New("auth-event consumer has no evictor")
        }
        if durable == "" {
                durable = defaultDurable
        }
        opts := []nats.Option{
                nats.Name(durable), nats.ReconnectWait(reconnectWait),
                nats.MaxReconnects(-1), nats.RetryOnFailedConnect(true), // the outage clock, not a retry count, decides
                nats.DisconnectErrHandler(func(_ *nats.Conn, err error) { c.onDisconnect(err) }),
                nats.ReconnectHandler(func(*nats.Conn) { c.onReconnect(ctx) }),
                nats.ClosedHandler(func(*nats.Conn) { c.onClosed() }),
        }
        opts = append(opts, authOptions()...)
        conn, err := nats.Connect(natsURL, opts...)
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("connect to NATS: %w", err)
        }
        c.conn = conn
        js, err := jetstream.New(conn)
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("create JetStream context: %w", err)
        }
        // Setup calls are bounded per attempt; ctx outlives them and serves the running consumer.
        sctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, setupTimeout)
        defer cancel()
        stream, err := js.Stream(sctx, c.Stream)
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("stream %s: %w", c.Stream, err)
        }
        sinfo, err := stream.Info(sctx)
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("stream info: %w", err)
        }
        prior, err := stream.Consumer(sctx, durable)
        exists := err == nil
        if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, jetstream.ErrConsumerNotFound) {
                return fmt.Errorf("look up consumer %q: %w", durable, err)
        }
        if exists {
                pinfo, ierr := prior.Info(sctx)
                if ierr != nil {
                        return fmt.Errorf("consumer info: %w", ierr)
                }
                if behindRetention(true, pinfo.AckFloor.Stream, sinfo.State.FirstSeq) {
                        slog.Error("auth-event consumer is behind stream retention; integration credentials stay closed until reconcile")
                        if err := c.gate.Close(sctx); err != nil {
                                return fmt.Errorf("close credential gate: %w", err)
                        }
                }
        }
        cons, err := stream.CreateOrUpdateConsumer(sctx, jetstream.ConsumerConfig{
                Durable: durable, FilterSubject: c.Filter, AckPolicy: jetstream.AckExplicitPolicy,
        })
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("bind durable consumer %q: %w", durable, err)
        }
        cc, err := cons.Consume(func(msg jetstream.Msg) { c.process(ctx, msg) })
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("start consuming: %w", err)
        }
        c.cc = cc
        behind := func(ctx context.Context) (bool, error) {
                si, err := stream.Info(ctx)
                if err != nil {
                        return false, err
                }
                ci, err := cons.Info(ctx)
                if err != nil {
                        return false, err
                }
                return behindRetention(true, ci.AckFloor.Stream, si.State.FirstSeq), nil
        }
        catchUp := func(ctx context.Context) { c.openWhenCaughtUp(ctx, cons) }
        c.mu.Lock()
        c.behind, c.catchUp = behind, catchUp
        c.mu.Unlock()
        go catchUp(ctx)
        go c.watchOutage(ctx)
        return nil
}

func (c *Consumer) process(ctx context.Context, msg jetstream.Msg) {
        if err := handle(ctx, c.rev, c.Evictor, msg.Data()); err != nil {
                slog.Error("auth event handling failed, will retry", "error", err)
                _ = msg.Nak()
                return
        }
        _ = msg.Ack()
}

// openWhenCaughtUp polls until nothing is pending or awaiting ack, then opens the gate. Each round
// rechecks retention first, so a poll that outlives an outage cannot open a gate the stream's
// retention has since closed; it ends when the consumer stops.
func (c *Consumer) openWhenCaughtUp(ctx context.Context, cons jetstream.Consumer) {
        t := time.NewTicker(time.Second)
        defer t.Stop()
        for {
                select {
                case <-ctx.Done():
                        return
                case <-t.C:
                }
                if c.stopping.Load() {
                        return
                }
                if behind, done := c.closeIfBehind(ctx); behind && done {
                        return
                }
                info, err := cons.Info(ctx)
                if err != nil || info.NumPending > 0 || info.NumAckPending > 0 {
                        continue
                }
                open, err := c.gate.Open(ctx)
                if err != nil {
                        slog.Error("open credential gate", "error", err)
                        continue
                }
                slog.Info("integration credentials caught up", "open", open)
                return
        }
}

// Stop halts consumption and closes the connection. It is safe to call more than once and from
// several goroutines (the shutdown path and the Dead path can both reach it).
func (c *Consumer) Stop() {
        c.stopping.Store(true)
        c.stopOnce.Do(func() {
                if c.cc != nil {
                        c.cc.Stop()
                }
                if c.conn != nil {
                        c.conn.Close()
                }
        })
}

// authOptions is the broker login from the environment: a NATS_CREDS file when set, else
// NATS_USER and NATS_PASSWORD.
func authOptions() []nats.Option {
        if creds := os.Getenv("NATS_CREDS"); creds != "" {
                return []nats.Option{nats.UserCredentials(creds)}
        }
        if user := os.Getenv("NATS_USER"); user != "" {
                return []nats.Option{nats.UserInfo(user, os.Getenv("NATS_PASSWORD"))}
        }
        return nil
}

package events

import (
        "context"
        "encoding/json"
        "fmt"
        "strings"

        "github.com/nats-io/nats.go"
        "github.com/nats-io/nats.go/jetstream"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/events/stream"
)

// SubjectPrefix is the root of initiative's entity-change subjects:
// initiative.events.<entity>.<action> (docs/messaging-nats.md, PADR-0026). The INITIATIVE_EVENTS
// stream binds initiative.events.>.
const SubjectPrefix = "initiative.events."

// jsPublisher is the part of jetstream.JetStream the publisher uses.
type jsPublisher interface {
        PublishMsg(ctx context.Context, m *nats.Msg, opts ...jetstream.PublishOpt) (*jetstream.PubAck, error)
}

// Publisher puts encounter events on the platform bus. It implements stream.Bus; the hub calls it
// from its own queue, so a slow or absent broker never reaches a write (fail-open, PADR-0024).
// There is no retry queue: an event that cannot be published is dropped and counted by the hub.
type Publisher struct {
        js   jsPublisher
        conn *nats.Conn
}

// NewPublisher connects to natsURL with the login from the environment (NATS_CREDS, or NATS_USER
// and NATS_PASSWORD). The client keeps retrying a broker that is down at start and after a loss, so
// the service starts either way; a publication made meanwhile fails and is dropped.
func NewPublisher(natsURL string) (*Publisher, error) {
        opts := append([]nats.Option{
                nats.Name("initiative-api-publisher"), nats.ReconnectWait(reconnectWait),
                nats.MaxReconnects(-1), nats.RetryOnFailedConnect(true),
        }, authOptions()...)
        conn, err := nats.Connect(natsURL, opts...)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, fmt.Errorf("connect to NATS: %w", err)
        }
        js, err := jetstream.New(conn)
        if err != nil {
                conn.Close()
                return nil, fmt.Errorf("create JetStream context: %w", err)
        }
        return &Publisher{js: js, conn: conn}, nil
}

// UserSubjectPrefix is the root of the subjects of events addressed to one user:
// initiative.user.<user_id>.<type>, where the dotted type (turn.yours) stays two tokens, so a
// consumer filters with initiative.user.*.turn.>.
const UserSubjectPrefix = "initiative.user."

// Subject is the subject an event is published on.
func Subject(e stream.Event) string {
        if e.UserID != "" {
                parts := strings.Split(e.Type, ".")
                for i := range parts {
                        parts[i] = token(parts[i])
                }
                return UserSubjectPrefix + token(e.UserID) + "." + strings.Join(parts, ".")
        }
        return SubjectPrefix + token(e.EntityType) + "." + token(e.Action)
}

// token keeps a value to one subject segment; a dot, wildcard or space would change the subject.
func token(s string) string {
        return strings.Map(func(r rune) rune {
                switch r {
                case '.', '*', '>', ' ':
                        return '_'
                }
                return r
        }, s)
}

// Publish sends e and waits for the stream's acknowledgement, bounded by ctx. event_id doubles as
// the Nats-Msg-Id, so a republished event is stored once inside the stream's duplicate window.
func (p *Publisher) Publish(ctx context.Context, e stream.Event) error {
        body, err := json.Marshal(e)
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("encode event %s: %w", e.EventID, err)
        }
        msg := &nats.Msg{Subject: Subject(e), Data: body, Header: nats.Header{}}
        msg.Header.Set(nats.MsgIdHdr, e.EventID)
        if _, err := p.js.PublishMsg(ctx, msg); err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("publish %s: %w", msg.Subject, err)
        }
        return nil
}

// Close flushes what the client still has buffered and closes the connection. Call it after the
// hub's queue has stopped (Hub.Close), so events the queue already handed over are not lost on a
// deploy. A broker that is down makes Drain give up; the events are then dropped, as in any outage.
func (p *Publisher) Close() {
        if err := p.conn.Drain(); err != nil {
                p.conn.Close()
        }
}

package stream

import (
        "context"
        "log/slog"
        "sync"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// busPublishTimeout bounds one publication, so a stuck broker cannot hold the queue.
const busPublishTimeout = 2 * time.Second

type busQueue struct {
        bus  Bus
        ch   chan Event
        done chan struct{}
        wg   sync.WaitGroup
}

// AttachBus publishes the bus form of every event to bus from a queue of the given size, in a
// goroutine, fail-open: a full queue or a failed publication drops the event (counted by Dropped
// and logged) and never affects the write or the WebSocket subscribers. Call Close to stop it.
func (h *Hub) AttachBus(bus Bus, queue int) {
        h.closeBus() // a second attach replaces the first, whose goroutine must not outlive it
        q := &busQueue{bus: bus, ch: make(chan Event, max(queue, 1)), done: make(chan struct{})}
        q.wg.Add(1)
        go func() {
                defer q.wg.Done()
                for {
                        select {
                        case ev := <-q.ch:
                                ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), busPublishTimeout)
                                if err := bus.Publish(ctx, ev); err != nil {
                                        h.dropped.Add(1)
                                        slog.Warn("publishing an encounter event to the bus failed", "event_id", ev.EventID, "type", ev.Type, "error", err)
                                }
                                cancel()
                        case <-q.done:
                                return
                        }
                }
        }()
        h.mu.Lock()
        h.bus = q
        h.mu.Unlock()
}

// Close stops the bus and notice goroutines, if started.
func (h *Hub) Close() {
        h.closeBus()
        h.stopNotices()
}

func (h *Hub) closeBus() {
        h.mu.Lock()
        q := h.bus
        h.bus = nil
        h.mu.Unlock()
        if q != nil {
                close(q.done)
                q.wg.Wait()
        }
}

// publishNoticeToBus queues one event per addressee for the bus, where a push service can consume
// it. It carries what the addressee's own stream carries and is published on the addressee's own
// subject. Two addressees are left out: one who muted the type (the consumer has no other way to
// see the mute) and one who is no longer a member of the session, since nothing ends a push the way
// a stream's eviction ends a stream (so a removed requester gets no change_request.approved).
// Fail-open like every bus publication, except the membership check, which fails closed.
func (h *Hub) publishNoticeToBus(n data.Notice, muted map[string]bool, isMember func(string) bool) {
        h.mu.RLock()
        q := h.bus
        h.mu.RUnlock()
        if q == nil {
                return
        }
        seen := map[string]bool{}
        for _, u := range n.Users {
                if u == "" || seen[u] || muted[u] {
                        continue
                }
                seen[u] = true
                if !isMember(u) {
                        continue
                }
                ev := noticeEvent(n, newEventID())
                ev.UserID = u
                select {
                case q.ch <- ev:
                default:
                        h.dropped.Add(1)
                        slog.Warn("the bus queue is full; dropping a notice", "event_id", ev.EventID, "type", ev.Type)
                }
        }
}

func (h *Hub) publishToBus(ctx context.Context, em *emission) {
        h.mu.RLock()
        q := h.bus
        h.mu.RUnlock()
        // A private encounter has no audience beyond its owner; the bus is for what a session's other
        // consumers may react to.
        if q == nil || (em.c.SessionID == "" && em.c.After.SessionID == "") {
                return
        }
        ev, ok := em.busEvent()
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        select {
        case q.ch <- ev:
        default:
                h.dropped.Add(1)
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "the bus queue is full; dropping an encounter event", "event_id", ev.EventID, "type", ev.Type)
        }
}

// Package stream turns committed encounter changes into the events subscribers receive. The data
// layer reports each commit to a Sink (data.Sink); the Hub here is that sink. It renders the
// committed document once per viewer, with the same view builder REST uses, and sends each
// subscriber only what its role may see: ids, never data.
package stream

import (
        "crypto/rand"
        "fmt"
        "strings"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// Source is the publisher name in every envelope.
const Source = "initiative-api"

// Event is one envelope (docs/messaging-nats.md). Data carries ids only: the changed combatant ids
// and, for turn and state changes, the status, round and current combatant id. A client that wants
// the content fetches the encounter, so the content always passes through the view builder.
type Event struct {
        EventID     string    `json:"event_id"`
        OccurredAt  time.Time `json:"occurred_at"`
        Source      string    `json:"source"`
        EncounterID string    `json:"encounter_id"`
        SessionID   string    `json:"session_id,omitempty"`
        // UserID is the addressee of a notice published to the bus (one event per addressee); it is
        // empty on every other event.
        UserID     string `json:"user_id,omitempty"`
        EntityType string `json:"entity_type"`
        EntityID   string `json:"entity_id"`
        Action     string `json:"action"`
        Type       string `json:"type"`
        // Revision orders a GM's or co-GM's events. Other viewers never get it: the stored revision
        // moves on every edit, hidden ones included, so it would show them edits they cannot see.
        Revision int64 `json:"revision,omitempty"`
        // ViewTag is, for every other viewer, the digest of the view the change left them with (the same
        // value their encounter view carries). They order events by it and refetch when it differs from
        // the one they hold; a change that leaves it unchanged produces no event for them at all.
        ViewTag string         `json:"view_tag,omitempty"`
        Origin  Origin         `json:"origin"`
        Data    map[string]any `json:"data"`
}

// Origin tags the kind of writer and, to the originating user's own streams only, the client id
// they sent, so a client can recognise its own echo.
type Origin struct {
        Kind     string `json:"kind"`
        ClientID string `json:"client_id,omitempty"`
}

// actionOf is the last segment of an event type ("turn.advanced" is "advanced").
func actionOf(eventType string) string {
        if i := strings.LastIndex(eventType, "."); i >= 0 {
                return eventType[i+1:]
        }
        return eventType
}

// newEventID returns a random (version 4) UUID.
func newEventID() string {
        var b [16]byte
        _, _ = rand.Read(b[:])
        b[6] = b[6]&0x0f | 0x40
        b[8] = b[8]&0x3f | 0x80
        return fmt.Sprintf("%x-%x-%x-%x-%x", b[0:4], b[4:6], b[6:8], b[8:10], b[10:])
}

// base is the envelope of c with no data and no client id: what every viewer shares.
func base(c data.Change, id string) Event {
        kind := c.Origin.Kind
        if kind == "" {
                kind = data.OriginSystem
        }
        return Event{
                EventID: id, OccurredAt: c.At.UTC(), Source: Source,
                EncounterID: c.After.ID, SessionID: c.SessionID,
                EntityType: "encounter", EntityID: c.After.ID,
                Origin: Origin{Kind: kind},
                Data:   map[string]any{},
        }
}

func typed(e Event, eventType string) Event {
        e.Type, e.Action = eventType, actionOf(eventType)
        return e
}

package stream

import (
        "context"
        "log/slog"
        "sync"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/domain"
)

// emission is one committed change on its way to its subscribers. It renders the after-document
// for a viewer at most once per distinct view (the GM level needs no rendering; the others are
// keyed by level, plus the actor for a player, because a player sees a hidden combatant they
// control), and is dropped when the fan-out ends, so no snapshot outlives it.
type emission struct {
        ctx context.Context
        r   Renderer
        c   data.Change
        id  string

        // The tracker definition is looked up once per emission (after, and before only if the game
        // system changed), so fan-out does no per-viewer reads.
        afterDef, beforeDef   []data.TrackerField
        afterOnce, beforeOnce sync.Once

        mu    sync.Mutex
        views map[viewKey]*viewInfo
}

func (em *emission) defAfter() []data.TrackerField {
        em.afterOnce.Do(func() { em.afterDef = em.r.Definition(em.ctx, em.c.After) })
        return em.afterDef
}

func (em *emission) defBefore() []data.TrackerField {
        if em.c.Before == nil || em.c.Before.GameSystemID == em.c.After.GameSystemID {
                return em.defAfter()
        }
        em.beforeOnce.Do(func() { em.beforeDef = em.r.Definition(em.ctx, *em.c.Before) })
        return em.beforeDef
}

type viewKey struct {
        level data.ViewLevel
        actor string
}

// viewInfo is what one viewer's rendering of the encounter shows of the ids an event can name.
type viewInfo struct {
        visible map[string]bool
        current string
        tag     string
        // beforeTag is the tag of the view before the change; known is false when the write path had no
        // before-document, and then the change cannot be shown to leave the view alone.
        beforeTag string
        known     bool
        err       error
}

// unchanged reports whether the change left this viewer's view as it was.
func (vi *viewInfo) unchanged() bool { return vi.err == nil && vi.known && vi.tag == vi.beforeTag }

func newEmission(ctx context.Context, r Renderer, c data.Change) *emission {
        return &emission{ctx: ctx, r: r, c: c, id: newEventID(), views: map[viewKey]*viewInfo{}}
}

func (em *emission) info(v data.Viewer) *viewInfo {
        k := viewKey{level: v.Level()}
        if v.Level() == data.ViewPlayer {
                k.actor = v.Actor()
        }
        em.mu.Lock()
        defer em.mu.Unlock()
        if vi, ok := em.views[k]; ok {
                return vi
        }
        vi := &viewInfo{visible: map[string]bool{}}
        view, err := domain.BuildView(em.c.After, em.defAfter(), v)
        if err != nil {
                vi.err = err
                slog.WarnContext(em.ctx, "rendering an encounter for an event subscriber failed; sending it without ids", "encounter", em.c.After.ID, "error", err)
        } else {
                list, _ := view["combatants"].([]map[string]any)
                for _, c := range list {
                        if id, ok := c["id"].(string); ok {
                                vi.visible[id] = true
                        }
                }
                vi.current, _ = view["current_combatant_id"].(string)
                vi.tag, _ = view[domain.FieldViewTag].(string)
                if b := em.c.Before; b != nil {
                        if bv, berr := domain.BuildView(*b, em.defBefore(), v); berr == nil {
                                vi.beforeTag, _ = bv[domain.FieldViewTag].(string)
                                vi.known = true
                        }
                }
        }
        em.views[k] = vi
        return vi
}

// idEvent reports whether t names changed combatants.
func idEvent(t string) bool {
        switch t {
        case data.EventCombatantAdded, data.EventCombatantUpdated, data.EventCombatantRemoved, data.EventCombatantRestored, data.EventControllers:
                return true
        }
        return false
}

// stateEvent reports whether t carries the encounter's status, round and current combatant.
func stateEvent(t string) bool {
        switch t {
        case data.EventTurn, data.EventStarted, data.EventEnded, data.EventReset:
                return true
        }
        return false
}

// forViewer is the event v may receive for the change, or false if none. It fails closed: when
// the viewer's rendering cannot be had the event is the generic encounter.updated with no ids,
// never the unfiltered one.
func (em *emission) forViewer(v data.Viewer) (Event, bool) {
        c := em.c
        ev := base(c, em.id)
        if c.Origin.Kind == data.OriginUser && c.Origin.ClientID != "" && v.Actor() != "" && v.Actor() == c.Origin.ActorID {
                ev.Origin.ClientID = c.Origin.ClientID
        }
        switch {
        case v.Level() == data.ViewNone:
                return Event{}, false
        case c.GMOnly:
                // A lock or inactive flag is limit and entitlement state; only the GM hears of it.
                if v.Level() != data.ViewGM {
                        return Event{}, false
                }
                ev = typed(ev, data.EventUpdated)
                ev.Revision = c.After.Revision
                return ev, true
        case v.Level() == data.ViewGM:
                return em.gmEvent(ev), true
        }
        if c.Type == data.EventCreated || c.Type == data.EventDeleted || c.Type == data.EventReleased {
                // These name no combatant and say the encounter itself appeared or went (a released
                // encounter no longer belongs to the viewer's session, so it is not rendered).
                return typed(ev, c.Type), true
        }
        vi := em.info(v)
        if vi.unchanged() {
                // A change the viewer cannot see: no event, no gap to explain, nothing to refetch.
                return Event{}, false
        }
        ev.ViewTag = vi.tag
        return em.filtered(ev, vi), true
}

func (em *emission) gmEvent(ev Event) Event {
        c := em.c
        ev = typed(ev, c.Type)
        ev.Revision = c.After.Revision
        switch {
        case idEvent(c.Type):
                if ids := ids(c); len(ids) > 0 {
                        ev.Data["combatant_ids"] = ids
                } else {
                        ev = typed(ev, data.EventUpdated)
                }
        case stateEvent(c.Type):
                ev.Data["status"], ev.Data["round"] = c.After.Status, c.After.Round
                if c.After.CurrentCombatantID != "" {
                        ev.Data["current_combatant_id"] = c.After.CurrentCombatantID
                }
        }
        return ev
}

// filtered is the event for a non-GM viewer: only ids the viewer's own rendering shows, and the
// current combatant only if the viewer can see it. A change that touched nothing the viewer can
// see becomes the generic encounter.updated with no ids, so the viewer refetches and a hidden
// combatant's id, name, health, notes and initiative never appear.
func (em *emission) filtered(ev Event, vi *viewInfo) Event {
        c := em.c
        if vi.err != nil {
                return typed(ev, data.EventUpdated)
        }
        ev = typed(ev, c.Type)
        switch {
        case idEvent(c.Type):
                var shown []string
                for _, id := range ids(c) {
                        if vi.visible[id] {
                                shown = append(shown, id)
                        }
                }
                if len(shown) == 0 {
                        return typed(ev, data.EventUpdated)
                }
                ev.Data["combatant_ids"] = shown
        case stateEvent(c.Type):
                ev.Data["status"], ev.Data["round"] = c.After.Status, c.After.Round
                if vi.current != "" {
                        ev.Data["current_combatant_id"] = vi.current
                }
        }
        return ev
}

// busEvent is the form published to the platform bus: the observer-level rendering of the change,
// with no client id, and nothing at all for a GM-only change. Any consumer may receive it, so it
// is as restricted as a player's.
func (em *emission) busEvent() (Event, bool) {
        if em.c.GMOnly {
                return Event{}, false
        }
        ev := base(em.c, em.id)
        ev.Origin.ClientID = ""
        if em.c.Type == data.EventCreated || em.c.Type == data.EventDeleted || em.c.Type == data.EventReleased {
                return typed(ev, em.c.Type), true
        }
        vi := em.info(data.BusViewer(em.c.After.SessionID))
        if vi.unchanged() {
                return Event{}, false
        }
        ev.ViewTag = vi.tag
        return em.filtered(ev, vi), true
}

package stream

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "log/slog"
        "slices"
        "sync"
        "sync/atomic"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// Renderer gives the hub the tracker definition BuildView needs, looked up once per change.
// *domain.Encounters implements it.
type Renderer interface {
        Definition(ctx context.Context, enc data.Encounter) []data.TrackerField
}

// Bus receives the observer-level form of each event for the platform message bus. Publish must
// not block; the hub hands it events from a bounded queue and drops (and counts) when the queue is
// full, since bus publication is fail-open.
type Bus interface {
        Publish(ctx context.Context, e Event) error
}

// Hub is the data.Sink: it fans each committed change out to the subscriptions it concerns, after
// the commit, without blocking the writer. There is no outbox: a crash between commit and
// publication loses the event, which clients cover by refetching when they reconnect. A viewer who
// is not a GM or co-GM is told only of changes that alter their own view (see Event.ViewTag), so a
// hidden edit leaves them no gap.
type Hub struct {
        r        Renderer
        mu       sync.RWMutex
        sub      map[*Subscription]struct{}
        bus      *busQueue
        stopping atomic.Bool
        live     atomic.Int64 // stream handlers still running, upgraded or not
        rel      *Relay
        prefs    Preferences
        managers Managers
        members  Members

        noticeClosed  bool // set under mu by Close; final
        noticeOnce    sync.Once
        noticeQ       chan noticeJob
        noticeDone    chan struct{}
        noticeWG      sync.WaitGroup
        noticePending sync.WaitGroup

        pendingTTL time.Duration

        dropped atomic.Int64 // events not delivered: full subscriber buffer closed, or full bus queue
        emitted atomic.Int64
}

// PendingTTL is how long a subscription may wait for Grant before the hub closes it, so a transport
// that never grants or closes one cannot leak it.
const PendingTTL = 10 * time.Second

// SetPendingTTL changes how long a pending subscription lives; it applies to later Opens.
func (h *Hub) SetPendingTTL(d time.Duration) {
        h.mu.Lock()
        h.pendingTTL = d
        h.mu.Unlock()
}

// New returns a hub that renders with r.
func New(r Renderer) *Hub {
        return &Hub{r: r, sub: map[*Subscription]struct{}{}, pendingTTL: PendingTTL}
}

// Dropped is the number of events not delivered because a subscriber's buffer or the bus queue was
// full.
func (h *Hub) Dropped() int64 { return h.dropped.Load() }

// Emitted is the number of committed changes the hub has handled.
func (h *Hub) Emitted() int64 { return h.emitted.Load() }

// Subscription is one connection's feed. It is made pending by Open, receives nothing until Grant
// gives it the viewer proving what the subscriber may see, and ends by Close, by Evict, or when the
// hub finds the subscriber behind. With an encounter id it follows that encounter; with none it
// follows every encounter the viewer's audience hears of (a session's, or an owner's private
// ones), which is how encounter.created reaches anyone, since a new encounter has no subscribers
// of its own. C is closed when the subscription ends.
type Subscription struct {
        C chan Event

        h           *Hub
        session     string // fixed by Open; the audience Evict matches, before and after Grant
        actor       string
        encounterID string
        userLevel   bool                        // receives only addressed events, across sessions; never has a viewer
        viewer      atomic.Pointer[data.Viewer] // nil until Grant
        overflowed  atomic.Bool
        shutdown    atomic.Bool // closed because the server is stopping

        mu     sync.Mutex // guards closed, the grant, and every send on and close of C
        closed bool
        timer  *time.Timer // ends a subscription never granted
}

// ErrEvicted means the subscription ended (evicted, closed, or overflowed) before Grant.
var ErrEvicted = errors.New("stream: subscription ended before it was granted")

// ErrGranted means Grant was called a second time: a viewer is fixed when first granted, so a
// higher level cannot be swapped in without ending the subscription and authorizing again.
var ErrGranted = errors.New("stream: subscription already granted")

// ErrViewer means Grant was given a viewer that cannot stream: a zero one, one with no actor (such
// as BusViewer), or one for a different actor or session than Open named.
var ErrViewer = errors.New("stream: viewer does not match the subscription")

// Open registers a pending subscription of actor in the session ("" for an owner's private
// encounters) with a buffer of buf events (at least 1). An empty encounterID follows every
// encounter in the viewer's audience. A pending subscription receives nothing, but Evict already
// reaches it, so an eviction that lands while the subscriber is being authorized is not lost:
// authorize, then Grant. The transport must Close the subscription on every path that does not
// end in Grant; one still pending after PendingTTL is closed by the hub.
func (h *Hub) Open(sessionID, actor, encounterID string, buf int) *Subscription {
        s := &Subscription{C: make(chan Event, max(buf, 1)), h: h, session: sessionID, actor: actor, encounterID: encounterID}
        h.mu.Lock()
        h.sub[s] = struct{}{}
        ttl := h.pendingTTL
        h.mu.Unlock()
        if ttl > 0 {
                s.mu.Lock()
                s.timer = time.AfterFunc(ttl, s.expire)
                s.mu.Unlock()
        }
        if h.stopping.Load() { // registered after Shutdown took its snapshot
                s.shutdown.Store(true)
                s.Close()
        }
        return s
}

// OpenUser registers a user-level subscription: the addressed events of every session actor
// belongs to, and no encounter state. It needs no Grant (there is no scope to authorize; each
// delivery checks the session membership). The transport must Close it.
func (h *Hub) OpenUser(actor string, buf int) *Subscription {
        s := &Subscription{C: make(chan Event, max(buf, 1)), h: h, actor: actor, userLevel: true}
        h.mu.Lock()
        h.sub[s] = struct{}{}
        h.mu.Unlock()
        if h.stopping.Load() {
                s.shutdown.Store(true)
                s.Close()
        }
        return s
}

// Grant makes the subscription live for v, which the caller obtained by authorizing the actor
// (data.Sessions.AuthorizeView or an owner viewer). It fails with ErrEvicted if the subscription
// ended since Open, with ErrViewer if v could not stream, and with ErrGranted on a second call.
func (s *Subscription) Grant(v data.Viewer) error {
        s.mu.Lock()
        defer s.mu.Unlock()
        if s.closed {
                return ErrEvicted
        }
        if v.Level() == data.ViewNone || v.Actor() == "" || v.Actor() != s.actor || v.SessionID() != s.session {
                return ErrViewer
        }
        if !s.viewer.CompareAndSwap(nil, &v) {
                return ErrGranted
        }
        if s.timer != nil {
                s.timer.Stop()
        }
        return nil
}

// send delivers ev without blocking. C is only ever sent on and closed under mu, so a send never
// meets a closed channel. overflow means the buffer was full.
func (s *Subscription) send(ev Event) (overflow bool) {
        s.mu.Lock()
        defer s.mu.Unlock()
        if s.closed {
                return false
        }
        select {
        case s.C <- ev:
                return false
        default:
                return true
        }
}

// Close ends the subscription. It is safe to call more than once and from any goroutine.
func (s *Subscription) Close() {
        s.mu.Lock()
        s.closeLocked()
        s.mu.Unlock()
        s.unregister()
}

// closeLocked ends the subscription; s.mu must be held.
func (s *Subscription) closeLocked() {
        if s.closed {
                return
        }
        s.closed = true
        close(s.C)
        if s.timer != nil {
                s.timer.Stop()
        }
}

func (s *Subscription) unregister() {
        s.h.mu.Lock()
        delete(s.h.sub, s)
        s.h.mu.Unlock()
}

// expire ends a subscription still pending at its deadline. The grant and the close are decided
// under s.mu, so a Grant that returned nil is never followed by this close.
func (s *Subscription) expire() {
        s.mu.Lock()
        if s.viewer.Load() != nil || s.closed {
                s.mu.Unlock()
                return
        }
        s.closeLocked()
        s.mu.Unlock()
        s.unregister()
}

// Ended reports whether the subscription has been closed (evicted, overflowed, or closed).
func (s *Subscription) Ended() bool {
        s.mu.Lock()
        defer s.mu.Unlock()
        return s.closed
}

// ServerStopping reports whether the hub closed the subscription because the server is shutting
// down, so the transport can say so (close code 1001) and the client reconnect elsewhere.
func (s *Subscription) ServerStopping() bool { return s.shutdown.Load() }

// Overflowed reports whether the hub closed the subscription because its buffer was full.
func (s *Subscription) Overflowed() bool { return s.overflowed.Load() }

// Evict closes every subscription (pending or live) of userID on the session, or of everyone on it
// when userID is empty, here and on the other pods (see Relay). A subscription's viewer is fixed
// when it is granted, so a user who is removed, blocked, leaves, or changes role must lose it: the
// client reconnects and is authorized again, at its new level or not at all.
func (h *Hub) Evict(sessionID, userID string) {
        if sessionID == "" {
                return // an empty session would match every private subscription
        }
        h.evictLocal(sessionID, userID)
        if r := h.relay(); r != nil {
                r.publishEvict(sessionID, userID)
        }
}

// EvictUser closes every subscription of userID, pending or live, in any session, user-level or on
// private encounters, here and on the other pods. Platform-wide access was revoked (a deny or a
// removed role in auth-api), not one session's, so nothing the user holds may stay open; the client
// reconnects and is authorized again, which auth-api refuses while the denial stands. It is not
// remembered: a user who is granted access again simply reconnects.
func (h *Hub) EvictUser(userID string) {
        if userID == "" {
                return // would match nobody here and, in a fan-out row, be mistaken for everybody
        }
        h.evictUserLocal(userID)
        if r := h.relay(); r != nil {
                r.publishEvict("", userID)
        }
}

func (h *Hub) evictUserLocal(userID string) {
        h.mu.RLock()
        var gone []*Subscription
        for s := range h.sub {
                if s.actor == userID {
                        gone = append(gone, s)
                }
        }
        h.mu.RUnlock()
        for _, s := range gone {
                s.Close()
        }
}

func (h *Hub) evictLocal(sessionID, userID string) {
        if sessionID == "" {
                return // an empty session would match every private subscription
        }
        h.mu.RLock()
        var gone []*Subscription
        for s := range h.sub {
                if s.session == sessionID && (userID == "" || s.actor == userID) {
                        gone = append(gone, s)
                }
        }
        h.mu.RUnlock()
        for _, s := range gone {
                s.Close()
        }
}

// evictAudience ends the subscriptions that would have heard c: a session's, or an owner's private
// ones.
func (h *Hub) evictAudience(c data.Change) {
        h.mu.RLock()
        var gone []*Subscription
        for s := range h.sub {
                if (c.SessionID != "" && s.session == c.SessionID) || (c.SessionID == "" && s.session == "" && !s.userLevel && s.actor == c.OwnerID) {
                        gone = append(gone, s)
                }
        }
        h.mu.RUnlock()
        for _, s := range gone {
                s.Close()
        }
}

// evictAll closes every subscription, pending or live, and returns how many. The relay does it when
// its tail restarts without a position, since evictions written in the gap are unknown: each client
// reconnects and is authorized again.
func (h *Hub) evictAll() int {
        h.mu.RLock()
        gone := make([]*Subscription, 0, len(h.sub))
        for s := range h.sub {
                gone = append(gone, s)
        }
        h.mu.RUnlock()
        for _, s := range gone {
                s.Close()
        }
        return len(gone)
}

// Emit implements data.Sink. It never blocks and never panics out: a failure to render or deliver
// for one subscriber affects only that subscriber.
func (h *Hub) Emit(ctx context.Context, c data.Change) {
        h.emitted.Add(1)
        em := newEmission(ctx, h.r, c)
        h.deliverLocal(ctx, em)
        h.publishToBus(ctx, em)
        for _, n := range turnNotices(c) {
                h.Notify(ctx, n)
        }
        if r := h.relay(); r != nil {
                r.publishChange(c, em.id)
        }
}

// deliverLocal sends the change to this pod's subscribers.
func (h *Hub) deliverLocal(ctx context.Context, em *emission) {
        for _, s := range h.snapshot(em.c.After.ID) {
                h.deliver(ctx, em, s)
        }
}

// emitRemote delivers a change another pod committed to this pod's subscribers. The bus and the
// relay are the originating pod's to feed, so neither runs here.
func (h *Hub) emitRemote(ctx context.Context, c data.Change, eventID string) {
        h.emitted.Add(1)
        em := newEmission(ctx, h.r, c)
        if eventID != "" {
                em.id = eventID // the same id on every pod, so a client can recognize a duplicate
        }
        h.deliverLocal(ctx, em)
}

// deliver sends the event s may receive for the change, recovering from a panic so one
// subscriber's failure cannot cost the others (or the bus) the event.
func (h *Hub) deliver(ctx context.Context, em *emission, s *Subscription) {
        defer func() {
                if r := recover(); r != nil {
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "delivering an encounter event to a subscriber panicked", "panic", r)
                }
        }()
        v := s.viewer.Load()
        if v == nil {
                return // pending
        }
        if !inAudience(*v, em.c) {
                return
        }
        ev, ok := em.forViewer(*v)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        if s.send(ev) {
                // A subscriber that cannot keep up is disconnected, not waited for: it reconnects and
                // refetches.
                s.overflowed.Store(true)
                h.dropped.Add(1)
                s.Close()
        }
}

func (h *Hub) snapshot(encounterID string) []*Subscription {
        h.mu.RLock()
        defer h.mu.RUnlock()
        out := make([]*Subscription, 0, len(h.sub))
        for s := range h.sub {
                if s.encounterID == "" || s.encounterID == encounterID {
                        out = append(out, s)
                }
        }
        return out
}

// inAudience reports whether v may hear of c at all. A session encounter's audience is its
// session's subscribers, taken from the session the encounter belonged to before the write (so a
// release or delete still reaches them); a private encounter's is its owner's.
func inAudience(v data.Viewer, c data.Change) bool {
        if v.Level() == data.ViewNone {
                return false
        }
        if c.SessionID != "" {
                return v.SessionID() == c.SessionID
        }
        return v.SessionID() == "" && v.Actor() == c.OwnerID
}

// ids are the ids a change reports, with duplicates removed.
func ids(c data.Change) []string {
        out := slices.Clone(c.CombatantID)
        slices.Sort(out)
        return slices.Compact(out)
}

// ErrShuttingDown means the hub no longer takes streams: the server is stopping.
var ErrShuttingDown = errors.New("stream: the server is shutting down")

// Shutdown ends every open subscription, marked as closed by the server stopping, and makes Open
// hand out subscriptions that are already closed. Call it before the HTTP server's own shutdown,
// which does not wait for upgraded connections.
func (h *Hub) Shutdown() {
        h.stopping.Store(true)
        h.mu.RLock()
        all := make([]*Subscription, 0, len(h.sub))
        for s := range h.sub {
                all = append(all, s)
        }
        h.mu.RUnlock()
        for _, s := range all {
                s.shutdown.Store(true)
                s.Close()
        }
}

// ShuttingDown reports whether Shutdown has been called.
func (h *Hub) ShuttingDown() bool { return h.stopping.Load() }

// Track marks one stream handler as running until the returned func is called. The HTTP server
// does not wait for hijacked (upgraded) connections, so Drain waits on these instead. Call it
// before any work that can fail and defer the result before the connection's own close.
func (h *Hub) Track() (done func()) {
        h.live.Add(1)
        var once sync.Once
        return func() { once.Do(func() { h.live.Add(-1) }) }
}

// Drain waits until every tracked stream handler has returned (each has sent its close frame and
// closed its connection), or ctx ends.
func (h *Hub) Drain(ctx context.Context) {
        t := time.NewTicker(20 * time.Millisecond)
        defer t.Stop()
        for h.live.Load() > 0 {
                select {
                case <-ctx.Done():
                        return
                case <-t.C:
                }
        }
}

package stream

import (
        "context"
        "log/slog"
        "slices"
        "sync"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

// Preferences says which notice types a user has turned off for a session.
type Preferences interface {
        Muted(ctx context.Context, userID, sessionID string) ([]string, error)
}

// Managers lists the user ids of a session's GM and co-GMs.
type Managers interface {
        ManagerIDs(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) ([]string, error)
}

// Members says whether a user currently belongs to a session.
type Members interface {
        IsMember(ctx context.Context, userID, sessionID string) (bool, error)
}

// SetMembers installs the membership check a user-level stream's deliveries need. Without one a
// user-level stream receives nothing (fail closed).
func (h *Hub) SetMembers(m Members) {
        h.mu.Lock()
        h.members = m
        h.mu.Unlock()
}

// SetPreferences installs the preference store consulted before an addressed event is delivered.
// Without one every addressed event is delivered.
func (h *Hub) SetPreferences(p Preferences) {
        h.mu.Lock()
        h.prefs = p
        h.mu.Unlock()
}

// SetManagers installs the lookup that addresses change-request submissions to the session's GM
// and co-GMs. Without one those submissions notify nobody.
func (h *Hub) SetManagers(m Managers) {
        h.mu.Lock()
        h.managers = m
        h.mu.Unlock()
}

// noticeJob is one notice waiting for the notice worker. resolve, when set, builds the notice (a
// lookup that reads the store) on the worker; local means it came from another pod, so it is
// delivered here and goes no further.
type noticeJob struct {
        n       data.Notice
        resolve func(ctx context.Context) (data.Notice, bool)
        local   bool
}

const (
        noticeQueue   = 1024
        noticeTimeout = 3 * time.Second // bounds every store read one notice makes
)

// enqueueNotice hands j to the notice worker, started on first use. It never blocks: the
// preference, membership and manager reads a notice needs run on the worker under a timeout, so a
// slow store delays notices only, never a write, a request, or the fan-out tail's evictions. A full
// queue, or a hub that has been closed, drops the notice (counted by Dropped, logged). The send
// happens under the read lock, so Close (which takes the write lock) never races a send that would
// then have no consumer.
func (h *Hub) enqueueNotice(j noticeJob) {
        h.noticeOnce.Do(h.startNotices)
        h.mu.RLock()
        defer h.mu.RUnlock()
        if h.noticeClosed || h.noticeQ == nil {
                h.dropNotice(j, "the hub is closed")
                return
        }
        h.noticePending.Add(1)
        select {
        case h.noticeQ <- j:
        default:
                h.noticePending.Done()
                h.dropNotice(j, "the notice queue is full")
        }
}

func (h *Hub) dropNotice(j noticeJob, why string) {
        h.dropped.Add(1)
        slog.Warn("dropping a notification: "+why, "type", j.n.Type, "session", j.n.SessionID)
}

// startNotices starts the worker unless the hub has already been closed, which is final for
// notices.
func (h *Hub) startNotices() {
        h.mu.Lock()
        if h.noticeClosed {
                h.mu.Unlock()
                return
        }
        done := make(chan struct{})
        q := make(chan noticeJob, noticeQueue)
        h.noticeQ, h.noticeDone = q, done
        h.mu.Unlock()
        h.noticeWG.Add(1)
        go func() {
                defer h.noticeWG.Done()
                for {
                        select {
                        case <-done: // a closed hub wins over a waiting job, so what is still queued is the drain's
                                h.drainNotices(q)
                                return
                        default:
                        }
                        select {
                        case j := <-q:
                                h.runNotice(j)
                                h.noticePending.Done()
                        case <-done:
                                h.drainNotices(q)
                                return
                        }
                }
        }()
}

// drainNotices discards what is still queued when the hub closes: counted and logged, not silent.
func (h *Hub) drainNotices(q chan noticeJob) {
        for {
                select {
                case j := <-q:
                        h.dropNotice(j, "the hub closed before it was delivered")
                        h.noticePending.Done()
                default:
                        return
                }
        }
}

func (h *Hub) runNotice(j noticeJob) {
        ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), noticeTimeout)
        defer cancel()
        defer func() {
                if p := recover(); p != nil {
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "delivering a notification panicked", "panic", p)
                }
        }()
        n := j.n
        if j.resolve != nil {
                var ok bool
                if n, ok = j.resolve(ctx); !ok {
                        return
                }
        }
        if n.SessionID == "" || len(n.Users) == 0 {
                return
        }
        muted := h.mutedUsers(n) // one read per addressee, shared by every delivery of this notice
        isMember := h.memberChecker(n)
        h.notifyLocal(n, muted, isMember)
        if j.local {
                return
        }
        if r := h.relay(); r != nil {
                r.publishNotice(n)
        }
        h.publishNoticeToBus(n, muted, isMember)
}

// Flush waits until every notice queued so far has been delivered (tests, and shutdown).
func (h *Hub) Flush() { h.noticePending.Wait() }

// stopNotices ends the notice worker for good: later notices are dropped and counted, and
// Flush returns.
func (h *Hub) stopNotices() {
        h.mu.Lock()
        h.noticeClosed = true
        started := h.noticeDone != nil
        if started {
                close(h.noticeDone) // inside the lock: anyone who sees noticeClosed can rely on done being closed
                h.noticeDone = nil
        }
        h.mu.Unlock()
        if started {
                h.noticeWG.Wait()
        }
}

// Notify implements data.Notifier. It queues the notice; the worker delivers it to each
// addressee's open streams of the notice's session (a per-encounter stream only for that
// encounter), skipping an addressee who muted the type, and relays it to other pods. A stream's
// viewer is fixed when it is granted and ends when membership ends, so only current members of
// the session receive it. It never blocks the caller.
func (h *Hub) Notify(_ context.Context, n data.Notice) {
        if n.SessionID == "" || len(n.Users) == 0 {
                return
        }
        h.enqueueNotice(noticeJob{n: n})
}

// notifyLocal delivers n to this pod's streams only, skipping the addressees in muted.
func (h *Hub) notifyLocal(n data.Notice, muted map[string]bool, isMember func(string) bool) {
        ev := noticeEvent(n, newEventID())
        subs := h.snapshot(n.EncounterID)
        done := map[string]bool{}
        for _, u := range n.Users {
                if u == "" || done[u] || muted[u] {
                        continue
                }
                done[u] = true
                member := func() bool { return isMember(u) }
                for _, s := range subs {
                        if s.addressedTo(u, n.SessionID, member) && s.send(ev) {
                                s.overflowed.Store(true)
                                h.dropped.Add(1)
                                s.Close()
                        }
                }
        }
}

// addressedTo reports whether s may receive a notice for user u in session: a session stream of
// u's in that session, or u's user-level stream when u is still a member (member is lazy).
func (s *Subscription) addressedTo(u, session string, member func() bool) bool {
        if s.userLevel {
                return s.actor == u && member()
        }
        v := s.viewer.Load()
        return v != nil && v.Actor() == u && v.SessionID() == session
}

// memberChecker returns a membership check for n's session that reads each addressee's membership
// at most once, however many deliveries (streams, bus) ask. It is used by one worker job only.
func (h *Hub) memberChecker(n data.Notice) func(string) bool {
        h.mu.RLock()
        members := h.members
        h.mu.RUnlock()
        checks := map[string]func() bool{}
        return func(u string) bool {
                f, ok := checks[u]
                if !ok {
                        f = h.memberOnce(members, u, n.SessionID)
                        checks[u] = f
                }
                return f()
        }
}

// memberOnce returns a check of u's membership in session, read at most once per notice and only
// if a user-level stream needs it. A failed read counts as not a member.
func (h *Hub) memberOnce(m Members, u, session string) func() bool {
        var once sync.Once
        var ok bool
        return func() bool {
                once.Do(func() {
                        if m == nil {
                                return
                        }
                        ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), muteLookupTimeout)
                        defer cancel()
                        var err error
                        if ok, err = m.IsMember(ctx, u, session); err != nil {
                                ok = false
                                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "checking membership for a user-level notice failed; not delivering", "user", u, "error", err)
                        }
                })
                return ok
        }
}

// muteLookupTimeout bounds each addressee's preference or membership read (a variable so a test
// can shorten it).
var muteLookupTimeout = time.Second

// mutedUsers returns the addressees who turned n's type off for its session, with one preference
// read per distinct addressee, each under its own timeout.
//
// A failed read fails open on purpose: a notification is worth more than a missed mute, and a mute
// only chooses what a client shows. It is never silent: the first failure is logged at WARN with
// how many addressees were left unchecked, and the remaining reads are skipped, so a dead store
// costs one timeout per notice, not one per addressee.
func (h *Hub) mutedUsers(n data.Notice) map[string]bool {
        h.mu.RLock()
        prefs := h.prefs
        h.mu.RUnlock()
        if prefs == nil {
                return nil
        }
        muted := map[string]bool{}
        seen := map[string]bool{}
        for i, u := range n.Users {
                if u == "" || seen[u] {
                        continue
                }
                seen[u] = true
                ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), muteLookupTimeout)
                list, err := prefs.Muted(ctx, u, n.SessionID)
                cancel()
                if err != nil {
                        slog.Warn("reading notification preferences failed; delivering without the mutes of the rest",
                                "type", n.Type, "session", n.SessionID, "unchecked", len(n.Users)-i, "error", err)
                        break
                }
                if slices.Contains(list, n.Type) {
                        muted[u] = true
                }
        }
        return muted
}

// noticeEvent is the wire form of n. It has no revision or view tag, which belong to the encounter
// state events and would tell a non-GM about edits it cannot see.
func noticeEvent(n data.Notice, id string) Event {
        d := n.Data
        if d == nil {
                d = map[string]any{}
        }
        return Event{
                EventID: id, OccurredAt: n.At.UTC(), Source: Source,
                EncounterID: n.EncounterID, SessionID: n.SessionID,
                EntityType: "encounter", EntityID: n.EncounterID,
                Type: n.Type, Action: actionOf(n.Type),
                Origin: Origin{Kind: data.OriginSystem}, Data: d,
        }
}

// turnNotices addresses a turn change: the new current combatant's controllers hear turn.yours and
// the next one's hear turn.next. Each carries only the combatant's id and name, which its
// controller always sees.
func turnNotices(c data.Change) []data.Notice {
        e := c.After
        if e.SessionID == "" || (c.Type != data.EventTurn && c.Type != data.EventStarted) || e.CurrentCombatantID == "" {
                return nil
        }
        var out []data.Notice
        add := func(kind, id string) {
                cb, ok := combatantOf(e, id)
                if !ok || cb.Removed || len(cb.ControllerUserIDs) == 0 {
                        return
                }
                out = append(out, data.Notice{
                        Type: kind, SessionID: e.SessionID, EncounterID: e.ID, Users: slices.Clone(cb.ControllerUserIDs), At: c.At,
                        Data: map[string]any{"combatant_id": cb.ID, "combatant_name": cb.Name, "round": e.Round},
                })
        }
        add(data.NoticeTurnYours, e.CurrentCombatantID)
        if i := slices.Index(e.TurnQueue, e.CurrentCombatantID); i >= 0 && len(e.TurnQueue) > 1 {
                add(data.NoticeTurnNext, e.TurnQueue[(i+1)%len(e.TurnQueue)])
        }
        return out
}

func combatantOf(e data.Encounter, id string) (data.Combatant, bool) {
        i := slices.IndexFunc(e.Combatants, func(c data.Combatant) bool { return c.ID == id })
        if i < 0 {
                return data.Combatant{}, false
        }
        return e.Combatants[i], true
}

// ChangeSubmitted tells the session's GM and co-GMs a player filed a change request.
func (h *Hub) ChangeSubmitted(_ context.Context, req data.ChangeRequest) {
        h.mu.RLock()
        m := h.managers
        h.mu.RUnlock()
        if m == nil {
                return
        }
        h.enqueueNotice(noticeJob{n: data.Notice{Type: data.NoticeChangeSubmitted, SessionID: req.SessionID}, resolve: func(ctx context.Context) (data.Notice, bool) {
                ids, err := m.ManagerIDs(ctx, req.SessionID)
                if err != nil {
                        slog.WarnContext(ctx, "finding the session's managers failed; the change request notice was not sent", "session", req.SessionID, "error", err)
                        return data.Notice{}, false
                }
                return requestNotice(data.NoticeChangeSubmitted, req, ids, req.CreatedAt), true
        }})
}

// ChangeDecided tells the player who filed the request that it was approved or rejected.
func (h *Hub) ChangeDecided(ctx context.Context, req data.ChangeRequest) {
        kind := data.NoticeChangeRejected
        if req.Status == data.ChangeApproved {
                kind = data.NoticeChangeApproved
        }
        at := req.CreatedAt
        if req.DecidedAt != nil {
                at = *req.DecidedAt
        }
