        h.Notify(ctx, requestNotice(kind, req, []string{req.RequestedBy}, at))
}

// requestNotice carries the ids of a change request and the decision, never the proposed values.
func requestNotice(kind string, req data.ChangeRequest, users []string, at time.Time) data.Notice {
        return data.Notice{
                Type: kind, SessionID: req.SessionID, EncounterID: req.EncounterID, Users: users, At: at,
                Data: map[string]any{"change_request_id": req.ID, "combatant_id": req.CombatantID},
        }
}

package stream

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "log/slog"
        "sync"
        "sync/atomic"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson/primitive"
)

// FanoutCollection is the collection pods share to reach each other's subscribers.
const FanoutCollection = "stream_fanout"

// FanoutTTL is how long a fan-out row is meant to live. The TTL monitor removes expired rows about
// once a minute, so a row can outlive it by up to two minutes in all. A pod that is down longer than
// that misses what it would have relayed; its tail then restarts without a position and ends every
// local stream (see Relay). Changing it makes the index creation conflict with the existing one, so
// startup fails until the index is dropped.
const FanoutTTL = 60 * time.Second

// FanoutIndexes is the TTL index of the fan-out collection.
var FanoutIndexes = []store.IndexSpec{{Name: "at_ttl", Keys: bson.D{{Key: "at", Value: 1}}, ExpireAfter: FanoutTTL}}

const (
        kindChange = "change"
        kindEvict  = "evict"
        kindNotice = "notice"
)

// fanoutDoc is one row of the fan-out collection. A change row carries the data.Change the
// encounter collection already holds the content of (the encounter document, as stored); an evict
// row carries only the session and user.
type fanoutDoc struct {
        ID      primitive.ObjectID `bson:"_id"`
        Pod     string             `bson:"pod"`
        Kind    string             `bson:"kind"`
        At      time.Time          `bson:"at"`
        EventID string             `bson:"event_id,omitempty"`
        Change  *data.Change       `bson:"change,omitempty"`
        Notice  *data.Notice       `bson:"notice,omitempty"`
        Session string             `bson:"session_id,omitempty"`
        User    string             `bson:"user_id,omitempty"`
}

// Relay carries committed changes and evictions between pods through a Mongo collection: each
// commit on this pod is inserted, and every pod tails the collection with a change stream and
// delivers what other pods wrote to its own subscribers. Delivery is fail-open: when the stream
// cannot be tailed the pod keeps serving its own subscribers, logs loudly, counts the failure
// (Failures) and reports Degraded.
//
// Revocation is never silent. Evictions have their own queue and writer, so a slow insert of
// changes never delays one; each insert has a timeout and a failed one is retried for up to
// evictWindow, then logged at error level and counted (EvictFailures). The tail resumes from its
// last token after a transient error; only when the server says the history is gone (or the tail
// never had a position) does it restart from now, and then it ends every local subscription
// (GapEvictions), because evictions written in the gap are unknown and each client must reconnect
// and be authorized again. A change row dropped because this pod's remote queue is full ends that
// change's audience here for the same reason. Rows lost on the writing side (a full change queue,
// or an insert that failed through its window) cannot be recovered by the other pods, which learn
// of the gap only from clients refetching. The final bound for anything lost beyond that is each stream's membership re-check
// (server.StreamOptions.RevocationBound, 7s by default), and the stream's maximum age behind it.
type Relay struct {
        h   *Hub
        col store.Collection
        pod string

        out    chan fanoutDoc // changes to insert
        evicts chan fanoutDoc // evictions to insert, on their own writer
        in     chan fanoutDoc // change rows from other pods, rendered off the tail goroutine
        done   chan struct{}
        once   sync.Once
        ran    atomic.Bool
        flight sync.WaitGroup // evictions queued or being inserted
        wg     sync.WaitGroup

        failures      atomic.Int64 // tail errors, ever
        streak        atomic.Int64 // tail errors since a row last arrived; only the first logs at error level
        dropped       atomic.Int64 // changes not relayed or not delivered (queue full or insert failed)
        evictFailures atomic.Int64 // evictions that could not be relayed
        gapEvictions  atomic.Int64 // times the tail restarted without a position and ended every stream
        degraded      atomic.Bool
        tailing       atomic.Bool // a change stream is open
}

const (
        insertTimeout = 5 * time.Second
        changeWindow  = 2 * time.Second  // retry window for a change row
        evictWindow   = 30 * time.Second // retry window for an eviction row
        relayBackoff  = 200 * time.Millisecond
        relayMaxWait  = 30 * time.Second
        drainWait     = 2 * time.Second // Stop's wait for queued evictions
)

// AttachRelay makes h relay through col, with pod naming this pod (a row a pod wrote is skipped by
// that pod, so the name must be unique per process). queue bounds the rows waiting to be inserted.
// Run must be called to start tailing; Stop ends both.
func (h *Hub) AttachRelay(col store.Collection, pod string, queue int) *Relay {
        q := max(queue, 1)
        r := &Relay{h: h, col: col, pod: pod, done: make(chan struct{}),
                out: make(chan fanoutDoc, q), evicts: make(chan fanoutDoc, q), in: make(chan fanoutDoc, q)}
        h.mu.Lock()
        h.rel = r
        h.mu.Unlock()
        return r
}

func (h *Hub) relay() *Relay {
        h.mu.RLock()
        defer h.mu.RUnlock()
        return h.rel
}

// Failures counts the times tailing failed; Dropped the changes not relayed; EvictFailures the
// evictions not relayed to other pods; GapEvictions the times every local stream was ended after a
// gap in the tail. Degraded reports that the tail is down right now.
func (r *Relay) Failures() int64      { return r.failures.Load() }
func (r *Relay) Dropped() int64       { return r.dropped.Load() }
func (r *Relay) EvictFailures() int64 { return r.evictFailures.Load() }
func (r *Relay) GapEvictions() int64  { return r.gapEvictions.Load() }
func (r *Relay) Degraded() bool       { return r.degraded.Load() }

// Tailing reports that the change stream is open, so rows other pods write now will arrive.
func (r *Relay) Tailing() bool { return r.tailing.Load() }

// Run starts the writers, the delivery loop and the tail. It returns at once; Stop ends them.
func (r *Relay) Run(ctx context.Context) {
        r.ran.Store(true)
        ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(ctx)
        r.wg.Add(1)
        go func() { defer r.wg.Done(); <-r.done; cancel() }()
        for _, f := range []func(context.Context){r.writeChanges, r.writeEvicts, r.deliverRemote, r.tail} {
                r.wg.Add(1)
                go func() { defer r.wg.Done(); f(ctx) }()
        }
}

// Stop ends the relay's goroutines and waits for them. Evictions still queued get drainWait to be
// inserted first, so a shutdown does not lose a revocation.
func (r *Relay) Stop() {
        r.once.Do(func() {
                if r.ran.Load() {
                        // Wait for every eviction queued or mid-insert, bounded by drainWait.
                        drained := make(chan struct{})
                        go func() { r.flight.Wait(); close(drained) }()
                        select {
                        case <-drained:
                        case <-time.After(drainWait):
                        }
                }
                close(r.done)
        })
        r.wg.Wait()
}

func (r *Relay) publishChange(c data.Change, eventID string) {
        d := fanoutDoc{ID: primitive.NewObjectID(), Pod: r.pod, Kind: kindChange, At: time.Now(), EventID: eventID, Change: &c}
        select {
        case r.out <- d:
        default:
                r.dropped.Add(1)
                slog.Warn("the fan-out queue is full; other pods miss an encounter event", "encounter", c.After.ID, "type", c.Type)
        }
}

// publishNotice carries an addressed event to the other pods, whose streams may hold its
// addressees. Like a change it is dropped, with a log line, when the queue is full.
func (r *Relay) publishNotice(n data.Notice) {
        d := fanoutDoc{ID: primitive.NewObjectID(), Pod: r.pod, Kind: kindNotice, At: time.Now(), Notice: &n}
        select {
        case r.out <- d:
        default:
                r.dropped.Add(1)
                slog.Warn("the fan-out queue is full; other pods miss a notification", "type", n.Type, "session", n.SessionID)
        }
}

func (r *Relay) publishEvict(session, user string) {
        d := fanoutDoc{ID: primitive.NewObjectID(), Pod: r.pod, Kind: kindEvict, At: time.Now(), Session: session, User: user}
        r.flight.Add(1)
        select {
        case r.evicts <- d:
        default:
                r.flight.Done()
                r.evictFailures.Add(1)
                slog.Error("the eviction queue is full; an eviction did not reach other pods, which end the stream on their own timers",
                        "session", session, "user", user)
        }
}

func (r *Relay) writeChanges(ctx context.Context) { r.writeFrom(ctx, r.out, changeWindow, nil) }
func (r *Relay) writeEvicts(ctx context.Context) {
        r.writeFrom(ctx, r.evicts, evictWindow, r.flight.Done)
}

func (r *Relay) writeFrom(ctx context.Context, q <-chan fanoutDoc, window time.Duration, done func()) {
        for {
                select {
                case d := <-q:
                        r.insert(ctx, d, window)
                        if done != nil {
                                done()
                        }
                case <-ctx.Done():
                        return
                }
        }
}

// insert writes d, each attempt under insertTimeout, retrying with backoff while window lasts. It
// does not sleep after the last attempt.
func (r *Relay) insert(ctx context.Context, d fanoutDoc, window time.Duration) {
        start, wait := time.Now(), relayBackoff
        var err error
        for {
                ictx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, insertTimeout)
                err = r.col.InsertOne(ictx, d)
                cancel()
                if err == nil {
                        return
                }
                if ctx.Err() != nil || time.Since(start)+wait > window {
                        break
                }
                select {
                case <-time.After(wait):
                case <-ctx.Done():
                }
                wait = min(wait*2, 2*time.Second)
        }
        if d.Kind == kindEvict {
                r.evictFailures.Add(1)
                slog.Error("an eviction did not reach other pods, which end the stream on their own timers",
                        "session", d.Session, "user", d.User, "error", err)
                return
        }
        r.dropped.Add(1)
        slog.Warn("relaying an encounter event to other pods failed", "type", d.Change.Type, "error", err)
}

// tail follows the collection until ctx ends. After a transient error it resumes from its last
// token. A token the server cannot resume (store.ErrHistoryLost) is dropped, and whenever the tail
// opens without a position after a failure, every local subscription ends (see Relay).
func (r *Relay) tail(ctx context.Context) {
        var token bson.Raw
        failed := false
        wait := time.Second
        for ctx.Err() == nil {
                cur, err := store.Watch(ctx, r.col, token)
                if err != nil {
                        failed = true
                        r.fail(ctx, "cannot watch the fan-out collection; this pod delivers local events only", err)
                        if errors.Is(err, store.ErrHistoryLost) {
                                token = nil
                        }
                        wait = r.sleep(ctx, wait)
                        continue
                }
                if token == nil && failed {
                        n := r.h.evictAll()
                        r.gapEvictions.Add(1)
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "the fan-out tail restarted without a position; every stream on this pod was ended", "streams", n)
                }
                // The position at open, so a failure before the first row still resumes from here.
                if t := cur.Token(); t != nil && token == nil {
                        token = t
                }
                r.degraded.Store(false)
                r.tailing.Store(true)
                for cur.Next(ctx) {
                        wait = time.Second
                        r.streak.Store(0)
                        token = cur.Token()
                        r.handle(ctx, cur.Doc())
                }
                r.tailing.Store(false)
                err = cur.Err()
                _ = cur.Close(context.WithoutCancel(ctx))
                if ctx.Err() != nil {
                        return
                }
                failed = true
                if errors.Is(err, store.ErrHistoryLost) {
                        token = nil
                }
                r.fail(ctx, "the fan-out change stream ended; resuming", err)
                wait = r.sleep(ctx, wait)
        }
}

func (r *Relay) fail(ctx context.Context, msg string, err error) {
        r.failures.Add(1)
        n := r.streak.Add(1)
        r.degraded.Store(true)
        if n > 1 && ctx.Err() == nil {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, msg, "error", err, "failures", n) // repeated: a standalone server logs once at error level
                return
        }
        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, msg, "error", err, "failures", n)
}

// sleep waits out the backoff and returns the next one.
func (r *Relay) sleep(ctx context.Context, d time.Duration) time.Duration {
        select {
        case <-time.After(d):
        case <-ctx.Done():
        }
        return min(d*2, relayMaxWait)
}

// handle routes a row from the tail. An eviction is applied here at once; a change row is queued for
// deliverRemote, so rendering never delays the next eviction row.
func (r *Relay) handle(ctx context.Context, raw bson.Raw) {
        defer func() {
                if p := recover(); p != nil {
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "handling a fan-out row panicked", "panic", p)
                }
        }()
        var d fanoutDoc
        if err := bson.Unmarshal(raw, &d); err != nil {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "a fan-out row could not be read", "error", err)
                return
        }
        if d.Pod == r.pod {
                return
        }
        switch {
        case d.Kind == kindEvict && d.Session == "" && d.User != "":
                r.h.evictUserLocal(d.User) // a platform-wide eviction: every stream of that user
        case d.Kind == kindEvict && d.Session == "":
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "dropped a fan-out eviction with no session and no user")
        case d.Kind == kindEvict:
                r.h.evictLocal(d.Session, d.User)
        case d.Kind == kindNotice && d.Notice != nil:
                r.h.enqueueNotice(noticeJob{n: *d.Notice, local: true}) // preferences are read on the worker, never on the tail
        case d.Kind == kindChange && d.Change != nil:
                select {
                case r.in <- d:
                default:
                        // A view_tag-suppressed stream hides a gap, so the audience must reconnect and refetch.
                        r.dropped.Add(1)
                        r.h.evictAudience(*d.Change)
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "the remote delivery queue is full; the change's audience on this pod was disconnected to refetch")
                }
        }
}

func (r *Relay) deliverRemote(ctx context.Context) {
        for {
                select {
                case d := <-r.in:
                        r.deliverOne(ctx, d)
                case <-ctx.Done():
                        return
                }
        }
}

func (r *Relay) deliverOne(ctx context.Context, d fanoutDoc) {
        defer func() {
                if p := recover(); p != nil {
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "delivering a fan-out row panicked", "panic", p)
                }
        }()
        r.h.emitRemote(ctx, *d.Change, d.EventID)
}

package reconcile

import (
        "bytes"
        "context"
        "encoding/json"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "io"
        "net/http"
        "net/url"
        "time"
)

const (
        readPage    = 100
        callTimeout = 10 * time.Second
        maxBody     = 1 << 20
)

// AuthAPI calls auth-api's internal tuple endpoints with the reconciler's own bearer
// (PADR-0037, PADR-0038). No service token is involved.
type AuthAPI struct {
        Base   string
        Tokens TokenSource
        HTTP   *http.Client
}

// NewAuthAPI returns a client for base using one fixed token.
func NewAuthAPI(base, token string) *AuthAPI { return NewAuthAPIWithSource(base, StaticToken(token)) }

// NewAuthAPIWithSource returns a client for base that takes its bearer from tokens.
func NewAuthAPIWithSource(base string, tokens TokenSource) *AuthAPI {
        return &AuthAPI{Base: base, Tokens: tokens, HTTP: &http.Client{Timeout: callTimeout}}
}

// Read returns every tuple on object, following continuation tokens.
func (a *AuthAPI) Read(ctx context.Context, object string) ([]Tuple, error) {
        var all []Tuple
        token := ""
        for {
                q := url.Values{"object": {object}, "page_size": {fmt.Sprint(readPage)}}
                if token != "" {
                        q.Set("continuation_token", token)
                }
                var page struct {
                        Tuples []Tuple `json:"tuples"`
                        Next   string  `json:"continuation_token"`
                }
                if err := a.do(ctx, http.MethodGet, "/internal/openfga/tuples?"+q.Encode(), nil, &page); err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                all = append(all, page.Tuples...)
                if page.Next == "" {
                        return all, nil
                }
                token = page.Next
        }
}

// Write applies one batch (at most 100 tuples in total) atomically.
func (a *AuthAPI) Write(ctx context.Context, writes, deletes []Tuple) error {
        body, err := json.Marshal(map[string][]Tuple{"writes": writes, "deletes": deletes})
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        return a.do(ctx, http.MethodPost, "/internal/openfga/tuples", body, nil)
}

// do sends one request. A 401 on a cached token refreshes it and retries once. A 401 on a token
// fetched in this run, or a second 401, is ErrTokenRejected and tells the source to stop later runs
// calling Auth0. A 403 is not retried.
func (a *AuthAPI) do(ctx context.Context, method, path string, body []byte, out any) error {
        token, err := a.Tokens.Token(ctx)
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("reconciler token: %w", err)
        }
        status, err := a.send(ctx, method, path, body, token, out)
        if status == http.StatusUnauthorized {
                fresh, rerr := a.Tokens.Refresh(ctx)
                switch {
                case errors.Is(rerr, ErrNoRefresh):
                        return a.rejected(ctx)
                case rerr != nil:
                        return fmt.Errorf("reconciler token: %w", rerr)
                }
                if status, err = a.send(ctx, method, path, body, fresh, out); status == http.StatusUnauthorized {
                        return a.rejected(ctx)
                }
        }
        if err == nil {
                a.Tokens.Accepted(ctx)
        }
        return err
}

// rejected records the rejection and returns ErrTokenRejected, noting when the record failed.
func (a *AuthAPI) rejected(ctx context.Context) error {
        if err := a.Tokens.Rejected(ctx); err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("%w (%v)", ErrTokenRejected, err)
        }
        return ErrTokenRejected
}

// send makes one call and returns the HTTP status (0 when none arrived) with its error.
func (a *AuthAPI) send(ctx context.Context, method, path string, body []byte, token string, out any) (int, error) {
        req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, method, a.Base+path, bytes.NewReader(body))
        if err != nil {
                return 0, err
        }
        req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+token)
        req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
        resp, err := a.HTTP.Do(req)
        if err != nil {
                return 0, fmt.Errorf("auth-api %s: %w", path, err)
        }
        defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
        switch {
        case resp.StatusCode == http.StatusUnauthorized || resp.StatusCode == http.StatusForbidden:
                return resp.StatusCode, ErrTokenRejected
        case resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK && resp.StatusCode != http.StatusNoContent:
                return resp.StatusCode, fmt.Errorf("auth-api %s: status %d", path, resp.StatusCode)
        case out == nil:
                return resp.StatusCode, nil
        }
        return resp.StatusCode, json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, maxBody)).Decode(out)
}

// ListMints returns one page of open mints at least minAge old (by auth-api's clock) after the
// cursor, and the cursor for the next page, empty on the last.
func (a *AuthAPI) ListMints(ctx context.Context, minAge time.Duration, after string) ([]Mint, string, error) {
        q := url.Values{"min_age": {minAge.String()}, "limit": {"200"}}
        if after != "" {
                q.Set("after", after)
        }
        var page struct {
                Mints []Mint `json:"mints"`
                Next  string `json:"next"`
        }
        if err := a.do(ctx, http.MethodGet, "/internal/openfga/session-mints?"+q.Encode(), nil, &page); err != nil {
                return nil, "", err
        }
        return page.Mints, page.Next, nil
}

// CloseMint deletes every tuple on the minted session and closes its mint.
func (a *AuthAPI) CloseMint(ctx context.Context, id string) error {
        return a.do(ctx, http.MethodPost, "/internal/openfga/session-mints/"+url.PathEscape(id)+"/close", nil, nil)
}

// Package reconcile rebuilds the OpenFGA session tuples from the membership collection, which is
// the source of truth (PADR-0035). It runs as the initiative-reconciler user with the
// openfga-writer role and an operator-minted bearer (PADR-0038). Every pass is idempotent: it
// reads the tuples on each session, then that session's current memberships, diffs them, and
// applies the difference, so a failed pass is finished by the next one. Reading tuples first means
// a grant or revoke that lands between the two reads is, at worst, corrected by the next pass
// (two narrow interleavings remain, accepted by PADR-0040 section 4). After the tuple pass it
// collects session mints that never became sessions (PADR-0040 section 1).
package reconcile

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "log/slog"
        "regexp"
        "sort"
        "strings"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

const (
        scanPage   = 500
        maxBatch   = 100 // auth-api's tuple write limit
        userPrefix = "user:"
        sessionObj = "session:"
        // mintMinAge keeps garbage collection away from sessions still being created; auth-api
        // compares it against its own clock.
        mintMinAge = 15 * time.Minute
        // maxMintPages bounds the garbage-collection paging loop so a cursor that never ends cannot
        // hold the job until its deadline.
        maxMintPages = 1000
        // defaultGCReserve is the end of the pass's time budget kept for garbage collection, so a slow
        // tuple pass cannot starve it.
        defaultGCReserve = 60 * time.Second
        // maxGMLessPerPass is the most GM-less sessions one pass will remove. More than that looks like
        // a wrong database or an empty members collection, which would otherwise wipe every session
        // older than mintMinAge and its tuples.
        maxGMLessPerPass = 10
)

// mintIDPattern is what auth-api mints. Anything else matches no session, which would read as
// "missing" and close a mint that is not ours to judge.
var mintIDPattern = regexp.MustCompile(`^[0-9a-f]{24}$`)

// ErrTokenRejected means auth-api refused the reconciler's bearer (expired, revoked, or missing
// the openfga-writer role). The pass stops at once; an operator must mint a new token.
var ErrTokenRejected = errors.New("reconcile: reconciler token rejected")

// Tuple is one relationship, as auth-api's tuple endpoints carry it.
type Tuple struct {
        User     string `json:"user"`
        Relation string `json:"relation"`
        Object   string `json:"object"`
}

// Members is the membership source.
type Members interface {
        // ScanMembers pages through every membership record, deleted ones included. The pass uses it
        // only to learn which sessions exist.
        ScanMembers(ctx context.Context, q data.Query) (data.Page[data.Member], error)
        // SessionMembers returns one session's membership records, deleted ones included, read fresh.
        SessionMembers(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) ([]data.Member, error)
}

// Mint is an open session mint auth-api lists for garbage collection.
type Mint struct {
        ID string `json:"id"`
}

// Mints is auth-api's mint surface (openfga-writer): open mints at least minAge old, paged by
// the cursor each page returns, and a close that deletes every tuple on the session.
type Mints interface {
        ListMints(ctx context.Context, minAge time.Duration, after string) (mints []Mint, next string, err error)
        CloseMint(ctx context.Context, id string) error
}

// Sessions looks sessions up by ID in the sessions collection, soft-deleted ones included.
type Sessions interface {
        // LookupSessions maps each found ID to whether it is soft-deleted. A missing ID is absent.
        LookupSessions(ctx context.Context, ids []string) (map[string]bool, error)
        // SessionsWithGM returns the IDs among ids that have a live GM member record.
        SessionsWithGM(ctx context.Context, ids []string) (map[string]bool, error)
        // RemoveGMLess soft-deletes a live session that has no GM, and its membership records.
        RemoveGMLess(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) error
}

// Tuples is auth-api's internal tuple surface.
type Tuples interface {
        // Read returns every tuple on object.
        Read(ctx context.Context, object string) ([]Tuple, error)
        Write(ctx context.Context, writes, deletes []Tuple) error
}

// Reconciler diffs memberships against tuples.
type Reconciler struct {
        Members Members
        Tuples  Tuples
        // Mints and Sessions enable the mint garbage collection; both nil skips it.
        Mints    Mints
        Sessions Sessions
        // GCReserve is how much of ctx's deadline the tuple pass leaves for garbage collection; zero
        // means 60 seconds. It has no effect when ctx has no deadline.
        GCReserve time.Duration
}

// Result counts one pass.
type Result struct {
        Sessions, Written, Deleted, Failed int
        // MintsClosed counts mints garbage collection closed; MintsSkipped counts those it left for
        // the next pass because the sessions lookup failed.
        MintsClosed, MintsSkipped int
        // GMLessRemoved counts live sessions with no GM that garbage collection soft-deleted.
        GMLessRemoved int
        // GMLessCapped is set when a pass reached maxGMLessPerPass and left further GM-less sessions.
        GMLessCapped bool
}

var roleRelations = map[string]bool{data.RoleGM: true, data.RoleCoGM: true, data.RolePlayer: true, data.RoleObserver: true}

// sessionObjects lists the session objects that have any membership record, live or deleted, so a
// session whose members are all gone is still visited and its tuples removed.
func (r Reconciler) sessionObjects(ctx context.Context) ([]string, error) {
        seen := map[string]bool{}
        cursor := ""
        for {
                page, err := r.Members.ScanMembers(ctx, data.Query{Limit: scanPage, Cursor: cursor})
                if err != nil {
                        return nil, fmt.Errorf("scan members: %w", err)
                }
                for _, m := range page.Items {
                        seen[m.SessionID] = true
                }
                if page.NextCursor == "" {
                        break
                }
                cursor = page.NextCursor
        }
        ids := make([]string, 0, len(seen))
        for id := range seen {
                ids = append(ids, id)
        }
        sort.Strings(ids)
        return ids, nil
}

// wanted is the tuple set one session's live memberships call for.
func wanted(sessionID string, members []data.Member) map[Tuple]bool {
        obj := sessionObj + sessionID
        out := map[Tuple]bool{}
        for _, m := range members {
                if m.DeletedAt != nil || !roleRelations[m.Role] {
                        continue
                }
                out[Tuple{User: userPrefix + m.UserID, Relation: m.Role, Object: obj}] = true
        }
        return out
}

// Run does one pass. It returns ErrTokenRejected if auth-api refuses the token; other failures
// are logged per session and counted, and returned together after every session was tried.
func (r Reconciler) Run(ctx context.Context) (Result, error) {
        tupleCtx, cancel := r.tuplePassContext(ctx)
        defer cancel()
        ids, err := r.sessionObjects(tupleCtx)
        if err != nil {
                return Result{}, err
        }
        res := Result{Sessions: len(ids)}
        for i, id := range ids {
                if tupleCtx.Err() != nil {
                        // The tuple pass's budget is spent: one line and one failure per session left, so the
                        // job fails and garbage collection (on the full context) still runs.
                        res.Failed += len(ids) - i
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: tuple pass ran out of time", "done", i, "left", len(ids)-i)
                        break
                }
                w, d, err := r.one(tupleCtx, id)
                res.Written += w
                res.Deleted += d
                if errors.Is(err, ErrTokenRejected) {
                        return res, err
                }
                if err != nil {
                        res.Failed++
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile session failed", "session", id, "error", err)
                }
        }
        // Garbage collection runs on the whole context, not the tuple pass's shorter one.
        if err := r.collect(ctx, &res); err != nil {
                return res, err
        }
        if res.Failed > 0 {
                return res, fmt.Errorf("reconcile: %d failures across %d sessions", res.Failed, res.Sessions)
        }
        return res, nil
}

// tuplePassContext is ctx with its deadline moved earlier by the GC reserve, so the tuple pass
// cannot use the budget garbage collection needs.
func (r Reconciler) tuplePassContext(ctx context.Context) (context.Context, context.CancelFunc) {
        deadline, ok := ctx.Deadline()
        if !ok {
                return context.WithCancel(ctx)
        }
        reserve := r.GCReserve
        if reserve <= 0 {
                reserve = defaultGCReserve
        }
        return context.WithDeadline(ctx, deadline.Add(-reserve))
}

// collect closes mints that never became live sessions: for each open mint at least mintMinAge
// old, one sessions lookup that includes soft-deleted records. A mint is closed only when that
// lookup succeeds and returns no record or a deleted one. A failed lookup skips the whole batch
// until the next pass. It pages to the end, because live sessions stay open and would otherwise
// fill the first page and hide a leaked mint.
func (r Reconciler) collect(ctx context.Context, res *Result) error {
        if r.Mints == nil || r.Sessions == nil {
                return nil
        }
        after := ""
        for page := 0; ; page++ {
                if page >= maxMintPages {
                        res.Failed++
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: mint paging did not end", "pages", page)
                        return nil
                }
                mints, next, err := r.Mints.ListMints(ctx, mintMinAge, after)
                if err != nil {
                        if errors.Is(err, ErrTokenRejected) {
                                return err
                        }
                        res.Failed++
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: listing session mints failed", "error", err)
                        return nil
                }
                if err := r.closeOrphans(ctx, mints, res); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                if next == "" {
                        return nil
                }
                if next == after {
                        res.Failed++
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: auth-api returned the same mint cursor twice", "cursor", next)
                        return nil
                }
                after = next
        }
}

func (r Reconciler) closeOrphans(ctx context.Context, mints []Mint, res *Result) error {
        if len(mints) == 0 {
                return nil
        }
        ids := make([]string, 0, len(mints))
        for _, m := range mints {
                if !mintIDPattern.MatchString(m.ID) {
                        res.MintsSkipped++
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: ignoring a mint with a malformed id", "mint", m.ID)
                        continue
                }
                ids = append(ids, m.ID)
        }
        if len(ids) == 0 {
                return nil
        }
        found, err := r.Sessions.LookupSessions(ctx, ids)
        if err != nil {
                // Counted as a failure too: a Mongo outage that persists must fail the job so the stall
                // alert fires, not exit 0 with the mints left open.
                res.MintsSkipped += len(ids)
                res.Failed++
                slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: sessions lookup failed; leaving this batch of mints for the next pass", "mints", len(ids), "error", err)
                return nil
        }
        gmless := r.gmLess(ctx, ids, found, res)
        for _, id := range ids {
                if deleted, exists := found[id]; exists && !deleted {
                        if !gmless[id] {
                                continue // a live session with its GM
                        }
                        if res.GMLessRemoved >= maxGMLessPerPass {
                                if !res.GMLessCapped {
                                        res.GMLessCapped = true
                                        res.Failed++
                                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: reached the cap of sessions with no GM removed in one pass; leaving the rest", "cap", maxGMLessPerPass)
                                }
                                continue
                        }
                        err := r.Sessions.RemoveGMLess(ctx, id)
                        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrConflict) {
                                slog.InfoContext(ctx, "reconcile: session has a GM or is too new to remove; leaving it", "session", id)
                                continue
                        }
                        if err != nil {
                                res.Failed++
                                slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: removing a session with no GM failed", "session", id, "error", err)
                                continue
                        }
                        res.GMLessRemoved++
                        slog.InfoContext(ctx, "reconcile: removed a live session that has no GM", "session", id)
                }
                if err := r.Mints.CloseMint(ctx, id); err != nil {
                        if errors.Is(err, ErrTokenRejected) {
                                return err
                        }
                        res.Failed++
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: closing a mint failed", "mint", id, "error", err)
                        continue
                }
                res.MintsClosed++
                slog.InfoContext(ctx, "reconcile: closed a mint with no live session", "mint", id)
        }
        return nil
}

// gmLess returns which of the live sessions among ids have no live GM record, using one query. A
// failed query counts as a failure and returns nothing, so no session is removed on a guess.
func (r Reconciler) gmLess(ctx context.Context, ids []string, found map[string]bool, res *Result) map[string]bool {
        var live []string
        for _, id := range ids {
                if deleted, exists := found[id]; exists && !deleted {
                        live = append(live, id)
                }
        }
        if len(live) == 0 {
                return nil
        }
        withGM, err := r.Sessions.SessionsWithGM(ctx, live)
        if err != nil {
                res.Failed++
                slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: GM lookup failed; leaving live sessions alone", "sessions", len(live), "error", err)
                return nil
        }
        out := map[string]bool{}
        for _, id := range live {
                if !withGM[id] {
                        out[id] = true
                }
        }
        return out
}

// one reconciles a single session and returns how many tuples it wrote and deleted. It reads the
// tuples first and the session's members second.
func (r Reconciler) one(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) (written, deleted int, err error) {
        obj := sessionObj + sessionID
        have, err := r.Tuples.Read(ctx, obj)
        if err != nil {
                return 0, 0, err
        }
        members, err := r.Members.SessionMembers(ctx, sessionID)
        if err != nil {
                return 0, 0, fmt.Errorf("read members: %w", err)
        }
        want := wanted(sessionID, members)
        haveSet := map[Tuple]bool{}
        var dels []Tuple
        for _, t := range have {
                if !roleRelations[t.Relation] || !strings.HasPrefix(t.User, userPrefix) {
                        continue // not a tuple this job owns
                }
                haveSet[t] = true
                if !want[t] {
                        dels = append(dels, t)
                }
        }
        var adds []Tuple
        for t := range want {
                if !haveSet[t] {
                        adds = append(adds, t)
                }
        }
        sortTuples(adds)
        sortTuples(dels)
        for _, t := range adds {
                slog.InfoContext(ctx, "reconcile: writing missing tuple", "user", t.User, "relation", t.Relation, "object", t.Object)
        }
        for _, t := range dels {
                slog.InfoContext(ctx, "reconcile: deleting extra tuple", "user", t.User, "relation", t.Relation, "object", t.Object)
        }
        // Deletes go first so a role change (old tuple out, new in) never leaves two roles at once.
        for len(dels) > 0 || len(adds) > 0 {
                var bd, ba []Tuple
                bd, dels = take(dels, maxBatch)
                ba, adds = take(adds, maxBatch-len(bd))
                if err := r.Tuples.Write(ctx, ba, bd); err != nil {
                        return written, deleted, err
                }
                written += len(ba)
                deleted += len(bd)
        }
        return written, deleted, nil
}

func take(ts []Tuple, n int) (head, rest []Tuple) {
        if n > len(ts) {
                n = len(ts)
        }
        return ts[:n], ts[n:]
}

func sortTuples(ts []Tuple) {
        sort.Slice(ts, func(i, j int) bool {
                a, b := ts[i], ts[j]
                return a.Object+a.Relation+a.User < b.Object+b.Relation+b.User
        })
}

package reconcile

import (
        "bytes"
        "context"
        "crypto/tls"
        "encoding/json"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "io"
        "log/slog"
        "net"
        "net/http"
        "strconv"
        "strings"
        "syscall"
        "time"

        "github.com/gomodule/redigo/redis"
)

const (
        // tokenCacheKey holds the cached Auth0 access token (PADR-0041).
        tokenCacheKey = "reconciler:token"
        // tokenSkew is how long before expiry a token stops being served from the cache.
        tokenSkew = 120 * time.Second
        // rejectedKey marks that auth-api rejected a freshly fetched token. While it exists no run
        // calls Auth0, so a persistent 401 (role removed, broken login mapping, wrong audience) cannot
        // spend the monthly token allowance. Delete it to retry sooner.
        rejectedKey = "reconciler:rejected"
        rejectedTTL = time.Hour
        // fetchGuardKey is set (NX, 60s) before every Auth0 call. A Redis that cannot take writes then
        // stops the run before Auth0 is called, and a working one limits fetches to one a minute, so a
        // cache that silently fails to store cannot cost a token per run.
        fetchGuardKey = "reconciler:fetch-guard"
        fetchGuardTTL = 60 * time.Second
        // auth0FailuresKey counts consecutive failed token fetches (TTL 2h, reset on success) and
        // auth0BackoffKey blocks fetching while it exists, for 5m * 2^(failures-1) capped at 1h. After it
        // expires the next fetch is the probe: success clears both keys, failure re-arms the backoff.
        // A cached token is still served during backoff; only Auth0 calls stop.
        auth0FailuresKey = "reconciler:auth0-failures"
        auth0BackoffKey  = "reconciler:auth0-backoff"
        failuresTTL      = 2 * time.Hour
        backoffBase      = 5 * time.Minute
        backoffMax       = time.Hour
        // maxLifetime caps a lifetime Auth0 reports, so a bad value cannot cache a token for years.
        maxLifetime = 30 * 24 * time.Hour
)

// TokenSource supplies the bearer the reconciler sends to auth-api.
type TokenSource interface {
        // Token returns a usable access token.
        Token(ctx context.Context) (string, error)
        // Refresh discards the current token after auth-api answered 401 and returns a new one. It
        // returns ErrNoRefresh when a replacement cannot help, such as a token fetched this run.
        Refresh(ctx context.Context) (string, error)
        // Rejected records that auth-api rejected a token fetched this run, so later runs stop. It
        // returns an error when the record could not be written.
        Rejected(ctx context.Context) error
        // Accepted records that auth-api accepted the token, ending any earlier rejection.
        Accepted(ctx context.Context)
}

// ErrAuth0Backoff is returned while earlier token fetches failed and the backoff has not expired.
// It is not a token rejection: the run fails with exit 1 and Auth0 is not called.
var ErrAuth0Backoff = errors.New("reconcile: Auth0 token fetch is backing off after earlier failures")

// ErrNoRefresh is returned by a source that cannot replace a rejected token.
var ErrNoRefresh = errors.New("reconcile: token cannot be refreshed")

// StaticToken is a TokenSource for one fixed token.
type StaticToken string

// Token returns the fixed token.
func (s StaticToken) Token(context.Context) (string, error) { return string(s), nil }

// Refresh always fails: a fixed token has no replacement.
func (s StaticToken) Refresh(context.Context) (string, error) { return "", ErrNoRefresh }

// Rejected does nothing for a fixed token.
func (s StaticToken) Rejected(context.Context) error { return nil }

// Accepted does nothing for a fixed token.
func (s StaticToken) Accepted(context.Context) {}

// ClientCredentials fetches an Auth0 machine-to-machine access token with the client-credentials
// grant and caches it in Redis until 120 seconds before it expires, so a job that runs every five
// minutes uses about 30 Auth0 tokens a month instead of 8,640 (PADR-0041). The secret is never
// logged or put in an error.
type ClientCredentials struct {
        TokenURL, ClientID, ClientSecret, Audience string
        Pool                                       *redis.Pool
        HTTP                                       *http.Client

        token    string // this run's token, so one run reads Redis once
        err      error  // sticky: one failed fetch fails the rest of the run without hitting Auth0 again
        fetched  bool   // the token came from Auth0 in this run, so a 401 on it is final
        accepted bool   // the rejection marker was already cleared this run
}

// NewClientCredentials returns a source that caches in pool.
func NewClientCredentials(tokenURL, clientID, clientSecret, audience string, pool *redis.Pool) *ClientCredentials {
        return &ClientCredentials{TokenURL: tokenURL, ClientID: clientID, ClientSecret: clientSecret, Audience: audience,
                Pool: pool, HTTP: &http.Client{
                        Timeout: callTimeout,
                        // A redirect would re-send the POST body, client secret included, to wherever it points.
                        CheckRedirect: func(*http.Request, []*http.Request) error { return http.ErrUseLastResponse },
                }}
}

// Token returns the cached token, or fetches and caches a new one.
func (c *ClientCredentials) Token(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
        if c.err != nil {
                return "", c.err
        }
        if c.token != "" {
                return c.token, nil
        }
        tok, err := c.rejectionOrCached()
        if err == nil && tok == "" {
                tok, err = c.fetchAndStore(ctx)
        }
        return c.remember(tok, err)
}

// rejectionOrCached fails with ErrTokenRejected while the rejection marker exists, and otherwise
// returns the cached token ("" when there is none).
func (c *ClientCredentials) rejectionOrCached() (string, error) {
        conn := c.Pool.Get()
        defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
        marked, err := redis.Bool(conn.Do("EXISTS", rejectedKey))
        if err != nil {
                return "", fmt.Errorf("read rejection marker: %w", err)
        }
        if marked {
                return "", fmt.Errorf("auth-api rejected the last fresh token, not calling Auth0 until %s expires: %w", rejectedKey, ErrTokenRejected)
        }
        return c.cached()
}

// Refresh clears the cached token and fetches a new one.
func (c *ClientCredentials) Refresh(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
        if c.fetched {
                return "", ErrNoRefresh // a token fetched this run was rejected; another would be too
        }
        // While Auth0 is backing off no replacement can be fetched, so keep the cached token and set no
        // rejection marker: the run fails with exit 1 and the next one reuses the cache.
        if err := c.checkBackoff(ctx); err != nil {
                return c.remember("", err)
        }
        c.token, c.err = "", nil
        conn := c.Pool.Get()
        defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
        if _, err := conn.Do("DEL", tokenCacheKey); err != nil {
                return c.remember("", fmt.Errorf("clear cached token: %w", err))
        }
        tok, err := c.fetchAndStore(ctx)
        return c.remember(tok, err)
}

// Rejected clears the bad cached token and sets the rejection marker, so no run calls Auth0 for an
// hour. The fetch guard still limits Auth0 calls to one a minute if the marker cannot be written.
func (c *ClientCredentials) Rejected(context.Context) error {
        conn := c.Pool.Get()
        defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
        _ = conn.Send("DEL", tokenCacheKey)
        if _, err := conn.Do("SET", rejectedKey, "1", "EX", int64(rejectedTTL/time.Second)); err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("record token rejection: %w", err)
        }
        return nil
}

// Accepted deletes the rejection marker once per run.
func (c *ClientCredentials) Accepted(ctx context.Context) {
        if c.accepted {
                return
        }
        c.accepted = true
        conn := c.Pool.Get()
        defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
        if _, err := conn.Do("DEL", rejectedKey); err != nil {
                slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: could not clear the token rejection marker", "error", err)
        }
}

func (c *ClientCredentials) remember(tok string, err error) (string, error) {
        if err != nil {
                c.err = err
                return "", err
        }
        c.token = tok
        return tok, nil
}

// cached returns the token in Redis, or "" when there is none. Its TTL ends tokenSkew before the
// token does, so a hit is always usable.
func (c *ClientCredentials) cached() (string, error) {
        conn := c.Pool.Get()
        defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
        tok, err := redis.String(conn.Do("GET", tokenCacheKey))
        if errors.Is(err, redis.ErrNil) {
                return "", nil
        }
        if err != nil {
                return "", fmt.Errorf("read cached token: %w", err)
        }
        return tok, nil
}

func (c *ClientCredentials) fetchAndStore(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
        if err := c.checkBackoff(ctx); err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        if err := c.takeFetchGuard(); err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        tok, lifetime, err := c.fetch(ctx)
        if err != nil {
                return "", c.recordFailure(ctx, err)
        }
        c.fetched = true
        defer c.clearFailures(ctx)
        ttl := lifetime - tokenSkew
        if ttl < time.Second {
                return tok, nil // too short to cache; the next run fetches again
        }
        conn := c.Pool.Get()
        defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
        if _, err := conn.Do("SET", tokenCacheKey, tok, "EX", int64(ttl/time.Second)); err != nil {
                return "", fmt.Errorf("cache token: %w", err)
        }
        return tok, nil
}

// checkBackoff fails without calling Auth0 while the backoff key exists.
func (c *ClientCredentials) checkBackoff(ctx context.Context) error {
        conn := c.Pool.Get()
        defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
        left, err := redis.Int64(conn.Do("PTTL", auth0BackoffKey))
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("read auth0 backoff: %w", err)
        }
        if left == -2 { // no key
                return nil
        }
        if left == -1 { // a key without expiry was set by hand; fail closed rather than guess
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "reconcile: Auth0 backoff key has no expiry; not calling Auth0 until it is deleted")
                return fmt.Errorf("%w (key %s has no expiry; delete it)", ErrAuth0Backoff, auth0BackoffKey)
        }
        remaining := (time.Duration(left) * time.Millisecond).Round(time.Second)
        slog.WarnContext(ctx, "reconcile: Auth0 token fetch is backing off; not calling Auth0", "remaining", remaining.String())
        return fmt.Errorf("%w (retry in %v)", ErrAuth0Backoff, remaining)
}

// recordFailure counts a failed fetch and arms the backoff: 5m, 10m, 20m, 40m, then 1h, or Auth0's
// Retry-After when that is longer (capped at 1h). It returns the fetch error, joined with the Redis
// error when the backoff could not be written. A Redis that cannot take this write could not take
// the fetch guard's either, so the run would already have stopped before calling Auth0; the error
// is still returned so a failure here is never silent.
func (c *ClientCredentials) recordFailure(ctx context.Context, fetchErr error) error {
        if ctx.Err() != nil {
                return fetchErr // a run timeout or SIGTERM is not Auth0's fault
        }
        var retryAfter time.Duration
        var fe *fetchError
        if errors.As(fetchErr, &fe) {
                retryAfter = fe.retryAfter
        }
        conn := c.Pool.Get()
        defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
        reply, err := redis.Int64s(recordFailureScript.Do(conn, auth0FailuresKey, auth0BackoffKey,
                int64(failuresTTL/time.Second), int64(backoffBase/time.Second), int64(backoffMax/time.Second), int64(retryAfter/time.Second)))
        if err != nil || len(reply) != 2 {
                return errors.Join(fetchErr, fmt.Errorf("arm auth0 backoff: %v", err))
        }
        slog.WarnContext(ctx, "reconcile: Auth0 token fetch failed; backing off", "failures", reply[0], "wait", (time.Duration(reply[1]) * time.Second).String())
        return fetchErr
}

// recordFailureScript counts a failure and arms the backoff in one atomic step, so a failure
// between two commands cannot leave a count with no TTL or a count with no backoff. KEYS: failures,
// backoff. ARGV: failures TTL, base, max, Retry-After (seconds). Returns {count, wait seconds}.
var recordFailureScript = redis.NewScript(2, `
local n = redis.call('INCR', KEYS[1])
redis.call('EXPIRE', KEYS[1], tonumber(ARGV[1]))
local wait = tonumber(ARGV[2]) * 2 ^ math.min(n - 1, 10)
local retry = tonumber(ARGV[4])
if retry > wait then wait = retry end
wait = math.floor(math.min(wait, tonumber(ARGV[3])))
redis.call('SET', KEYS[2], '1', 'EX', wait)
return {n, wait}
`)

// clearFailures ends the backoff after a successful fetch. A failure here only leaves keys that
// expire on their own, so it is logged, not returned.
func (c *ClientCredentials) clearFailures(ctx context.Context) {
        conn := c.Pool.Get()
        defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
        if _, err := conn.Do("DEL", auth0BackoffKey, auth0FailuresKey); err != nil {
                slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "reconcile: could not clear the Auth0 backoff keys", "error", err)
        }
}

// fetchError is a failed token request. retryAfter is Auth0's Retry-After in seconds, when sent on
// a 429.
type fetchError struct {
        msg        string
        retryAfter time.Duration
}

func (e *fetchError) Error() string { return e.msg }

// takeFetchGuard claims the right to call Auth0. It fails when Redis cannot take the write or
// another fetch happened in the last minute, in both cases before any Auth0 call.
func (c *ClientCredentials) takeFetchGuard() error {
        conn := c.Pool.Get()
        defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
        reply, err := redis.String(conn.Do("SET", fetchGuardKey, "1", "NX", "EX", int64(fetchGuardTTL/time.Second)))
        if errors.Is(err, redis.ErrNil) {
                return fmt.Errorf("a token was fetched in the last %v; not calling Auth0 again", fetchGuardTTL)
        }
        if err != nil || reply != "OK" {
                return fmt.Errorf("fetch guard: redis cannot take writes, not calling Auth0: %v", err)
        }
        return nil
}

// fetch requests a token from Auth0. Errors carry the status, never the response body or secret.
func (c *ClientCredentials) fetch(ctx context.Context) (string, time.Duration, error) {
        body, err := json.Marshal(map[string]string{
                "grant_type": "client_credentials", "client_id": c.ClientID, "client_secret": c.ClientSecret, "audience": c.Audience,
        })
        if err != nil {
                return "", 0, err
        }
        req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, c.TokenURL, bytes.NewReader(body))
        if err != nil {
                return "", 0, errors.New("build token request")
        }
        req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
        resp, err := c.HTTP.Do(req)
        if err != nil {
                // Only the failure class: the URL error text could echo the request.
                return "", 0, &fetchError{msg: fmt.Sprintf("token endpoint unreachable (%s)", failureClass(err))}
        }
        defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
        if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
                fe := &fetchError{msg: fmt.Sprintf("token endpoint returned status %d", resp.StatusCode)}
                if resp.StatusCode == http.StatusTooManyRequests {
                        fe.retryAfter = parseRetryAfter(resp.Header.Get("Retry-After"))
                }
                return "", 0, fe
        }
        var out struct {
                AccessToken string `json:"access_token"`
                TokenType   string `json:"token_type"`
                ExpiresIn   int64  `json:"expires_in"`
        }
        if err := json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, maxBody)).Decode(&out); err != nil || strings.TrimSpace(out.AccessToken) == "" || !strings.EqualFold(out.TokenType, "Bearer") {
                return "", 0, &fetchError{msg: "token endpoint returned no bearer access token"}
        }
        if out.ExpiresIn <= 0 {
                return "", 0, &fetchError{msg: "token endpoint returned no token lifetime"}
        }
        return out.AccessToken, time.Duration(min(out.ExpiresIn, int64(maxLifetime/time.Second))) * time.Second, nil
}

// parseRetryAfter reads a Retry-After given in seconds, capped at the backoff maximum. A number
// too large for int64 is the cap; anything else (a date, a negative, text) is no hint.
func parseRetryAfter(v string) time.Duration {
        secs, err := strconv.ParseInt(strings.TrimSpace(v), 10, 64)
        switch {
        case errors.Is(err, strconv.ErrRange) && !strings.HasPrefix(strings.TrimSpace(v), "-"):
                return backoffMax
        case err != nil || secs <= 0:
                return 0
        }
        return time.Duration(min(secs, int64(backoffMax/time.Second))) * time.Second
}

// failureClass names why a request failed without quoting the error, which can carry the URL.
func failureClass(err error) string {
        var netErr net.Error
        var dns *net.DNSError
        var cert *tls.CertificateVerificationError
        switch {
        case errors.As(err, &cert):
                return "tls certificate"
        case errors.As(err, &dns):
                return "dns"
        case errors.Is(err, syscall.ECONNREFUSED):
                return "connection refused"
        case errors.As(err, &netErr) && netErr.Timeout():
                return "timeout"
        default:
                return "network"
        }
}

// Package relations is the client for auth-api's POST /authz/check-relation (PADR-0035).
//
// The caller's own bearer token is forwarded on every call (PADR-0037, no service tokens), so a
// check only answers for the user holding that token. Only positive decisions are cached, for a
// few seconds, so a removal takes effect within positiveTTL and a new grant at once. Any failure
// to get a decision is an error: session data fails closed (503), never open.
package relations

import (
        "bytes"
        "context"
        "encoding/json"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "io"
        "log/slog"
        "net/http"
        "sync"
        "time"
)

const (
        // positiveTTL bounds how long a removal can go unnoticed on a cached allow.
        positiveTTL = 5 * time.Second
        // maxEntries bounds the cache; past it, expired entries go first, then everything.
        maxEntries  = 10000
        callTimeout = 3 * time.Second
        maxBody     = 4 << 10
)

var (
        // ErrUnavailable means auth-api or OpenFGA could not give a decision. Callers answer 503.
        ErrUnavailable = errors.New("relations: authorization unavailable")
        // ErrUnauthorized means auth-api rejected the user's token. Callers answer 401.
        ErrUnauthorized = errors.New("relations: token rejected")
        // ErrDenied means auth-api refused the change: the caller lacks the relation, or is denied
        // the service. Callers answer 403.
        ErrDenied = errors.New("relations: not permitted")
        // ErrLimited means auth-api's mint or tuple limits stopped the call. Callers answer 429.
        ErrLimited = errors.New("relations: rate limited")
        // ErrConflict means auth-api refused the change as conflicting (the session's tuple cap).
        // Callers answer 409.
        ErrConflict = errors.New("relations: conflict")
        // ErrRejected means auth-api answered 400: this service sent a malformed request, so retrying
        // the same call never helps. Callers answer 500.
        ErrRejected = errors.New("relations: request rejected as malformed")
)

// Client calls auth-api. The zero value is not usable; use New.
type Client struct {
        base string
        http *http.Client
        now  func() time.Time

        mu    sync.Mutex
        cache map[string]time.Time // key -> expiry of a cached allow
}

// New returns a Client for the auth-api base URL.
func New(baseURL string) *Client {
        return &Client{base: baseURL, http: &http.Client{Timeout: callTimeout}, now: time.Now, cache: map[string]time.Time{}}
}

// Check reports whether the holder of token has relation on object (for example
// "session:abc", "can_view"). user is the verified caller ID and only keys the cache; auth-api
// decides from the token. A deny is (false, nil).
func (c *Client) Check(ctx context.Context, user, token, object, relation string) (bool, error) {
        if user == "" || token == "" || object == "" || relation == "" {
                return false, fmt.Errorf("relations: user, token, object and relation are required")
        }
        key := user + "\x00" + object + "\x00" + relation
        if c.allowed(key) {
                return true, nil
        }
        ok, err := c.fetch(ctx, token, object, relation)
        if err != nil {
                return false, err
        }
        if ok {
                c.remember(key)
        }
        return ok, nil
}

func (c *Client) fetch(ctx context.Context, token, object, relation string) (bool, error) {
        payload, err := json.Marshal(map[string]string{"object": object, "relation": relation})
        if err != nil {
                return false, fmt.Errorf("relations: encode request: %w", err)
        }
        req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, c.base+"/authz/check-relation", bytes.NewReader(payload))
        if err != nil {
                return false, fmt.Errorf("relations: build request: %w", err)
        }
        req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+token)
        req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
        req.Header.Set("Accept", "application/json")

        resp, err := c.http.Do(req)
        if err != nil {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "check-relation call failed", "error", err)
                return false, ErrUnavailable
        }
        defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()

        switch resp.StatusCode {
        case http.StatusOK:
                var body struct {
                        Allowed bool `json:"allowed"`
                }
                if err := json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, maxBody)).Decode(&body); err != nil {
                        return false, ErrUnavailable
                }
                return body.Allowed, nil
        case http.StatusUnauthorized:
                return false, ErrUnauthorized
        default:
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "check-relation rejected", "status", resp.StatusCode)
                return false, ErrUnavailable
        }
}

func (c *Client) allowed(key string) bool {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        exp, ok := c.cache[key]
        return ok && c.now().Before(exp)
}

func (c *Client) remember(key string) {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        if len(c.cache) >= maxEntries {
                now := c.now()
                for k, exp := range c.cache {
                        if !now.Before(exp) {
                                delete(c.cache, k)
                        }
                }
                if len(c.cache) >= maxEntries {
                        c.cache = map[string]time.Time{}
                }
        }
        c.cache[key] = c.now().Add(positiveTTL)
}

// Tuple is one membership relation, written for the session object the call names.
type Tuple struct {
        User     string `json:"user"` // "user:<canonical id>"
        Relation string `json:"relation"`
}

// Mint asks auth-api for a new session ID and writes the caller's gm tuple on it. The ID comes
// from auth-api, never from the client (PADR-0040).
func (c *Client) Mint(ctx context.Context, token string) (string, error) {
        var out struct {
                SessionID string `json:"session_id"`
        }
        if err := c.call(ctx, http.MethodPost, "/authz/sessions", token, nil, http.StatusCreated, &out); err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        if out.SessionID == "" {
                return "", ErrUnavailable
        }
        return out.SessionID, nil
}

// EndSession deletes every tuple on the session and closes its mint. Only the GM may.
func (c *Client) EndSession(ctx context.Context, token, sessionID string) error {
        return c.call(ctx, http.MethodDelete, "/authz/sessions/"+sessionID, token, nil, http.StatusNoContent, nil)
}

// WriteTuples applies membership tuple changes on one session in a single atomic write. Writing a
// tuple that exists and deleting one that does not are no-ops, so a retry is safe.
func (c *Client) WriteTuples(ctx context.Context, token, sessionID string, writes, deletes []Tuple) error {
        body := struct {
                SessionID string  `json:"session_id"`
                Writes    []Tuple `json:"writes"`
                Deletes   []Tuple `json:"deletes"`
        }{sessionID, writes, deletes}
        if body.Writes == nil {
                body.Writes = []Tuple{}
        }
        if body.Deletes == nil {
                body.Deletes = []Tuple{}
        }
        return c.call(ctx, http.MethodPost, "/authz/session-tuples", token, body, http.StatusOK, nil)
}

// call sends one authenticated request and maps the answer: 401 is ErrUnauthorized, 403 ErrDenied,
// 429 ErrLimited, anything else but want (or an unreadable body) ErrUnavailable.
func (c *Client) call(ctx context.Context, method, path, token string, body any, want int, out any) error {
        if token == "" {
                return ErrUnauthorized
        }
        var rdr io.Reader
        if body != nil {
                payload, err := json.Marshal(body)
                if err != nil {
                        return fmt.Errorf("relations: encode request: %w", err)
                }
                rdr = bytes.NewReader(payload)
        }
        req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, method, c.base+path, rdr)
        if err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("relations: build request: %w", err)
        }
        req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+token)
        req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
        req.Header.Set("Accept", "application/json")
        resp, err := c.http.Do(req)
        if err != nil {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "auth-api call failed", "path", path, "error", err)
                return ErrUnavailable
        }
        defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
        switch resp.StatusCode {
        case want:
                if out == nil {
                        return nil
                }
                if err := json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, maxBody)).Decode(out); err != nil {
                        return ErrUnavailable
                }
                return nil
        case http.StatusUnauthorized:
                return ErrUnauthorized
        case http.StatusForbidden:
                return ErrDenied
        case http.StatusTooManyRequests:
                return ErrLimited
        case http.StatusConflict:
                return ErrConflict
        case http.StatusBadRequest:
                slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "auth-api rejected a session call as malformed", "path", path)
                return ErrRejected
        }
        slog.WarnContext(ctx, "auth-api rejected a session call", "path", path, "status", resp.StatusCode)
        return ErrUnavailable
}

// Package seed loads the versioned game-system definitions checked into this repo. Loading is
// idempotent: unchanged definitions are skipped and keep their identifiers.
package seed

import (
        "context"
        "embed"
        "encoding/json"
        "fmt"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
)

//go:embed systems/families.json systems/systems.json
var files embed.FS

// Upserter is the part of data.SystemRepo the loader needs.
type Upserter interface {
        Upsert(ctx context.Context, sys data.GameSystem) (data.Outcome, error)
}

// Result counts what a load did.
type Result struct{ Created, Updated, Unchanged int }

type systemFile struct {
        ID      string              `json:"id"`
        Name    string              `json:"name"`
        Family  string              `json:"family"`
        Aliases []string            `json:"aliases"`
        Gated   bool                `json:"gated"`
        Fields  []data.TrackerField `json:"fields"` // optional override of the family's fields
}

// Definitions parses the seed files and expands each system's tracker fields from its family
// (a system may carry its own fields instead). Every result passes data.ValidateSystem.
func Definitions() ([]data.GameSystem, error) {
        var families map[string][]data.TrackerField
        if err := readJSON("systems/families.json", &families); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        var systems []systemFile
        if err := readJSON("systems/systems.json", &systems); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        return expand(families, systems)
}

// expand builds and validates the systems, and checks identifiers across the whole set: IDs are
// unique, and no alias repeats another alias or equals any system's ID (an alias lookup must
// never be ambiguous with an ID lookup).
func expand(families map[string][]data.TrackerField, systems []systemFile) ([]data.GameSystem, error) {
        out := make([]data.GameSystem, 0, len(systems))
        for _, s := range systems {
                fields := s.Fields
                if len(fields) == 0 {
                        var ok bool
                        if fields, ok = families[s.Family]; !ok {
                                return nil, fmt.Errorf("seed: system %q uses unknown family %q", s.ID, s.Family)
                        }
                }
                aliases := s.Aliases
                if aliases == nil {
                        aliases = []string{}
                }
                sys := data.GameSystem{ID: s.ID, Name: s.Name, Family: s.Family, Aliases: aliases, Gated: s.Gated, Fields: fields}
                if err := data.ValidateSystem(sys); err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                out = append(out, sys)
        }
        return out, checkIdentifiers(out)
}

func checkIdentifiers(systems []data.GameSystem) error {
        owner := map[string]string{} // identifier -> system that claims it
        for _, s := range systems {
                if prev, dup := owner[s.ID]; dup {
                        return fmt.Errorf("seed: id %q used by both %q and %q", s.ID, prev, s.ID)
                }
                owner[s.ID] = s.ID
        }
        for _, s := range systems {
                for _, a := range s.Aliases {
                        if prev, dup := owner[a]; dup {
                                return fmt.Errorf("seed: alias %q of %q collides with %q", a, s.ID, prev)
                        }
                        owner[a] = s.ID
                }
        }
        return nil
}

func readJSON(name string, v any) error {
        b, err := files.ReadFile(name)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if err := json.Unmarshal(b, v); err != nil {
                return fmt.Errorf("seed: parse %s: %w", name, err)
        }
        return nil
}

// Load upserts every seeded definition and reports the outcomes. It stops at the first error.
func Load(ctx context.Context, repo Upserter) (Result, error) {
        defs, err := Definitions()
        if err != nil {
                return Result{}, err
        }
        var r Result
        for _, d := range defs {
                o, err := repo.Upsert(ctx, d)
                if err != nil {
                        return r, fmt.Errorf("seed: %s: %w", d.ID, err)
                }
                switch o {
                case data.Created:
                        r.Created++
                case data.Updated:
                        r.Updated++
                default:
                        r.Unchanged++
                }
        }
        return r, nil
}

package server

import (
        "cmp"
        "encoding/json"
        "errors"
        "io"
        "net/http"
        "regexp"
        "slices"

        "github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
)

// maxBodyBytes bounds a request body on the resource routes: 256 KiB, far above a roster or an
// encounter with the most combatants and conditions the data layer allows, and far below what a
// hostile client could use to pin memory.
const maxBodyBytes = 256 << 10

// limitBody caps the request body so a handler never decodes more than maxBodyBytes.
func limitBody() gin.HandlerFunc {
        return func(c *gin.Context) {
                c.Request.Body = http.MaxBytesReader(c.Writer, c.Request.Body, maxBodyBytes)
                c.Next()
        }
}

var errTrailingData = errors.New("trailing data after the JSON value")

var unknownField = regexp.MustCompile(`unknown field "([^"]*)"`)

// bindJSON decodes the body into v and refuses a field v does not declare. It writes the typed
// error and returns false: 413 payload_too_large past the cap, 422 unknown_body_field naming the
// field, 400 for anything else that is not the JSON the route takes.
func bindJSON(c *gin.Context, v any) bool {
        dec := json.NewDecoder(c.Request.Body)
        dec.DisallowUnknownFields()
        err := dec.Decode(v)
        if err == nil {
                // One JSON value only: trailing data is a malformed request. Reading it can still hit the cap.
                _, extra := dec.Token()
                if errors.Is(extra, io.EOF) {
                        return true
                }
                err = cmp.Or(extra, errTrailingData)
        }
        var tooBig *http.MaxBytesError
        if errors.As(err, &tooBig) {
                c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusRequestEntityTooLarge, ErrorBody{Code: "payload_too_large", Message: "The request body is too large.", CorrelationID: CorrelationID(c)})
                return false
        }
        if m := unknownField.FindStringSubmatch(err.Error()); m != nil {
                c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusUnprocessableEntity, ErrorBody{Code: "unknown_body_field", Message: "The request body has a field this route does not accept.", Key: m[1], CorrelationID: CorrelationID(c)})
                return false
        }
        badRequest(c, "request body must be JSON in the shape this route takes")
        return false
}

// checkFields validates a projection list against the names a resource may project. The names are
// the resource's data.Spec allow-list plus any derived keys; anything else is a 422 like every
// other rejected list parameter.
func checkFields(c *gin.Context, fields, allowed []string) bool {
        for _, f := range fields {
                if !slices.Contains(allowed, f) {
                        c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusUnprocessableEntity, ErrorBody{Code: "invalid", Message: "cannot project " + f, Key: f, CorrelationID: CorrelationID(c)})
                        return false
                }
        }
        return true
}

// projectMap keeps the named keys of a rendered resource, plus always (the identity keys a
// client needs to address what it received). Projecting the rendered output rather than the stored
// document means an unselected field is absent, never a zero value that reads as real data.
func projectMap(m map[string]any, fields []string, always ...string) map[string]any {
        if len(fields) == 0 {
                return m
        }
        out := make(map[string]any, len(fields)+len(always))
        for _, k := range append(slices.Clone(fields), always...) {
                if v, ok := m[k]; ok {
                        out[k] = v
                }
        }
        return out
}

// toMap renders v through its JSON form, for projecting a stored resource.
func toMap(v any) map[string]any {
        b, err := json.Marshal(v)
        if err != nil {
                return map[string]any{}
        }
        var m map[string]any
        _ = json.Unmarshal(b, &m)
        return m
}

// fieldsOf reads and validates ?fields= for a route that takes no other list parameters.
func fieldsOf(c *gin.Context, allowed []string) ([]string, bool) {
        fields := splitList(c.Query("fields"))
        return fields, checkFields(c, fields, allowed)
}

package server

import (
        "net/http"

        "github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/authz-client.go/authz"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/domain"
)

type changeRequestHandlers struct{ svc *domain.ChangeRequests }

// setupChangeRequestHandlers registers change requests on a session's combatants. They sit in the
// user-only group like the session routes: integration credentials never reach them. The
// responses are request records, never encounters, so there is nothing for the view builder to
// redact here; the encounter itself still changes only through UpdateInSession.
func setupChangeRequestHandlers(g *gin.RouterGroup, svc *domain.ChangeRequests) {
        h := changeRequestHandlers{svc}
        g.POST("/sessions/:id/encounters/:eid/combatants/:cid/change-requests", h.submit)
        g.GET("/sessions/:id/encounters/:eid/change-requests", h.list)
        g.POST("/sessions/:id/encounters/:eid/change-requests/:rid/approve", h.approve)
        g.POST("/sessions/:id/encounters/:eid/change-requests/:rid/reject", h.reject)
}

type changeBody struct {
        Health map[string]int `json:"health"`
        // Conditions change by name: add sets (adds or updates) conditions, remove drops them.
        Conditions struct {
                Add    []data.Condition `json:"add"`
                Remove []string         `json:"remove"`
        } `json:"conditions"`
}

func (h changeRequestHandlers) submit(c *gin.Context) {
        var b changeBody
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        req, err := h.svc.Submit(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), c.Param("eid"), c.Param("cid"),
                domain.ChangeInput{Health: b.Health, AddConditions: b.Conditions.Add, RemoveConditions: b.Conditions.Remove})
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusCreated, req)
}

func (h changeRequestHandlers) list(c *gin.Context) {
        q, ok := pageQuery(c)
        if !ok {
                return
