        return data.Character{Name: b.Name, Kind: b.Kind, Notes: b.Notes, Health: b.Health, ExternalRefs: b.ExternalRefs}
}

// rosterCreate is the body of POST /rosters.
type rosterCreate struct {
        Name       *string         `json:"name"`
        Favorite   *bool           `json:"favorite"`
        Characters []characterBody `json:"characters"`
}

// rosterPatch is the body of PATCH /rosters/:id. Characters change through their own routes.
type rosterPatch struct {
        Revision int64   `json:"revision"`
        Name     *string `json:"name"`
        Favorite *bool   `json:"favorite"`
}

// conflict writes err, attaching the roster's current state to a revision conflict so the client
// can merge and retry.
func (h rosterHandlers) conflict(c *gin.Context, err error, id string) {
        var current any
        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrRevisionConflict) {
                if r, gerr := h.svc.Get(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), id); gerr == nil {
                        current = r
                }
        }
        writeError(c, err, current)
}

func (h rosterHandlers) list(c *gin.Context) {
        q, ok := parseQuery(c, map[string]string{"favorite": typeBool})
        if !ok || !checkFields(c, q.Fields, data.RosterSpec.Projectable) {
                return
        }
        // The projection is applied to the rendered roster, so an unselected field is absent rather
        // than a zero value.
        fields := q.Fields
        q.Fields = nil
        page, err := h.svc.List(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), q)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        items := make([]map[string]any, 0, len(page.Items))
        for _, r := range page.Items {
                items = append(items, projectMap(toMap(r), fields, "id", "revision"))
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, gin.H{"items": items, "next_cursor": page.NextCursor})
}

func (h rosterHandlers) get(c *gin.Context) {
        fields, ok := fieldsOf(c, data.RosterSpec.Projectable)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        r, err := h.svc.Load(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"))
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, projectMap(toMap(r), fields, "id", "revision"))
}

func (h rosterHandlers) create(c *gin.Context) {
        var b rosterCreate
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        if b.Name == nil {
                badRequest(c, "request body must be a JSON roster with a name")
                return
        }
        if !requireRoom(c, h.ent, domain.KeySavedRosters, h.svc.Count) {
                return
        }
        chars := make([]data.Character, 0, len(b.Characters))
        for _, ch := range b.Characters {
                chars = append(chars, ch.character())
        }
        owner := authz.Viewer(c)
        r, err := h.svc.Create(c.Request.Context(), owner, owner, *b.Name, b.Favorite != nil && *b.Favorite, chars)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusCreated, r)
}

func (h rosterHandlers) update(c *gin.Context) {
        var b rosterPatch
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        rev, ok := revisionOf(c, b.Revision)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        owner, id := authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id")
        cur, err := h.svc.Load(c.Request.Context(), owner, id)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        name, fav := cur.Name, cur.Favorite
        if b.Name != nil {
                name = *b.Name
        }
        if b.Favorite != nil {
                fav = *b.Favorite
        }
        r, err := h.svc.Rename(c.Request.Context(), owner, owner, id, rev, name, fav)
        if err != nil {
                h.conflict(c, err, id)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, r)
}

func (h rosterHandlers) remove(c *gin.Context) {
        rev, ok := revisionOf(c, 0)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        owner := authz.Viewer(c)
        if err := h.svc.Delete(c.Request.Context(), owner, owner, c.Param("id"), rev); err != nil {
                h.conflict(c, err, c.Param("id"))
                return
        }
        c.Status(http.StatusNoContent)
}

// characterRequest is a character body plus the roster revision it is based on.
type characterRequest struct {
        characterBody
        Revision int64 `json:"revision"`
}

func (h rosterHandlers) addCharacter(c *gin.Context) {
        var b characterRequest
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        rev, ok := revisionOf(c, b.Revision)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        owner := authz.Viewer(c)
        r, err := h.svc.AddCharacter(c.Request.Context(), owner, owner, c.Param("id"), rev, b.character())
        if err != nil {
                h.conflict(c, err, c.Param("id"))
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusCreated, r)
}

func (h rosterHandlers) replaceCharacter(c *gin.Context) {
        var b characterRequest
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        rev, ok := revisionOf(c, b.Revision)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        owner := authz.Viewer(c)
        r, err := h.svc.UpdateCharacter(c.Request.Context(), owner, owner, c.Param("id"), rev, c.Param("cid"), b.character())
        if err != nil {
                h.conflict(c, err, c.Param("id"))
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, r)
}

func (h rosterHandlers) removeCharacter(c *gin.Context) {
        rev, ok := revisionOf(c, 0)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        owner := authz.Viewer(c)
        r, err := h.svc.RemoveCharacter(c.Request.Context(), owner, owner, c.Param("id"), rev, c.Param("cid"))
        if err != nil {
                h.conflict(c, err, c.Param("id"))
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, r)
}

package server

import (
        "net/http"
        "strconv"

)

type sessionHandlers struct{ svc *domain.Sessions }

// setupSessionHandlers registers the session routes. They sit in the user-only group: session
// membership belongs to a person, so an integration credential cannot reach it.
func setupSessionHandlers(g *gin.RouterGroup, svc *domain.Sessions) {
        h := sessionHandlers{svc}
        g.POST("/sessions", h.create)
        g.GET("/sessions", h.list)
        g.POST("/sessions/join", h.join)
        g.GET("/sessions/:id", h.get)
        g.PATCH("/sessions/:id", h.update)
        g.DELETE("/sessions/:id", h.remove)
        g.POST("/sessions/:id/leave", h.leave)
        g.GET("/sessions/:id/members", h.members)
        g.PATCH("/sessions/:id/members/:user", h.setRole)
        g.DELETE("/sessions/:id/members/:user", h.removeMember)
        g.POST("/sessions/:id/invites", h.createInvite)
        g.GET("/sessions/:id/invites", h.invites)
        g.DELETE("/sessions/:id/invites/:invite", h.revokeInvite)
        g.GET("/sessions/:id/requests", h.requests)
        g.POST("/sessions/:id/requests/:request/approve", h.approve)
        g.POST("/sessions/:id/requests/:request/reject", h.reject)
        g.GET("/sessions/:id/blocks", h.blocks)
        g.POST("/sessions/:id/blocks", h.block)
        g.DELETE("/sessions/:id/blocks/:user", h.unblock)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) create(c *gin.Context) {
        var b struct {
                Name                string `json:"name"`
                AllowCoGMEncounters bool   `json:"allow_co_gm_create_encounter"`
        }
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        s, err := h.svc.CreateWith(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), b.Name, b.AllowCoGMEncounters)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusCreated, s)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) list(c *gin.Context) {
        q, ok := pageQuery(c)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        page, err := h.svc.List(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), q)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, gin.H{"items": page.Items, "next_cursor": page.NextCursor})
}

func (h sessionHandlers) get(c *gin.Context) {
        s, err := h.svc.Get(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"))
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, s)
}

// update changes the session's name or its co-GM encounter-creation setting. GM only.
func (h sessionHandlers) update(c *gin.Context) {
        var b struct {
                Name                *string `json:"name"`
                AllowCoGMEncounters *bool   `json:"allow_co_gm_create_encounter"`
                Revision            int64   `json:"revision"`
        }
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        rev, ok := revisionOf(c, b.Revision)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        s, err := h.svc.Update(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), rev, domain.SessionChanges{Name: b.Name, AllowCoGMEncounters: b.AllowCoGMEncounters})
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, s)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) remove(c *gin.Context) {
        rev, err := strconv.ParseInt(c.Query("revision"), 10, 64)
        if err != nil || rev < 1 {
                badRequest(c, "revision query parameter is required")
                return
        }
        if err := h.svc.Delete(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), rev); err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.Status(http.StatusNoContent)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) leave(c *gin.Context) {
        if err := h.svc.Leave(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id")); err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.Status(http.StatusNoContent)
}

func writePage(c *gin.Context, items any, next string, err error) {
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, gin.H{"items": items, "next_cursor": next})
}

func (h sessionHandlers) members(c *gin.Context) {
        q, ok := pageQuery(c)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        p, err := h.svc.Members(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), q)
        writePage(c, p.Items, p.NextCursor, err)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) invites(c *gin.Context) {
        q, ok := pageQuery(c)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        p, err := h.svc.Invites(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), q)
        writePage(c, p.Items, p.NextCursor, err)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) requests(c *gin.Context) {
        q, ok := pageQuery(c)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        p, err := h.svc.Requests(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), c.Query("status") != "all", q)
        writePage(c, p.Items, p.NextCursor, err)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) blocks(c *gin.Context) {
        q, ok := pageQuery(c)
        if !ok {
                return
        }
        p, err := h.svc.Blocks(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), q)
        writePage(c, p.Items, p.NextCursor, err)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) setRole(c *gin.Context) {
        var b struct {
                Role string `json:"role"`
        }
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        m, err := h.svc.SetRole(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), c.Param("user"), b.Role)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, m)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) removeMember(c *gin.Context) {
        if err := h.svc.Remove(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), c.Param("user")); err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.Status(http.StatusNoContent)
}

// createInvite returns the code once; only its hash is stored.
func (h sessionHandlers) createInvite(c *gin.Context) {
        var b struct {
                TTLHours int `json:"ttl_hours"`
        }
        if c.Request.ContentLength != 0 {
                if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                        return
                }
                if b.TTLHours < 0 {
                        badRequest(c, "request body must be JSON with an optional ttl_hours")
                        return
                }
        }
        in, code, err := h.svc.CreateInvite(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), time.Duration(b.TTLHours)*time.Hour)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusCreated, gin.H{"invite": in, "code": code})
}

func (h sessionHandlers) revokeInvite(c *gin.Context) {
        in, err := h.svc.RevokeInvite(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), c.Param("invite"))
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, in)
}

// join answers with the request's id and state only: a pending requester sees no session data.
func (h sessionHandlers) join(c *gin.Context) {
        var b struct {
                Code string `json:"code"`
        }
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        if b.Code == "" {
                badRequest(c, "request body must be JSON with a code")
                return
        }
        jr, err := h.svc.Join(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), b.Code)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusAccepted, gin.H{"id": jr.ID, "status": jr.Status})
}

func (h sessionHandlers) approve(c *gin.Context) {
        var b struct {
                Role string `json:"role"`
        }
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        m, err := h.svc.Approve(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), c.Param("request"), b.Role)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, m)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) reject(c *gin.Context) {
        var b struct {
                Reason string `json:"reason"`
        }
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        jr, err := h.svc.Reject(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), c.Param("request"), b.Reason)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, jr)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) block(c *gin.Context) {
        var b struct {
                UserID string `json:"user_id"`
        }
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        if err := h.svc.Block(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), b.UserID); err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.Status(http.StatusNoContent)
}

func (h sessionHandlers) unblock(c *gin.Context) {
        if err := h.svc.Unblock(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"), c.Param("user")); err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.Status(http.StatusNoContent)
}

package server

import (
        "context"
        "log/slog"
        "net/http"
        "sync"
        "time"

        "github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
        apicores "github.com/sweetrpg/api-core.go/server"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/api-core.go/vo"
)

// Ping is the shallow liveness and readiness probe target (PADR-0017).
//
//        @Summary        Ping
//        @Tags                status
//        @Produce        json
//        @Success        200        {object}        vo.PingResponseVO
//        @Router                /status/ping [get]
func ping(c *gin.Context) {
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, apicores.PingHandler())
}

// Readiness is the deep dependency check behind /status/health. PADR-0017 keeps it off the
// readiness and liveness probes (both use /status/ping): it is for operators and dashboards and
// must not gate routing or restarts. Each check runs under a timeout, and the answer is cached for ttl, so probes from every kubelet
// and load balancer cost the dependency one call per ttl, not one each.
type Readiness struct {
        checks  []Check
        ttl     time.Duration
        timeout time.Duration
        now     func() time.Time

        mu       sync.Mutex
        at       time.Time
        messages []string
}

// Check is one dependency's liveness test.
type Check struct {
        Name string
        Fn   func(ctx context.Context) error
}

// NewReadiness builds the check set; a zero ttl or timeout takes 5s and 2s.
func NewReadiness(ttl, timeout time.Duration, checks ...Check) *Readiness {
        if ttl <= 0 {
                ttl = 5 * time.Second
        }
        if timeout <= 0 {
                timeout = 2 * time.Second
        }
        return &Readiness{checks: checks, ttl: ttl, timeout: timeout, now: time.Now}
}

// Problems returns one message per failing check (none when ready), from the cache when it is
// younger than the ttl. It holds a lock while it checks, so concurrent probes share one round.
func (r *Readiness) Problems(ctx context.Context) []string {
        r.mu.Lock()
        defer r.mu.Unlock()
        if !r.at.IsZero() && r.now().Sub(r.at) < r.ttl {
                return r.messages
        }
        msgs := []string{}
        // The answer is cached for every caller, so a probe that hangs up must not cancel the check
        // and leave a false "unavailable" for the next ttl.
        base := context.WithoutCancel(ctx)
        for _, ch := range r.checks {
                cctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(base, r.timeout)
                if err := ch.Fn(cctx); err != nil {
                        slog.ErrorContext(ctx, "health check failed", "check", ch.Name, "error", err)
                        msgs = append(msgs, ch.Name+": unavailable") // the detail stays in the log, not the response
                }
                cancel()
        }
        r.at, r.messages = r.now(), msgs
        return msgs
}

// Health is the deep dependency check. It answers 503 while a dependency is down. Probes use
// /status/ping (PADR-0017), so this never removes a pod from the Service or restarts it.
//
//        @Summary        Health check
//        @Tags                status
//        @Produce        json
//        @Success        200        {object}        vo.HealthResponseVO
//        @Failure        503        {object}        vo.HealthResponseVO
//        @Router                /status/health [get]
func health(r *Readiness) gin.HandlerFunc {
        return func(c *gin.Context) {
                msgs := []string{}
                if r != nil {
                        msgs = r.Problems(c.Request.Context())
                }
                status := http.StatusOK
                if len(msgs) > 0 {
                        status = http.StatusServiceUnavailable
                }
                c.JSON(status, vo.HealthResponseVO{Messages: msgs, Errors: len(msgs)})
        }
}

func setupStatusHandlers(r *gin.Engine, ready *Readiness) {
        r.GET("/status/ping", ping)
        r.GET("/status/health", health(ready))
}

package server

import (
        "context"
        cryptorand "crypto/rand"
        "encoding/hex"
        "encoding/json"
        "errors"
        "log/slog"
        "net/http"
        "net/url"
        "path"
        "strings"
        "sync/atomic"
        "time"

        "github.com/coder/websocket"
        "github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/authz-client.go/authz"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/constants"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/domain"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/events/stream"
)

// WebSocket close codes the service sends (application range 4000-4999).
const (
        CloseCredentialRevoked websocket.StatusCode = 4001 // the credential that opened the stream is gone
        CloseMembershipChanged websocket.StatusCode = 4002 // access changed or ended; reconnect to find out
        CloseSlowConsumer      websocket.StatusCode = 4003 // the client fell behind; reconnect and refetch
        CloseMaxAge            websocket.StatusCode = 4004 // the connection reached its maximum age
        maxGuardErrors                              = 3
        maxRecheckFailures                          = 3 // failed membership re-checks in a row that end a stream
        checkTimeout                                = 2 * time.Second
        readLimit                                   = 1024
        streamBuffer                                = 64 // events buffered per connection
        writeTimeout                                = 10 * time.Second
        pongTimeout                                 = 60 * time.Second
        ticketQuery                                 = "ticket"
        ticketKey                                   = "initiative.stream_ticket"
        ticketPresentKey                            = "initiative.stream_ticket_present"
        redactedKey                                 = "initiative.stream_redacted"
)

// StreamOptions are the transport settings of the event stream.
type StreamOptions struct {
        // AllowedOrigins are host patterns (path.Match) a browser's Origin may match; no Origin header
        // is always allowed.
        AllowedOrigins []string
        // MaxAge ends a stream after this long (default 1h); Heartbeat is the interval of the
        // application heartbeat and the protocol ping (default 20s).
        MaxAge, Heartbeat time.Duration
        // RecheckInterval is how often a stream re-reads its membership and credential (default 5s,
        // never above Heartbeat).
        RecheckInterval time.Duration
        // Buffer is how many events a stream may fall behind before it is closed (default 64).
        Buffer int
        // beforeWrite, when set by a test, runs before each event is written.
        beforeWrite func()
        // onServe, when set by a test, runs as serve starts, holding a pod slot.
        onServe func()
        // afterRenew, when set by a test, runs after each seat renewal attempt.
        afterRenew func()
        // ConcurrentMax is the most streams one user may hold (Services.Leases counts them, with
        // seats lasting LeaseTTL unless renewed and kept Grace after a drop); MaxPerPod is this pod's
        // cap, answered with close code 1013. Zero disables the cap.
        ConcurrentMax, CredentialMax, MaxPerPod int
        LeaseTTL, Grace                         time.Duration
        // Pod names this pod on its seats.
        Pod string
}

func (o StreamOptions) buffer() int {
        if o.Buffer > 0 {
                return o.Buffer
        }
        return streamBuffer
}

func (o StreamOptions) leaseTTL() time.Duration {
        if o.LeaseTTL > 0 {
                return o.LeaseTTL
        }
        return constants.DefaultStreamLeaseTTL
}

func (o StreamOptions) maxAge() time.Duration {
        if o.MaxAge > 0 {
                return o.MaxAge
        }
        return constants.DefaultStreamMaxAge
}

func (o StreamOptions) recheck() time.Duration {
        d := o.RecheckInterval
        if d <= 0 {
                d = constants.DefaultStreamRecheckInterval
        }
        return min(d, o.heartbeat())
}

// The re-check runs inline in the connection's loop, so a slow client delays it. The loop is held
// by one write at a time and a due re-check runs before every event write, so stallAllowance is the
// most one pass can hold it: a heartbeat write that the client takes up to writeTimeout to accept,
// then a seat renewal bounded by checkTimeout. A write that does not complete in writeTimeout ends
// the stream anyway.
const stallAllowance = writeTimeout + checkTimeout

// RevocationBound is the longest a stream stays open after its member is removed, demoted or
// blocked, when the eviction itself is lost (the evictor or the relay is down) and the client keeps
// up: one re-check interval to the next re-check, plus the time that check may take.
func (o StreamOptions) RevocationBound() time.Duration { return o.recheck() + checkTimeout }

// StalledBound is RevocationBound when the client's socket is slow enough to hold the loop for a
// whole heartbeat write and renewal first.
func (o StreamOptions) StalledBound() time.Duration { return o.RevocationBound() + stallAllowance }

// FaultBound is RevocationBound when the store cannot answer the re-checks: the stream closes at
// the third consecutive failed one, so each of three intervals may also spend a full check timeout.
func (o StreamOptions) FaultBound() time.Duration {
        return maxRecheckFailures * (o.recheck() + checkTimeout)
}

// StalledFaultBound is FaultBound with a slow client.
func (o StreamOptions) StalledFaultBound() time.Duration { return o.FaultBound() + stallAllowance }

// renewTimeout bounds one seat renewal, which holds the connection's loop.
func (o StreamOptions) renewTimeout() time.Duration { return min(o.heartbeat()/2, checkTimeout) }

func (o StreamOptions) heartbeat() time.Duration {
        if o.Heartbeat > 0 {
                return o.Heartbeat
        }
        return constants.DefaultStreamHeartbeat
}

// RedactTicket takes the stream ticket out of the request URL before anything logs it, and keeps
// it for the stream handler. The ticket is single-use and short-lived, so a leaked one is mostly
// harmless, but it should never reach an access log.
func RedactTicket() gin.HandlerFunc {
        return func(c *gin.Context) {
                c.Set(redactedKey, true)
                if c.Request.URL.RawQuery != "" {
                        q := c.Request.URL.Query()
                        if q.Has(ticketQuery) {
                                for _, t := range q[ticketQuery] { // the first non-empty value is the ticket
                                        if t != "" {
                                                c.Set(ticketKey, t)
                                                break
                                        }
                                }
                                c.Set(ticketPresentKey, true)
                                q.Del(ticketQuery)
                                c.Request.URL.RawQuery = q.Encode()
                                c.Request.RequestURI = c.Request.URL.RequestURI() // Recovery and tracing read this one
                        }
                }
                c.Next()
        }
}

type streamHandlers struct {
        svc  Services
        opts StreamOptions
        open *atomic.Int64 // connections on this pod
        // refusing bounds the refusals in flight (each holds a socket for the close handshake); nil is
        // unbounded.
        refusing chan struct{}
}

// maxRefusals is how many "pod full" closes may be in flight at once.
const maxRefusals = 32

// setupStreamHandlers registers POST /streams/tickets (user bearer only) on u, and GET /streams on
// r: with ticket= it opens the stream for that ticket, with no ticket it needs the caller's bearer
// (a user's, or an integration credential with the read scope) like every other route.
func setupStreamHandlers(r *gin.Engine, u *gin.RouterGroup, s Services) {
        h := streamHandlers{svc: s, opts: s.Stream, open: new(atomic.Int64), refusing: make(chan struct{}, maxRefusals)}
        u.POST("/streams/tickets", h.mint)
        g := r.Group("", h.ticketGate, typedErrors(), authenticate(s.Credentials, authz.RequireAnyRole(s.Authz, constants.ServiceName, authz.RoleUser)))
        g.Use(withOrigin(), withGate(s.Relations))
        g.GET("/streams", h.bearerStream)
}

type ticketBody struct {
        SessionID   string `json:"session_id"`
        EncounterID string `json:"encounter_id"`
        ClientID    string `json:"client_id"`
        // Scope is empty for an encounter stream or "user" for the user-level stream.
        Scope string `json:"scope"`
}

// scopeUser asks for the user-level stream.
const scopeUser = "user"

func (h streamHandlers) mint(c *gin.Context) {
        var b ticketBody
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        if b.Scope != "" && b.Scope != scopeUser {
                writeError(c, data.ErrInvalid, nil)
                return
        }
        ticket, ttl, err := h.svc.Streams.Mint(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), domain.TicketRequest{SessionID: b.SessionID, EncounterID: b.EncounterID, ClientID: b.ClientID, UserLevel: b.Scope == scopeUser})
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.Header("Cache-Control", "no-store")
        c.JSON(http.StatusCreated, gin.H{"ticket": ticket, "expires_in": int(ttl.Seconds())})
}

// originAllowed applies the same rule the upgrade does, before a ticket is spent on it.
func (o StreamOptions) originAllowed(r *http.Request) bool {
        origin := r.Header.Get("Origin")
        if origin == "" {
                return true
        }
        u, err := url.Parse(origin)
        if err != nil || u.Host == "" {
                return false
        }
        if strings.EqualFold(u.Host, r.Host) {
                return true
        }
        for _, p := range o.AllowedOrigins {
                if ok, _ := path.Match(strings.ToLower(p), strings.ToLower(u.Host)); ok {
                        return true
                }
        }
        return false
}

// ticketGate serves a request that carries a ticket; any other request continues to the bearer
// chain. The ticket stands in for a bearer the browser cannot send on an upgrade.
func (h streamHandlers) ticketGate(c *gin.Context) {
        if _, ok := c.Get(redactedKey); !ok && c.Request.URL.Query().Has(ticketQuery) {
                // RedactTicket was not installed ahead of the access log: refuse rather than serve a
                // request whose ticket may already be in a log. Wiring bug, not a client error.
                slog.ErrorContext(c.Request.Context(), "a stream ticket arrived but RedactTicket is not installed; refusing")
                c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusInternalServerError, ErrorBody{Code: "internal", Message: "Something went wrong.", CorrelationID: CorrelationID(c)})
                return
        }
        if present, _ := c.Get(ticketPresentKey); present != true {
                c.Next()
                return
        }
        ticket := c.GetString(ticketKey)
        if ticket == "" { // ?ticket= with no value
                c.Abort()
                writeError(c, domain.ErrTicketInvalid, nil)
                return
        }
        c.Abort()
        if !h.opts.originAllowed(c.Request) {
                c.JSON(http.StatusForbidden, ErrorBody{Code: "origin_not_allowed", Message: "This origin may not open a stream.", CorrelationID: CorrelationID(c)})
                return
        }
        ctx := c.Request.Context()
        // Peek reads the ticket without spending it, so a junk ticket is a plain 401 (no upgrade, no
        // socket held) and a refused connect leaves a real one redeemable.
        claim, err := h.svc.Streams.Peek(ctx, ticket)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        release, ok := h.admit()
        if !ok {
                h.refuseFull(c)
                return
        }
        defer release()
        a := serveArgs{
                actor: claim.UserID, session: claim.SessionID, encounter: claim.EncounterID, clientID: claim.ClientID,
                userLevel: claim.UserLevel,
                authorize: func(ctx context.Context) (data.Viewer, error) { return h.svc.Streams.View(ctx, claim) },
                redeem:    func(ctx context.Context) error { _, err := h.svc.Streams.Redeem(ctx, ticket); return err },
        }
        h.serve(c, a)
}

// bearerStream opens the stream for a caller authenticated by bearer: authorized exactly as the
// REST reads are (OpenFGA through the caller's own token, fail closed).
func (h streamHandlers) bearerStream(c *gin.Context) {
        if !h.opts.originAllowed(c.Request) {
                c.JSON(http.StatusForbidden, ErrorBody{Code: "origin_not_allowed", Message: "This origin may not open a stream.", CorrelationID: CorrelationID(c)})
                return
        }
        actor, session, enc := authz.Viewer(c), c.Query("session_id"), c.Query("encounter_id")
        userLevel := c.Query("scope") == scopeUser
        if userLevel && (session != "" || enc != "") {
                writeError(c, data.ErrInvalid, nil)
                return
        }
        if _, viaCredential := c.Get(credentialContextKey); viaCredential && (session != "" || userLevel) {
                // Session data is authorized with the caller's own bearer (PADR-0037); a credential has none.
                userOnly(c)
                return
        }
        client := c.Query("client_id")
        if client == "" {
                client = c.GetHeader(clientIDHeader)
        }
        if !data.ValidClientID(client) {
                client = "" // ignored, never rejected, like the origin of a write
        }
        a := serveArgs{actor: actor, session: session, encounter: enc, clientID: client, userLevel: userLevel}
        a.authorize = func(ctx context.Context) (data.Viewer, error) {
                return h.svc.Streams.BearerView(ctx, actor, session, enc)
        }
        if id, ok := c.Get(credentialContextKey); ok {
                a.guard = liveGuard(func(ctx context.Context) (bool, error) {
                        return h.svc.Credentials.Live(ctx, id.(string))
                })
        }
        if id, ok := c.Get(credentialContextKey); ok {
                a.credentialID = id.(string)
        }
        release, ok := h.admit()
        if !ok {
                h.refuseFull(c)
                return
        }
        defer release()
        h.serve(c, a)
}

var errCredentialRevoked = errors.New("credential revoked")

// liveGuard turns a liveness read into a heartbeat guard. A revoked credential ends the stream at
// once. A store error keeps it until maxGuardErrors checks in a row have failed, then closes it as
// revoked: fail closed, but not on one blip, so a missed revocation is bounded by that many
// heartbeats. A successful read resets the count.
func liveGuard(live func(context.Context) (bool, error)) func(context.Context) error {
        var failed int
        return func(ctx context.Context) error {
                ok, err := live(ctx)
                switch {
                case err != nil:
                        if failed++; failed >= maxGuardErrors {
                                return errCredentialRevoked
                        }
                case !ok:
                        return errCredentialRevoked
                default:
                        failed = 0
                }
                return nil
        }
}

type serveArgs struct {
        actor, session, encounter, clientID string
        // userLevel opens the user-level stream: addressed events only, no viewer, no encounter heads.
        userLevel bool
        authorize func(context.Context) (data.Viewer, error)
        // guard runs on every re-check, besides the membership re-check; an error ends the stream.
        guard func(context.Context) error
        // credentialID is set when an integration credential opened the stream; its seats count in
        // their own bucket. seat is the seat serve takes and releases.
        credentialID string
        seat         seat
        // redeem spends the ticket that opened the stream; nil for a bearer stream. It runs after the
        // seat is taken, so a refused connect never spends it.
        redeem func(context.Context) error
}

// serve opens a pending subscription, authorizes, grants, takes a seat, spends the ticket (if any),
// and upgrades. Everything that can fail does so before the upgrade, as an ordinary HTTP error.
// The seat comes after authorization so a caller who may not see the scope costs the lease store
// nothing, and the ticket after the seat so a refusal leaves it redeemable.
func (h streamHandlers) serve(c *gin.Context, a serveArgs) {
        if hook := h.opts.onServe; hook != nil {
                hook()
        }
        ctx := c.Request.Context()
        defer h.svc.Hub.Track()() // outlives the connection's close below, so Drain sees the close frame sent
        released := false
        defer func() {
                if !released {
                        h.release(a.seat, false) // refused before the upgrade
                }
        }()
        if h.svc.Hub.ShuttingDown() {
                writeError(c, stream.ErrShuttingDown, nil)
                return
        }
        sub, v, err := h.openSub(ctx, a)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        defer sub.Close()
        if a.seat, err = h.takeSeat(ctx, a); err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        if a.redeem != nil {
                if err = a.redeem(ctx); err != nil { // lost a race for the ticket
                        writeError(c, err, nil)
                        return
                }
        }
        var heads []domain.Head
        var truncated bool
        if !a.userLevel {
                if heads, truncated, err = h.svc.Streams.Heads(ctx, v, a.encounter); err != nil {
                        writeError(c, err, nil)
                        return
                }
        }
        keep := false // only a dropped connection keeps its seat for the grace period
        released = true
        defer func() { h.release(a.seat, keep) }()
        conn, err := websocket.Accept(c.Writer, c.Request, &websocket.AcceptOptions{OriginPatterns: h.opts.AllowedOrigins})
        if err != nil {
                return // Accept has answered
        }
        defer func() { _ = conn.CloseNow() }()
        conn.SetReadLimit(readLimit) // clients send nothing but control frames
        cl := &connection{h: h, conn: conn, sub: sub, v: v, a: a}
        if err := cl.hello(ctx, heads, truncated); err != nil {
                return
        }
        keep = cl.run(ctx)
}

// openSub registers the stream's subscription: a user-level one needs no scope, any other is
// authorized and granted its viewer. A subscription returned with an error is already closed.
func (h streamHandlers) openSub(ctx context.Context, a serveArgs) (*stream.Subscription, data.Viewer, error) {
        if a.userLevel {
                return h.svc.Hub.OpenUser(a.actor, h.opts.buffer()), data.Viewer{}, nil
        }
        sub := h.svc.Hub.Open(a.session, a.actor, a.encounter, h.opts.buffer())
        v, err := a.authorize(ctx)
        if err == nil {
                err = sub.Grant(v)
                if errors.Is(err, stream.ErrEvicted) && sub.ServerStopping() {
                        err = stream.ErrShuttingDown // closed by Shutdown after the check above
                } else if errors.Is(err, stream.ErrEvicted) || errors.Is(err, stream.ErrViewer) {
                        err = data.ErrNotMember
                }
        }
        if err != nil {
                sub.Close()
                return nil, v, err
        }
        return sub, v, nil
}

type headFrame struct {
        Type string `json:"type"`
        domain.Head
}

// seat is a stream's place in the lease store; the zero seat means none is kept (no store, or no
// cap configured).
type seat struct {
        lease data.StreamLease
        held  bool
}

// takeSeat gives the stream a seat in its bucket: the user's for a user's stream, the credential's
// own for an integration credential's, so a leaked or runaway credential cannot lock its owner out
// of their own stream. Over the cap is data.ErrStreamLimit; a store failure is
// data.ErrStreamUnavailable and a new connect fails closed (streams already open stay up).
func (h streamHandlers) takeSeat(ctx context.Context, a serveArgs) (seat, error) {
        max, bucket := h.opts.ConcurrentMax, "user:"+a.actor
        if a.credentialID != "" {
                max, bucket = h.opts.CredentialMax, "cred:"+a.credentialID
        }
        if h.svc.Leases == nil || max <= 0 {
                return seat{}, nil
        }
        l := data.StreamLease{ID: randomHex(), Bucket: bucket, UserID: a.actor, SessionID: a.session, ClientID: a.clientID, Pod: h.opts.Pod}
        if err := h.svc.Leases.Acquire(ctx, l, h.opts.leaseTTL(), max); err != nil {
                if errors.Is(err, data.ErrStreamUnavailable) {
                        slog.WarnContext(ctx, "the stream seat store is unavailable; refusing a new stream", "error", err)
                }
                return seat{}, err
        }
        return seat{l, true}, nil
}

// release ends a seat. Only a connection that dropped (keep) holds it for the grace period; one the
// server ended, or that never opened, frees it now.
func (h streamHandlers) release(s seat, keep bool) {
        if !s.held {
                return
        }
        if err := h.svc.Leases.Release(context.Background(), s.lease, h.opts.Grace, keep); err != nil {
                slog.Warn("releasing a stream seat failed; it lapses on its own", "seat", s.lease.ID, "error", err)
        }
}

// admit takes one of this pod's connection slots, or reports the pod full. A compare-and-swap, so a
// refused attempt never counts.
func (h streamHandlers) admit() (release func(), ok bool) {
        max := int64(h.opts.MaxPerPod)
        if max <= 0 {
                return func() {}, true
        }
        for {
                n := h.open.Load()
                if n >= max {
                        return nil, false
                }
                if h.open.CompareAndSwap(n, n+1) {
                        return func() { h.open.Add(-1) }, true
                }
        }
}

// refuseFull answers a holder of a real ticket (or an authenticated caller) on a full pod: it
// accepts the upgrade and closes with 1013, since a browser cannot read the status of a refused
// upgrade. The ticket was only peeked, so the client keeps it. Each refusal holds a socket for the
// close handshake, so they are bounded; past the bound the answer is a plain 503.
func (h streamHandlers) refuseFull(c *gin.Context) {
        if h.refusing != nil {
                select {
                case h.refusing <- struct{}{}:
                        defer func() { <-h.refusing }()
                default:
                        c.Header("Retry-After", "5")
                        c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusServiceUnavailable, ErrorBody{Code: "pod_full", Message: "This server is full; try again shortly.", CorrelationID: CorrelationID(c)})
                        return
                }
        }
        conn, err := websocket.Accept(c.Writer, c.Request, &websocket.AcceptOptions{OriginPatterns: h.opts.AllowedOrigins})
        if err != nil {
                return
        }
        _ = conn.Close(websocket.StatusTryAgainLater, "pod full")
}

func randomHex() string {
        b := make([]byte, 12)
        _, _ = cryptorand.Read(b)
        return hex.EncodeToString(b)
}

type connection struct {
        h    streamHandlers
        conn *websocket.Conn
        sub  *stream.Subscription
        v    data.Viewer
        a    serveArgs

        renewFailing    bool
        recheckFailures int
}

func (cl *connection) write(ctx context.Context, v any) error {
        b, err := json.Marshal(v)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, writeTimeout)
        defer cancel()
        return cl.conn.Write(ctx, websocket.MessageText, b)
}

// scopeOf names what a stream follows: the addressed events of the user, or encounter state.
func scopeOf(a serveArgs) string {
        if a.userLevel {
                return scopeUser
        }
        return "encounter"
}

// hello sends the hello frame and one head frame per encounter in scope. They carry only what the
// viewer's own views carry: the scope they asked for and each visible encounter's revision (GM) or
// view tag.
func (cl *connection) hello(ctx context.Context, heads []domain.Head, truncated bool) error {
        opts := cl.h.opts
        if err := cl.write(ctx, gin.H{
                "type": "hello", "scope": scopeOf(cl.a), "session_id": cl.a.session, "encounter_id": cl.a.encounter, "client_id": cl.a.clientID,
                "heartbeat_seconds": int(opts.heartbeat().Seconds()), "max_age_seconds": int(opts.maxAge().Seconds()), "heads_truncated": truncated,
        }); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        for _, hd := range heads {
                if err := cl.write(ctx, headFrame{"head", hd}); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
        }
        return nil
}

// run delivers events until the stream ends for any reason, and closes with the code that says
// why. Client frames are read and discarded (CloseRead), so pongs reset the idle watch.
func (cl *connection) run(ctx context.Context) (keepSeat bool) {
        ctx = cl.conn.CloseRead(ctx)
        tick := time.NewTicker(cl.h.opts.heartbeat())
        defer tick.Stop()
        recheck := time.NewTicker(cl.h.opts.recheck())
        defer recheck.Stop()
        maxAge := time.NewTimer(cl.h.opts.maxAge())
        defer maxAge.Stop()
        pings := make(chan struct{}, 1)
        go cl.pinger(ctx, pings)
        for {
                select {
                case ev, ok := <-cl.sub.C:
                        if !ok {
                                if cl.sub.ServerStopping() {
                                        _ = cl.conn.Close(websocket.StatusGoingAway, "server restarting")
                                } else if cl.sub.Overflowed() {
                                        _ = cl.conn.Close(CloseSlowConsumer, "slow consumer")
                                } else {
                                        _ = cl.conn.Close(CloseMembershipChanged, "membership changed")
                                }
                                return
                        }
                        // A backlog of events must not postpone the re-check: it goes first whenever it is due,
                        // so the loop is held by at most one write (see stallAllowance).
                        if code, why, due := cl.recheckIfDue(ctx, recheck); due && code != 0 {
                                _ = cl.conn.Close(code, why)
                                return false
                        }
                        if hook := cl.h.opts.beforeWrite; hook != nil {
                                hook()
                        }
                        if cl.sub.Ended() { // evicted: what is still buffered must not be written
                                continue
                        }
                        if cl.write(ctx, ev) != nil {
                                return true
                        }
                case <-recheck.C:
                        if code, why := cl.check(ctx); code != 0 {
                                _ = cl.conn.Close(code, why)
                                return false
                        }
                case <-tick.C:
                        if cl.write(ctx, gin.H{"type": "heartbeat"}) != nil {
                                return true
                        }
                        select {
                        case pings <- struct{}{}:
                        default:
                        }
                        cl.renew(ctx)
                case <-maxAge.C:
                        _ = cl.conn.Close(CloseMaxAge, "maximum connection age")
                        return false
                case <-ctx.Done():
                        return true
                }
        }
}

// renew keeps the stream's seat alive, bounded so a hung store cannot stall the heartbeat, the
// membership re-check or the credential guard. A failure leaves the stream up; the seat lapses after
// the lease TTL if renewals keep failing, and the next connect for that user then has room.
func (cl *connection) renew(ctx context.Context) {
        if !cl.a.seat.held {
                return
        }
        ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, cl.h.opts.renewTimeout()) // part of stallAllowance
        defer cancel()
        err := cl.h.svc.Leases.Renew(ctx, cl.a.seat.lease, cl.h.opts.leaseTTL())
        if err != nil && !cl.renewFailing {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "renewing a stream seat failed; it lapses if renewals keep failing", "seat", cl.a.seat.lease.ID, "error", err)
        }
        cl.renewFailing = err != nil // log the first failure of a run, not every heartbeat
        if hook := cl.h.opts.afterRenew; hook != nil {
                hook()
        }
}

// check re-reads what the stream was authorized on, every recheck interval. Failed reads fail
// closed after three in a row, for membership and for a credential alike: a store that cannot say
// whether the member is still allowed does not keep the stream open for the maximum age. Each
// failed membership read is logged with the running count. The stream closes with
// CloseMembershipChanged, and a connect (which re-reads the member's record) is refused while the
// store is down. A user-level stream has no membership to re-check: each notice it receives is
// checked for membership instead. A credential-backed stream runs the credential guard.
func (cl *connection) check(ctx context.Context) (websocket.StatusCode, string) {
        // Each read has its own timeout: a slow credential guard must not leave the membership
        // re-check an expired context, which would count as a failed re-check. (A credential stream has
        // no session and a session stream has no credential, so one of the two is a no-op.)
        if cl.a.guard != nil {
                gctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, checkTimeout)
                err := cl.a.guard(gctx)
                cancel()
                if errors.Is(err, errCredentialRevoked) {
                        return CloseCredentialRevoked, "credential revoked"
                }
        }
        rctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, checkTimeout) // a hung store must not stall delivery
        defer cancel()
        err := cl.h.svc.Streams.Recheck(rctx, cl.v)
        switch {
        case errors.Is(err, domain.ErrAccessChanged):
                return CloseMembershipChanged, "membership changed"
        case errors.Is(err, domain.ErrRecheckFailed):
                cl.recheckFailures++
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "re-checking a stream's membership failed", "failures", cl.recheckFailures, "of", maxRecheckFailures, "error", err)
                if cl.recheckFailures >= maxRecheckFailures {
                        return CloseMembershipChanged, "membership could not be verified"
                }
        default:
                cl.recheckFailures = 0
        }
        return 0, ""
}

// recheckIfDue runs the re-check if its tick is waiting, without blocking.
func (cl *connection) recheckIfDue(ctx context.Context, t *time.Ticker) (code websocket.StatusCode, why string, due bool) {
        select {
        case <-t.C:
                code, why = cl.check(ctx)
                return code, why, true
        default:
                return 0, "", false
        }
}

// pinger sends a protocol ping per request and ends the connection when no pong comes back.
func (cl *connection) pinger(ctx context.Context, pings <-chan struct{}) {
        for {
                select {
                case <-pings:
                        pctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, pongTimeout)
                        err := cl.conn.Ping(pctx)
                        cancel()
                        if err != nil && ctx.Err() == nil {
                                _ = cl.conn.Close(websocket.StatusGoingAway, "idle")
                                return
                        }
                case <-ctx.Done():
                        return
                }
        }
}

package server

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "log/slog"
        "net/http"
        "strconv"

        "github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/authz-client.go/authz"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/data"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/domain"
        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/entitlements"
)

// userChecker binds an entitlements client to one request's user and bearer token.
type userChecker struct {
        client      *entitlements.Client
        user, token string
        // record holds the user's last first-party entitlement values. A first-party check writes
        // them; an integration credential, which has no user bearer to call entitlements-api with
        // (PADR-0034, PADR-0037), reads them instead (viaCredential).
        record        *data.OwnerEntitlements
        viaCredential bool
}

func (u userChecker) Check(ctx context.Context, key string, p entitlements.Policy) (entitlements.Result, error) {
        if u.viaCredential {
                return u.recorded(ctx, key)
        }
        res, err := u.client.Check(ctx, u.user, u.token, key, p)
        if err == nil && u.record != nil && !res.Stale && !res.Degraded {
                u.remember(ctx, res)
        }
        return res, err
}

// recorded answers from the owner's record. With no record of the key, or one older than the max
// age, it fails closed with ErrLimitsNotSynced: the owner has to use the app once with their own sign-in.
func (u userChecker) recorded(ctx context.Context, key string) (entitlements.Result, error) {
        if u.record == nil {
                return entitlements.Result{}, domain.ErrLimitsNotSynced
        }
        e, err := u.record.Get(ctx, u.user, key)
        if errors.Is(err, data.ErrNotFound) || (err == nil && !u.record.Fresh(e)) {
                return entitlements.Result{}, domain.ErrLimitsNotSynced
        }
        if err != nil {
                return entitlements.Result{}, err
        }
        return entitlements.Result{Key: e.Key, Type: e.Type, Flag: e.Flag, Limit: e.Limit}, nil
}

// remember records a fresh first-party result. It is best effort: a failed write keeps the old
// record and is logged, and the request goes on.
func (u userChecker) remember(ctx context.Context, results ...entitlements.Result) {
        entries := make([]data.OwnerEntitlement, 0, len(results))
        for _, r := range results {
                entries = append(entries, data.OwnerEntitlement{Key: r.Key, Type: r.Type, Limit: r.Limit, Flag: r.Flag})
        }
        if err := u.record.Record(ctx, u.user, entries...); err != nil {
                slog.WarnContext(ctx, "recording owner entitlements failed", "error", err)
        }
}

const ownerRecordKey = "initiative.ownerEntitlements"

// withOwnerRecord puts the owner-entitlement record on the request, for checkerFor.
func withOwnerRecord(rec *data.OwnerEntitlements) gin.HandlerFunc {
        return func(c *gin.Context) {
                if rec != nil {
                        c.Set(ownerRecordKey, rec)
                }
                c.Next()
        }
}

func checkerFor(c *gin.Context, client *entitlements.Client) domain.Checker {
        rec, _ := c.Get(ownerRecordKey)
        r, _ := rec.(*data.OwnerEntitlements)
        _, viaCredential := c.Get(credentialContextKey)
        return userChecker{client: client, user: authz.Viewer(c), token: authz.Token(c), record: r, viaCredential: viaCredential}
}

type themeBody struct {
        Name              string `json:"name"`
        Style             string `json:"style"`
        MainColor         string `json:"main_color"`
        Accent1Color      string `json:"accent1_color"`
        Accent2Color      string `json:"accent2_color"`
        Background1Color  string `json:"background1_color"`
        Background2Color  string `json:"background2_color"`
        PrimaryFontName   string `json:"primary_font_name"`
        SecondaryFontName string `json:"secondary_font_name"`
        BackgroundImage   string `json:"background_image"`
}

type themePatchBody struct {
        Revision          int64   `json:"revision"`
        Name              *string `json:"name"`
        Style             *string `json:"style"`
        MainColor         *string `json:"main_color"`
        Accent1Color      *string `json:"accent1_color"`
        Accent2Color      *string `json:"accent2_color"`
        Background1Color  *string `json:"background1_color"`
        Background2Color  *string `json:"background2_color"`
        PrimaryFontName   *string `json:"primary_font_name"`
        SecondaryFontName *string `json:"secondary_font_name"`
        BackgroundImage   *string `json:"background_image"`
}

type themeHandlers struct {
        svc *domain.Themes
        ent *entitlements.Client
}

func setupThemeHandlers(g *gin.RouterGroup, svc *domain.Themes, ent *entitlements.Client) {
        h := themeHandlers{svc: svc, ent: ent}
        g.GET("/themes", h.list)
        g.GET("/themes/packaged", h.packaged)
        g.GET("/themes/:id", h.get)
        g.POST("/themes", h.create)
        g.PATCH("/themes/:id", h.update)
        g.DELETE("/themes/:id", h.remove)
}

func (h themeHandlers) list(c *gin.Context) {
        q := data.Query{Cursor: c.Query("cursor")}
        if v := c.Query("limit"); v != "" {
                n, err := strconv.Atoi(v)
                if err != nil || n < 1 {
                        badRequest(c, "limit must be a positive integer")
                        return
                }
                q.Limit = n
        }
        page, err := h.svc.List(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), q)
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, gin.H{"items": page.Items, "next_cursor": page.NextCursor})
}

func (h themeHandlers) packaged(c *gin.Context) {
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, gin.H{"items": h.svc.Packaged()})
}

func (h themeHandlers) get(c *gin.Context) {
        t, err := h.svc.Get(c.Request.Context(), authz.Viewer(c), c.Param("id"))
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, t)
}

func (h themeHandlers) create(c *gin.Context) {
        var b themeBody
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        owner := authz.Viewer(c)
        t, err := h.svc.Create(c.Request.Context(), owner, owner, domain.ThemeInput{
                Name: b.Name, Style: b.Style, MainColor: b.MainColor, Accent1Color: b.Accent1Color,
                Accent2Color: b.Accent2Color, Background1Color: b.Background1Color, Background2Color: b.Background2Color,
                PrimaryFontName: b.PrimaryFontName, SecondaryFontName: b.SecondaryFontName, BackgroundImage: b.BackgroundImage,
        }, checkerFor(c, h.ent))
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusCreated, t)
}

func (h themeHandlers) update(c *gin.Context) {
        var b themePatchBody
        if !bindJSON(c, &b) {
                return
        }
        if b.Revision < 1 {
                badRequest(c, "request body must be JSON with the theme's current revision")
                return
        }
        owner := authz.Viewer(c)
        t, err := h.svc.Update(c.Request.Context(), owner, owner, c.Param("id"), b.Revision, domain.ThemePatch{
                Name: b.Name, Style: b.Style, MainColor: b.MainColor, Accent1Color: b.Accent1Color,
                Accent2Color: b.Accent2Color, Background1Color: b.Background1Color, Background2Color: b.Background2Color,
                PrimaryFontName: b.PrimaryFontName, SecondaryFontName: b.SecondaryFontName, BackgroundImage: b.BackgroundImage,
        })
        if err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, t)
                return
        }
        c.JSON(http.StatusOK, t)
}

func (h themeHandlers) remove(c *gin.Context) {
        rev, err := strconv.ParseInt(c.Query("revision"), 10, 64)
        if err != nil || rev < 1 {
                badRequest(c, "revision query parameter is required")
                return
        }
        owner := authz.Viewer(c)
        if err := h.svc.Delete(c.Request.Context(), owner, owner, c.Param("id"), rev); err != nil {
                writeError(c, err, nil)
                return
        }
        c.Status(http.StatusNoContent)
}

package server

import (
        "bytes"
        "encoding/json"
        "net/http"

        "github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
)

// typedErrors rewrites the {"error", "message"} bodies that shared middleware writes (authz-client.go
// for 401/403/503, api-core's rate limiter for 429/503) into this API's typed {"code", "message",
// "correlation_id"} shape, so a client reads one error format. Bodies that already have a code pass
// through, and the library's error string becomes the code (the token one, invalid_token, is
// unauthorized like the credential 401). It wraps the writer once per request.
func typedErrors() gin.HandlerFunc {
        return func(c *gin.Context) {
                if _, done := c.Writer.(*typedWriter); !done {
                        c.Writer = &typedWriter{ResponseWriter: c.Writer, c: c}
                }
                c.Next()
        }
}

type typedWriter struct {
        gin.ResponseWriter
        c *gin.Context
}

func (w *typedWriter) Write(b []byte) (int, error) {
        switch w.Status() {
        case http.StatusUnauthorized, http.StatusForbidden, http.StatusTooManyRequests, http.StatusServiceUnavailable:
                var old struct {
                        Error   string  `json:"error"`
                        Message string  `json:"message"`
                        Code    *string `json:"code"`
                }
                if json.Unmarshal(bytes.TrimSpace(b), &old) == nil && old.Error != "" && old.Code == nil {
                        code := old.Error
                        if code == "invalid_token" {
                                code = "unauthorized"
                        }
                        out, err := json.Marshal(ErrorBody{Code: code, Message: old.Message, CorrelationID: CorrelationID(w.c)})
                        if err != nil {
                                return 0, err
                        }
                        // The rewritten body has a different length than the one the caller sized.
                        w.Header().Del("Content-Length")
                        if _, err := w.ResponseWriter.Write(out); err != nil {
                                return 0, err
                        }
                        return len(b), nil
                }
        }
        return w.ResponseWriter.Write(b)
}

package startup

import (
        "context"
        "fmt"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/mongodb.go/database"
)

// pingTimeout bounds the check that a freshly opened client reaches the server.
const pingTimeout = 10 * time.Second

// DB is what ConnectDB needs from the database package, so a test can fail it on demand.
type DB struct {
        Setup    func() error // opens the client; an SRV or URI failure is an error
        Ping     func(ctx context.Context) error
        Teardown func()
}

// LiveDB is the real database package.
func LiveDB() DB {
        return DB{
                Setup: func() (err error) {
                        // SetupDatabase panics on a connect or resolve failure.
                        defer func() {
                                if r := recover(); r != nil {
                                        err = redacted("", fmt.Errorf("%v", r)) // a URL parse panic carries the connection string
                                }
                        }()
                        database.SetupDatabase()
                        return nil
                },
                Ping:     func(ctx context.Context) error { return database.Db.Client().Ping(ctx, nil) },
                Teardown: database.TeardownDatabase,
        }
}

// ConnectDatabase opens the database and checks it answers (the driver connects lazily, so a
// process would otherwise fail on its first query), retrying as p says. A client that opened but
// did not answer is closed before the next try.
func ConnectDatabase(p Policy) error {
        ApplyDriverTimeouts()
        return ConnectDB(LiveDB(), p)
}

// ConnectDB is ConnectDatabase over c.
func ConnectDB(c DB, p Policy) error {
        return Do("connect to the database", p, func() error {
                if err := c.Setup(); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), pingTimeout)
                defer cancel()
                if err := c.Ping(ctx); err != nil {
                        c.Teardown()
                        return err
                }
                return nil
        })
}

package startup

import (
        "os"
        "strings"
)

// Driver timeouts applied when the connection settings do not set them. Without them a dead
// database holds every request for the driver's 30s server-selection wait, and an operation on a
// half-open connection waits forever.
var driverDefaults = []struct{ key, value string }{
        {"serverSelectionTimeoutMS", "5000"},
        {"connectTimeoutMS", "5000"},
        {"socketTimeoutMS", "30000"},
}

// WithDriverTimeouts adds the default driver timeouts to a connection string's query for each one
// it does not already name (names compare case-insensitively, as the driver does).
func WithDriverTimeouts(rawQuery string) string {
        have := map[string]bool{}
        for _, kv := range strings.Split(rawQuery, "&") {
                if k, _, _ := strings.Cut(kv, "="); k != "" {
                        have[strings.ToLower(k)] = true
                }
        }
        out := rawQuery
        for _, d := range driverDefaults {
                if have[strings.ToLower(d.key)] {
                        continue
                }
                if out != "" {
                        out += "&"
                }
                out += d.key + "=" + d.value
        }
        return out
}

// WithDriverTimeoutsURI is WithDriverTimeouts for a whole connection string. The parameters are
// appended to the raw text, so credentials, hosts and escaping come through byte for byte.
func WithDriverTimeoutsURI(raw string) string {
        base, query, hasQuery := strings.Cut(raw, "?")
        if frag := strings.IndexByte(query, '#'); frag >= 0 { // a fragment ends the query
                return raw
        }
        if q := WithDriverTimeouts(query); q != query || !hasQuery {
                return base + "?" + q
        }
        return raw
}

// ApplyDriverTimeouts puts the default timeouts into the connection settings the database package
// reads from the environment: DB_URI when that is set (the package then ignores DB_OPTS), DB_OPTS
// otherwise.
func ApplyDriverTimeouts() {
        if raw, ok := os.LookupEnv("DB_URI"); ok {
                _ = os.Setenv("DB_URI", WithDriverTimeoutsURI(raw))
                return
        }
        _ = os.Setenv("DB_OPTS", WithDriverTimeouts(os.Getenv("DB_OPTS")))
}

// Package startup holds the retry that process start-up uses against its dependencies. A pod that
// starts while the cluster resolver or a dependency is briefly unreachable should wait it out, not
// crash-loop: the dependency is usually back within seconds.
package startup

import (
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "regexp"
        "strings"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/common.go/logging"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/mongo"
)

// Policy bounds a retry. Attempts are spaced by a delay that doubles from First up to Max, and stop
// once the time spent plus the next wait would pass Budget (attempt time counts, not only the
// waits). An error Permanent reports (an authentication failure, an index conflict) will not fix
// itself, so it gets PermanentAfter tries in all.
type Policy struct {
        Budget, First, Max time.Duration
        PermanentAfter     int
        Permanent          func(error) bool
        // Sleep and Now are time.Sleep and time.Now unless a test replaces them.
        Sleep func(time.Duration)
        Now   func() time.Time
}

// DefaultPolicy is a minute of retries, 1s growing to 10s, and two tries for an authentication
// failure.
func DefaultPolicy() Policy {
        return Policy{Budget: time.Minute, First: time.Second, Max: 10 * time.Second, PermanentAfter: 2, Permanent: IsAuthError, Sleep: time.Sleep, Now: time.Now}
}

// IndexPolicy is p for creating indexes: a conflict with an existing index never succeeds on
// retry, so it fails on the first try.
func IndexPolicy(p Policy) Policy {
        p.Permanent, p.PermanentAfter = IsIndexConflict, 1
        return p
}

// Do runs attempt until it succeeds, retrying as p says. Each failed attempt is logged at warn with
// what was being done; the last error is returned once the budget is spent. Connection URIs in an
// error (user:password@host) are masked in both the log and the returned error.
func Do(what string, p Policy, attempt func() error) error {
        if p.Sleep == nil {
                p.Sleep = time.Sleep
        }
        if p.Now == nil {
                p.Now = time.Now
        }
        if p.First <= 0 {
                p.First = time.Second // a zero delay would loop without waiting
        }
        p.Max = max(p.Max, p.First)
        start := p.Now()
        backoff := p.First
        var permanent int
        for n := 1; ; n++ {
                err := attempt()
                if err == nil {
                        return nil
                }
                if p.Permanent != nil && p.Permanent(err) {
                        if permanent++; permanent >= max(p.PermanentAfter, 1) {
                                return redacted(fmt.Sprintf("%s failed after %d tries and will not fix itself: ", what, permanent), err)
                        }
                }
                if elapsed := p.Now().Sub(start); elapsed+backoff > p.Budget {
                        return redacted(fmt.Sprintf("%s failed after %d tries over %s: ", what, n, elapsed.Round(time.Millisecond)), err)
                }
                logging.Logger.Warn("startup step failed; retrying", "step", what, "attempt", n, "retry_in", backoff.String(), "error", Redact(err.Error()))
                p.Sleep(backoff)
                backoff = min(backoff*2, p.Max)
        }
}

var uriUserinfo = regexp.MustCompile(`://[^/@\s]*@`)

// Redact masks the user:password part of any connection URI in s.
func Redact(s string) string { return uriUserinfo.ReplaceAllString(s, "://***@") }

// redactedError carries a masked message and still unwraps to the original, so errors.Is and As
// work on it.
type redactedError struct {
        msg string
        err error
}

func (e redactedError) Error() string { return e.msg }
func (e redactedError) Unwrap() error { return e.err }

func redacted(prefix string, err error) error {
        return redactedError{msg: prefix + Redact(err.Error()), err: err}
}

// IsAuthError reports whether err looks like a rejected credential, which waiting does not fix.
func IsAuthError(err error) bool {
        m := strings.ToLower(err.Error())
        return strings.Contains(m, "authenticationfailed") || strings.Contains(m, "authentication failed")
}

// MongoIndexOptionsConflict and MongoIndexKeySpecsConflict are the server's answers to creating an
// index that differs from an existing one of the same name or keys (a changed TTL, say). They
// only go away when someone drops or changes the index.
const (
        MongoIndexOptionsConflict  = 85
        MongoIndexKeySpecsConflict = 86
)

// IsIndexConflict reports whether err is an index definition conflict.
func IsIndexConflict(err error) bool {
        var ce mongo.CommandError
        return errors.As(err, &ce) && (ce.HasErrorCode(MongoIndexOptionsConflict) || ce.HasErrorCode(MongoIndexKeySpecsConflict))
}

// Package fake is an in-memory store.Collection for tests. It evaluates the filter operators the
// data layer emits (equality, $gt/$gte/$lt/$lte/$ne/$in/$regex, $and, $or) and the $set/$unset/
// $inc update operators, and enforces _id and unique-index uniqueness.
package fake

import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "reflect"
        "regexp"
        "sort"
        "strings"
        "sync"
        "time"

        "github.com/sweetrpg/initiative-api/store"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson"
        "go.mongodb.org/mongo-driver/bson/primitive"
)

// DB is an in-memory store.Database.
type DB struct {
        mu   sync.Mutex
        cols map[string]*Collection
}

// NewDB returns an empty DB.
func NewDB() *DB { return &DB{cols: map[string]*Collection{}} }

// Collection returns (creating on first use) the named collection.
func (d *DB) Collection(name string) store.Collection { return d.Fake(name) }

// Fake returns the concrete fake collection, for assertions on its contents.
func (d *DB) Fake(name string) *Collection {
        d.mu.Lock()
        defer d.mu.Unlock()
        if c, ok := d.cols[name]; ok {
                return c
        }
        c := &Collection{}
        d.cols[name] = c
        return c
}

// Collection is one in-memory collection.
type Collection struct {
        mu      sync.Mutex
        docs    []bson.M
        Indexes []store.IndexSpec
        // FindCalls records each Find's options, so tests can prove limits and sorts reach the query.
        FindCalls []store.FindOptions

        watchers    []*cursor
        lostHistory bool // the next resumed Watch fails with ErrHistoryLost
}

// Docs returns a copy of the stored documents.
func (c *Collection) Docs() []bson.M {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        return append([]bson.M(nil), c.docs...)
}

func toM(v any) (bson.M, error) {
        b, err := bson.Marshal(v)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        var m bson.M
        return m, bson.Unmarshal(b, &m)
}

func decode(m bson.M, out any) error {
        b, err := bson.Marshal(m)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        return bson.Unmarshal(b, out)
}

func (c *Collection) InsertOne(_ context.Context, doc any) error {
        m, err := toM(doc)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        if c.violatesUnique(m, -1) {
                return store.ErrDuplicate
        }
        c.docs = append(c.docs, m)
        c.wake()
        return nil
}

func (c *Collection) FindOne(_ context.Context, filter bson.D, out any) error {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        for _, d := range c.docs {
                if ok, err := match(d, filter); err != nil {
                        return err
                } else if ok {
                        return decode(d, out)
                }
        }
        return store.ErrNotFound
}

func (c *Collection) Find(_ context.Context, filter bson.D, opts store.FindOptions, out any) error {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        c.FindCalls = append(c.FindCalls, opts)
        var hits []bson.M
        for _, d := range c.docs {
                ok, err := match(d, filter)
                if err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                if ok {
                        hits = append(hits, d)
                }
        }
        sort.SliceStable(hits, func(i, j int) bool { return less(hits[i], hits[j], opts.Sort) })
        if opts.Limit > 0 && int64(len(hits)) > opts.Limit {
                hits = hits[:opts.Limit]
        }
        res := make(bson.A, 0, len(hits))
        for _, h := range hits {
                res = append(res, project(h, opts.Projection))
        }
        return decode(bson.M{"r": res}, &wrap{out})
}

// wrap decodes {"r": [...]} straight into a slice pointer via reflection.
type wrap struct{ out any }

func (w *wrap) UnmarshalBSON(b []byte) error {
        var raw bson.Raw = b
        arr, err := raw.LookupErr("r")
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        rv := reflect.ValueOf(w.out).Elem()
        rv.Set(reflect.MakeSlice(rv.Type(), 0, 0))
        vals, err := arr.Array().Values()
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        for _, v := range vals {
                item := reflect.New(rv.Type().Elem())
                if err := v.Unmarshal(item.Interface()); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                rv.Set(reflect.Append(rv, item.Elem()))
        }
        return nil
}

func project(d bson.M, proj bson.D) bson.M {
        if len(proj) == 0 {
                return d
        }
        out := bson.M{"_id": d["_id"]}
        for _, e := range proj {
                if v, ok := d[e.Key]; ok {
                        out[e.Key] = v
                }
        }
        return out
}

func (c *Collection) FindOneAndUpdate(_ context.Context, filter, update bson.D, out any) error {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        for i, d := range c.docs {
                ok, err := match(d, filter)
                if err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                if !ok {
                        continue
                }
                next := cloneM(d)
                if err := applyUpdate(next, update); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                if c.violatesUnique(next, i) {
                        return store.ErrDuplicate
                }
                c.docs[i] = next
                return decode(next, out)
        }
        return store.ErrNotFound
}

// UpsertOne applies update to the first match, or inserts a document seeded from the filter's
// top-level equality fields and then updated.
func (c *Collection) UpsertOne(_ context.Context, filter, update bson.D) error {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        for i, d := range c.docs {
                ok, err := match(d, filter)
                if err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                if !ok {
                        continue
                }
                next := cloneM(d)
                if err := applyUpdate(next, update); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
                c.docs[i] = next
                return nil
        }
        doc := bson.M{}
        for _, e := range filter {
                if _, isOp := e.Value.(bson.D); !isOp && e.Key[0] != '$' {
                        doc[e.Key] = e.Value
                }
        }
        if err := applyUpdate(doc, update); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if c.violatesUnique(doc, -1) {
                return store.ErrDuplicate
        }
        c.docs = append(c.docs, doc)
        return nil
}

func (c *Collection) DeleteOne(_ context.Context, filter bson.D) error {
        c.mu.Lock()
        defer c.mu.Unlock()
        for i, d := range c.docs {
                ok, err := match(d, filter)
                if err != nil {
                        return err
                }
